## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Actual Boolean family, Fourier atoms, and corrected influence

On m binary data bits define g(y)=(-1)^(y_1 y_2+...+y_{m-1}y_m), and g'(y)=g(y)-2*1{y=0}. Since g(0)=1, both are Boolean. Let f_m(x,y)=g(y)-2x*1{y=0}. For a disjoint blocks put F_{a,m}=product_{j=1}^a f_m(x_j,y_j), with n=a(m+1) coordinates. This matches the lead's a-fold tensor family, not a splice sharing one data block among all selectors.

Set delta=2^{-m} and u=2^{-m/2}. Each bent coefficient equals e_T u, e_T in {+1,-1}. The product of two-bit Walsh tables proves this directly. The fraction of positive signs is w_+=(1+u)/2, since sum_T hat(g)(T)=g(0)=1. Using the fair binary selector character, the parent Fourier coefficients are

    hat(f_m)(selector absent,T)=e_T u-delta,
    hat(f_m)(selector present,T)=delta.

Thus their squared masses are delta(1-e_T u)^2 and delta^2. The data marginal for T is their sum. Parseval holds exactly. The empty mass is p_empty=(u-delta)^2, and the tensor empty mass is p_empty^a.

Every data coordinate of g has influence 1/2. At the origin and its m neighbors g has value +1, so all edges incident to the origin are originally nonpivotal. Flipping the origin turns both directed orientations of each such edge pivotal. Hence each data influence of g' is 1/2+2delta, not 1/2+delta. Averaging the two branches gives

    Inf_x(f_m)=delta,
    Inf_{y_i}(f_m)=1/2+delta,
    J:=I_data(f_m)=m/2+m delta,
    I_0:=I(f_m)=m/2+(m+1)delta.

Disjoint multiplication preserves each factor's physical influences, so I(F)=a I_0 and I_sel=a delta. This corrected the first pilot's anticipated formula; the failed assertion is retained in out/PILOT_FAILURE.json.

### 2. Exact entropy increment and its asymptotic scale

Write

    A(u)=-2 sum_{e=+/-1} w_e (1-eu)^2 log2(1-eu),
    B(u)=sum_{e=+/-1} w_e (1-2eu)^2 log2|1-2eu|,
    w_e=(1+eu)/2,

with 0*log(0)=0. Substitution in the displayed Fourier masses gives

    H(f_m)=m+m delta+A(u),
    [H(g)+H(g')]/2=m-B(u),
    Delta_0=m delta+A(u)+B(u),  Delta(F)=a Delta_0.

The last identity follows from tensor entropy additivity and independent uniform selector branches. These identities supply exact finite expressions; the checker independently starts from the actual Boolean truth tables.

For fixed c, (1-ce u)^2 ln(1-ce u)=-ce u+(3/2)c^2 u^2+O(u^3). Averaging with w_e gives (-c+(3/2)c^2)u^2+O(u^4); symmetry removes odd powers. Therefore A=-delta/ln2+O(delta^2), B=4delta/ln2+O(delta^2), and

    Delta_0=delta[m+3/ln2+O(delta)],
    H(f_m)=m+O(m delta),  I_0=m/2+O(m delta).

Only this leading asymptotic is used; this stage does not re-open the lead's already computed second-order sharpness coefficient.

### 3. Whole-set mask: a complete decoder and uniform influence payment

Fix (a,m), the block order and the coordinate labels before drawing the Fourier set. The decoder reads n bits and outputs exactly the indicated subset. This is a full fixed-length binary code for every Fourier set, including the empty set; it needs neither a gate name per selected coordinate nor a spectral-model selection. Its exact cost is C_mask=n. Since I_0>=m/2 and m>=2,

    C_mask/I=(m+1)/I_0 <= 2(m+1)/m <=3.

For each fixed a,

    C_mask/I ->2,
    C_mask/Delta = (m+1)/Delta_0 ~ 2^m.

This reference works because the data spectrum is dense and total data influence is linear in the dimension. It would return n/I on an arbitrary Boolean seed and is not a universal reference bound.

### 4. Actual one-root generator, with every normalizer and the empty atom paid

Use one support R=[n], ordered by blocks (selector then m data coordinates), gamma=6/5, and theta_s(r)=min(1,(s/r)^gamma). After conditioning the root experiment on a nonempty root set, the sole root is deterministic: its root odds and rarity cancel exactly. Select a size s>=1 with probability 1/[s(s+1)], an s-subset with probability W_s(S)/Z_{n,s}, where

    W_s(S)=product_{r in S} theta_s(r),
    Z_{n,s}=e_s(theta_s(1),...,theta_s(n)),

then choose no children with probability 1/2. The selected successful single-visit run has probability

    q(S)=W_s(S)/[2s(s+1)Z_{n,s}].

These single-visit probabilities form a subprobability on nonempty sets. Larger collections of successful repeated visits may improve the output reference; they are not used or claimed optimized. The sole support is a coding parameter table, not a claim that F is one regular gate or one DNF conjunction. Direct subprobability/Gibbs coding is sufficient here.

Let p=p_empty^a and v=1-p. Give the empty set mass p and each nonempty set reference mass v q(S). This fixed reference depends only on the known parameters. Its complete expected negative logarithm is

    C_queue=h2(v)+sum_{S nonempty} P_F(S)
                    [1+log2(s(s+1))+log2 Z_{n,s}-sum_{r in S}log2 theta_s(r)].

There is no uncharged empty flag, root normalization, codebook choice or posterior label entropy. A posterior restricted to these single-root single-visit runs is a point mass given S. The empty mixture probability is paid by h2(v), rather than discarded.

Since theta<=1, Z_{n,s}<=binom(n,s)<=2^n. Every rank r<=n, so -sum log2 theta_s(r)<=gamma s log2(n/s). Using s(s+1)<=3^s and sup_{t in [0,n]} t log2(n/t)=n/(e ln2), and I=E|S|>=am/2>=1, gives

    C_queue <= [2+log2 3+3(1+gamma/(e ln2))] I <9 I.

Here h2(v)<=1<=I, Pr(S nonempty)<=I, n<=3I, and dropping v in the positive n-cost terms can only enlarge the bound. The strict numerical sign is exact: e>8/3, ln2>2/3, and log2 3<8/5 imply the bracket is less than 69/8<9. The first two inequalities follow from the exponential series and the first positive term of ln2=2 atanh(1/3), and the last follows from 3^5<2^8. No floating-point constant is a proof step.

### 5. Queue asymptotic and code/Delta ratio

Use the same constant as the addressing subset-generator analysis:

    w(t)=1 (0<=t<=1/2), w(t)=(2t)^(-gamma) (1/2<t<=1),
    integral_0^1 z w(t)/(1+z w(t)) dt=1/2,
    G=integral_0^1 log2(1+z w(t))dt-(1/2)log2 z
                                         -(1/2)integral_0^1 log2 w(t)dt.

The equation has one positive solution z. As in stage 1, coefficient tilting by independent Bernoulli odds z_n theta_s(r), with mean s, expresses log Z as sum log(1+z_n theta)-s log z_n plus log Pr(K=s). Chebyshev and bounded adjacent atom ratios on a central O(sqrt n) window show the last term is o(n), without invoking an unproved local limit theorem. Riemann sums yield the displayed integral whenever s/n tends to 1/2. The normalization logarithm has rate integral log(1+zw)-(1/2)log z.

For completeness, the spectral law of f_m is within total variation u+delta of the law with selector always absent and the m data bits fair and independent: summing the absolute differences of the absent-selector masses and the selector mass gives at most 2u+2delta. For fixed a the tensor total variation is at most a(u+delta). The largest nonempty queue cost is O(n log n), so replacing the true law by this ideal law changes the normalized expectation by o(1). In the ideal law a selector ranks are absent and all other rank indicators are fair; since a is fixed, omitting those ranks changes Riemann sums and the expected selected log-weight rate by o(1). Binomial concentration gives s/n->1/2, the size logarithm is o(n), and the empty mixture cost is at most one bit. Consequently

    C_queue/(am) ->G,
    C_queue/I ->2G,
    C_queue/Delta ~G 2^m.

The elementary uniform-subset/Gibbs lower bound and Z<=binom(n,s) upper bound give

    1<=G<=1+(gamma/2)(1-1/(2ln2)),
    2<=2G<=2+gamma(1-1/(2ln2)) < 2.3344.

The decimal is a display only. Unlike addressing, this single-support reference pays no a-bit address name or root rarity. Copying addressing's 2+2G limit would add a nonexistent address charge. Limits in growing a and fixed m are not inferred from this fixed-a asymptotic; the uniform <9I estimate covers that regime directly.

### 6. Tensor guard on the lead's derivative boundary charge

The raw derivative influence is not spectator-invariant. For one factor, D_x f_m=(g-g')/2=1{y=0}, so 2I(D)=m delta. In a tensor product with an independent spectator h,

    I(D*h)=I(D)+||D||_2^2 I(h)=I(D)+delta I(h),

although Delta_x H and B_x are unchanged by h. This is immediate from the product Fourier law, and is checked from actual physical derivatives below.

Consider the actual tree revealing selectors in block order and stopping after all a selectors. At the jth selector (j=0,...,a-1), the earlier factors are independently restricted branches of average data influence J, and the later factors still have influence I_0. Thus the expected total raw boundary charge is exactly

    T_raw=sum_visited E[2I(D_v)]
         =a m delta + delta a(a-1)(2J+delta),
    T_raw/I(F)=delta[m+(a-1)(2J+delta)]/I_0.

For every fixed even m this tends to infinity linearly with a. This is not a double-counting-free resource merely because each selector is queried once: every node's derivative also bills the same independent data spectators.

This overcharge survives the exact-B bad-node filter. For a single factor let q_T=(e_T u-delta)^2+delta^2. Then q_T>=delta/2. Its selector-present atom is delta^2, so

    q_T h2(delta^2/q_T)>=delta^2 log2(q_T/delta^2)
                               >=delta^2(m-1).

Summing all 2^m rows proves B_x>=delta(m-1). Both B_x and Inf_x=delta are invariant under the remaining independent factors and under fixing earlier selectors. Therefore, for any finite threshold K, choosing an even m with m-1>K makes EVERY selector node of this selector-first tree satisfy B_x>K Inf_x. Counting only those nodes does not remove the quadratic-in-a raw charge.

The narrow repair is to remove independent tensor spectators before pricing the boundary. The factorwise charge is T_factored=a m delta, with T_factored/I=m delta/I_0<=2delta<=1/2. More generally, if f=g(x_A)h(x_Ac) and i belongs to A, then Delta_i H(f)=Delta_i H(g), Inf_i(f)=Inf_i(g), and B_i(f)=B_i(g). Hence a valid node inequality for g can be lifted using I(D_i g), retaining the spectator's terminal entropy, instead of I(D_i f). This transfer uses no additional influence payment. It is an algebraic correction for exact disjoint factors; no analogous rule for overlapping or approximately independent cores is established here.

This refutes only a universal raw-charge bound for this specified selector-first stopping rule, including its bad-node filter. It does not refute the lead's existence of a better adaptive stopping tree, good data-coordinate choices, factor removal, C019, or FEI. It explains why a successful tree must make its boundary charge or selection tensor-aware.

The table displays rounded centers of rational intervals, not standalone floating-point certificates. # A016 stage 3: a shared bit defeats factor-only boundary pricing

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Test the stage-2 proposed repair of B04's C019 boundary mechanism: remove every exact tensor spectator before summing 2I(D_v) at nodes with exact spectral erasure B_v>K Inf_v. Use a growing Boolean family whose bent-splice modules share one physical data bit. Decide whether factor removal suffices on the selector-first rule, and produce a complete paid alternative with its terminal entropy retained. All variables are independent fair bits, signs are Boolean +/-1, entropy/costs are in bits, and derivative influence means L2 Dirichlet energy, not trinary edge-disagreement probability.

The theorem below holds for all integers a>=1 and even m>=4. The shared-bit family has n=1+am physical coordinates. The selector-first rule queries x_1,...,x_a and then stops. Factor removal is the strongest possible removal of exact independent sign factors on disjoint physical coordinates, recomputed at every reached node. The alternative queries the shared bit z first and stops with explicitly coded leaves.

Outcome: factor removal still leaves a quadratic-in-a boundary bill, although the root has no independent factor at all. For every fixed K choose m with (m-1)/2>K; all selected nodes are genuinely bad and their factor-minimized bill divided by I tends to infinity. This refutes that specified rule/repair, not the existence of a universal adaptive tree. Shared-bit-first has zero K=4 bad-node bill and keeps the terminal cost explicit. A fixed normalized root reference has complete cost <=(8/3)I on the same family. Full FEI remains OPEN.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. A single shared physical bit

For each module j use a private selector x_j and m-1 private data bits t_j=(t_{j,1},...,t_{j,m-1}), together with one COMMON data bit z. Define

    c_j(t_j)=(-1)^(t_{j,2}t_{j,3}+...+t_{j,m-2}t_{j,m-1}),
    b_j(z,t_j)=(-1)^(z t_{j,1}) c_j(t_j),
    f_j(z,x_j,t_j)=b_j(z,t_j)-2 x_j 1{z=0,t_j=0},
    F(z,x,t)=product_{j=1}^a f_j(z,x_j,t_j).

The values at a flipped point remain Boolean, since b_j(0,0)=1. Each b_j is exactly the m-bit paired quadratic bent core from stage 2, with its first bit identified across modules. This is not a disjoint product: all modules depend on the same z.

Set delta=2^{-m}. At z=0 the module is

    psi_j(x_j,t_j)=c_j(t_j)-2 x_j 1{t_j=0},

and at z=1 it is (-1)^(t_{j,1}) c_j(t_j). The private blocks tensorize only AFTER revealing z. Denote the z=0 module's data influence and total influence by

    J_0=(m-2)/2+2(m-1)delta,
    L_0=J_0+2delta=(m-2)/2+2m delta.

The private selector influences before revealing z are delta. At z=0 t_{j,1} has influence 2delta, and each other private data coordinate has influence 1/2+2delta; at z=1 these influences are 1 and 1/2. Averaging gives every private data coordinate of F influence 1/2+delta.

For the shared z edge, the ratio of the two branches is a product of independent block signs

    (-1)^(t_{j,1}) [1-2 x_j 1{t_j=0}].

Each such sign has mean -2delta. Thus

    d_z=Inf_z(F)=[1-(-2delta)^a]/2,
    V=(m-1)/2+m delta,
    I(F)=a V+d_z.

This is linear in a, and d_z>=3/8 (a crude bound sufficient below). The exact child influences are

    I(F|z=0)=a L_0,
    I(F|z=1)=am/2,
    I(F)=d_z+[I(F|z=0)+I(F|z=1)]/2.

The identity uses the actual physical coordinate z. No oblique or spectral basis change is used.

### 2. Exact physical factorization, including all descendants

Write F=(-1)^P, with the unique multilinear GF(2) algebraic normal form

    P=sum_j [z t_{j,1}+sum_{k=1}^{(m-2)/2}t_{j,2k}t_{j,2k+1}
                          +x_j(1+z) product_{l=1}^{m-1}(1+t_{j,l})].

Define its interaction graph by connecting all variables in every nonzero monomial. A sign factors over disjoint physical variable sets iff its ANF splits as a sum of polynomials on those sets. Uniqueness of the multilinear ANF proves the forward implication; the reverse follows by multiplying the corresponding signs. Consequently the graph components give the finest exact independent sign-factor partition. This applies to arbitrary Boolean factors, not only quadratic factors.

At the root, each monomial x_j z product_l t_{j,l} is present with coefficient one; none can cancel with another module or a quadratic term. It connects every coordinate of module j to z. Therefore F's interaction graph is connected: no proper independent tensor factor can be stripped at the root.

After fixing the first j selectors, each unqueried module still connects its entire block to z. A previously fixed selector with value 1 has monomial z product_l t_{j,l}; because m>=4 its degree exceeds that of the original quadratic terms, so its entire data block remains connected to z. A previously fixed selector with value 0 leaves z t_{j,1} connected to z and separates its m-2 other data bits into independent quadratic pairs. These and only these are removable components. The exclusion m=2 is deliberate: that endpoint has an additional cancellation making a flipped branch separable, and is outside this all-parameter charge formula.

The checker reconstructs the ANF from the full truth table at every reached node; it does not infer connectedness from the constructor's syntax.

### 3. The actual selector-first bill after optimal factor removal

At a selector x_j, the half-difference is a signed indicator of {z=0,t_j=0} multiplied by the other modules restricted to z=0. Its squared norm is delta. The indicator itself contributes I(D)=m delta/2. Every remaining independent z=0 spectator adds delta times its influence. After j previous selectors are fixed (j=0,...,a-1), earlier modules have average z=0 influence J_0 and later unqueried modules have influence L_0. Hence summing the ACTUAL probability-weighted raw charges yields

    T_raw=sum_v pi_v 2I(D_v)
         =a m delta+delta a(a-1)(J_0+L_0)
         =a m delta+delta a(a-1)[m-2+(4m-2)delta].

Now remove all disjoint sign factors not containing the queried coordinate, recomputing the finest partition at each node. By Section 2 only the quadratic pairs in earlier modules with selector value 0 can reduce the bill. Each such module's removed influence is (m-2)/2; its contribution to I(D) is delta(m-2)/2. There are on average j/2 such earlier modules. Therefore

    T_factor=T_raw-delta a(a-1)(m-2)/4
            =a m delta+delta a(a-1)[(3/4)(m-2)+(4m-2)delta].

This is the minimum derivative bill achievable by exact disjoint factor removal for this rule. Removing more would remove a variable in the queried component and would not be a permitted independent spectator operation. Equivalent factorizations and signs do not change retained-coordinate L2 energies.

At every fixed even m>=4, V is fixed and d_z is bounded, so

    T_factor/I(F) ~ delta[(3/4)(m-2)+(4m-2)delta] a/V -> infinity.

This is a connected-family obstruction to the factor-only repair, not the already known disjoint tensor obstruction.

### 4. The exact-B bad-node filter does not rescue that rule

The claimed divergence must survive the lead's localization; a scalar h2 upper bound would not suffice. Fix any selector-first descendant and any remaining selector. At z=0 the other restricted modules form one Boolean sign H_0; at z=1 they form another sign H_1. In data Fourier rows where the queried module's t_{j,1} is absent, the z=1 Fourier coefficient is zero. The module's m-2 private quadratic core has coefficients e*2u, where u=2^{-m/2}; the point indicator has coefficient 2delta. In each such row, for either Fourier z-bit, the parent selector-absent and selector-present coefficients are

    (e u-delta) hat(H_0)(R),    delta hat(H_0)(R).

Let q_e=(e u-delta)^2+delta^2. Since 1-2u+2u^2>=1/2, q_e>=delta/2. The conditional binary entropy in this row is at least

    hat(H_0)(R)^2 delta^2 log2(q_e/delta^2)
                      >=hat(H_0)(R)^2 delta^2 (m-1).

There are 2^{m-2} choices of the queried private quadratic index and two Fourier z-bits. Sum R using ||H_0||_2^2=1. Discarding the other nonnegative entropy rows gives, at EVERY selector node,

    B_v >= [delta/2](m-1),  Inf_v=delta,
    B_v/Inf_v >= (m-1)/2.

These quantities are unchanged by removing an independent factor. Given any fixed finite nonnegative K, choose even m>=4 with (m-1)/2>K. Every selector of the chosen rule is genuinely bad, so T_factor is exactly its bad-node charge and its ratio diverges as a grows. For K=4, m=10 already works. An exact formula-only certificate at (a,m)=(8192,10), which does NOT construct its truth table, proves T_factor/I>10 using d_z<=1; see out/GROWING_WITNESS.json. The all-dimensional proof above supplies the Boolean family and factorization, rather than extrapolating from the finite tests.

The refuted statement is specifically: stripping exact independent sign factors suffices to bound the bad-node 2I(D) sum of this selector-first stopping rule by an absolute multiple of I. No universal adaptive-tree theorem is refuted.

### 5. Shared-bit-first: the separator changes the payment

Reveal z first and stop. Since a one-bit conditional entropy is at most one,

    B_z<=1<4 d_z,

using d_z>=3/8. Thus this tree has ZERO internal nodes with B_v>4 Inf_v, and its bad-node derivative bill is zero. This statement concerns its one queried coordinate; it does not declare its leaves free.

At z=0 the product of private psi_j modules has a complete uniform-mask code of am bits. Since L_0>=(m-2)/2>=m/4 for m>=4,

    H(F|z=0)<=am<=4 I(F|z=0).

At z=1 the private t_{j,1} bits form a fixed character, while the m-2 private quadratic coordinates per module have a flat spectrum. Therefore

    H(F|z=1)=a(m-2)<=2 I(F|z=1).

The exact entropy split Delta_z=B_z+JS_z, the reviewed JS bound JS_z<=(2/ln2)d_z, and B_z<=1<=(8/3)d_z give the terminal-inclusive estimate

    H(F)<= (8/3+2/ln2)d_z +4 average_b I(F|z=b)
         <= (8/3+2/ln2) I(F).

The queried influence d_z and the average terminal influence are disjoint terms of the physical influence identity from Section 1. This pays one separator step and both leaves without reusing a data bank. It does not continue the tree by silently charging all leaf variables to a bank already spent.

The contrast is structural: root connectedness did not force large cumulative charges. The coordinate whose revelation exposes independent components was available and had a fixed positive influence. Selecting that coordinate, rather than relying only on factor removal at the initial node, is what repairs this family.

### 6. A complete normalized reference for the SAME paid separator

Let R be a subset of all am private coordinates. Define Q_0(R)=2^{-am}, the complete z=0 uniform-mask reference. Define Q_1(R)=2^{-a(m-2)} on the known z=1 flat support: every x_j is absent, every t_{j,1} is present, and each other private data coordinate is free; Q_1 is zero elsewhere. Both references have total mass one and include their complete terminal costs.

Fix the root reference across all sampled Fourier sets by

    Q(A,R)=[Q_0(R)+Q_1(R)]/4, A in {0,1},

where A records the Fourier z-bit. This is a literal normalized law: choose a physical branch label fairly, draw its terminal reference, then choose the Fourier z-bit fairly, finally erase the latent branch label. Its normalization is exactly one. Both label choices and every terminal mask are included; no known spectrum is provided as uncharged side information. Q_0 makes the law positive on every Fourier set, including the empty set.

On Q_1's support Q_1/Q_0=4^a. The true parent spectral marginal mass on that support is

    alpha=[1+delta^a]/2.

Indeed, the z=1 child puts all its mass there. In one z=0 psi_j factor, the event x_j absent and t_{j,1} present has mass delta: there are 2^{m-2} coefficients equal to -2delta, and their squared mass is delta. The private z=0 factors are independent, giving delta^a. The exact physical-child/spectral-marginal identity gives the displayed alpha after averaging the two children.

Thus the full expected reference cost has the closed form

    CE_Q=am+2-alpha log2(1+4^a)
        <=a(m-1)+2.

No posterior term is omitted: the log2(1+4^a) saving comes from summing the two complete reference probabilities, not counting extra descriptions of one generator run. Let x=a(m-1)>=3. Then I(F)>=x/2+3/8 and

    a(m-1)+2=x+2 <=(8/3)(x/2+3/8) <=(8/3)I(F).

Consequently H(F)<=CE_Q<=(8/3)I(F). This stronger complete-reference estimate closes C020's coding control for this particular shared-bit obstruction while simultaneously exhibiting the correct separator. It is not a universal claim that arbitrary overlapping leaves have these terminal reference laws.

### 7. First unsupported universal line

The new obstruction proves that factor removal alone does not make a fixed rule's boundary cost nonreusing, even on a physically connected family. The new paid rule proves only a one-bit-separator case with an explicit leaf reference and d_z bounded below. The first unsupported extension is: every Boolean function admits an adaptive sequence of physical separators or queries with both affordable exact-B boundary cost and complete terminal reference cost, all charged once to its original influence. Arbitrary irreducible functions may have no such positive-influence separator, and a separator itself may have low influence and rare-log debt. Neither connectedness nor a fixed small separator cardinality provides that missing selection/payment theorem.

# A016 stage 4: two weak shared separators with a single paid private reserve

Full FEI remains OPEN. This stage consumes A018's independent acceptance of the previous A016 connected-spine theorem and A019's exclusive derivative account. It closes the recorded next benchmark: two shared coordinates, unequal nonlinear masks, and no uniformly influential shared first pivot. This is a scoped test and payment certificate, not a universal separator-selection theorem or a new literature priority claim.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

All physical bits are independent fair bits in {0,1}; Boolean outputs are signs; entropy and complete reference CE are in bits. For every integer a>=1, every even m>=4, and every list of arbitrary Boolean masks A_j:{0,1}^2->{0,1}, let z,w be shared and let t_j be disjoint m-bit private blocks. Put

\[
c_j(t_j)=(-1)^{t_{j,1}t_{j,2}+\cdots+t_{j,m-1}t_{j,m}},\quad
P_j=1_{t_j=0},\quad f_j=c_j-2A_j(z,w)P_j,\quad F=\prod_j f_j.
\]

Since c_j(0)=1, these are actual Boolean functions. Let delta=2^-m, p_j=E A_j, P=sum p_j, J=I_private(F), d=Inf_z(F)+Inf_w(F), and E_zw=sum_{S contains z,w} Fhat(S)^2. The prescribed stopping tree queries z, then w on both children, and stops with the complete private products. Define T=sum_v Pr(v) 2I(D_v), and T_bad by keeping only nodes with exact B_v>4 Inf_v. The claimed all-parameter statements are

\[
J=am/2+2m\delta P,\quad I(F)=J+d,\quad
T=T_{private}+2E_{zw},\quad T_{private}\le2J,\quad
2E_{zw}\le d,\quad T_{bad}\le T\le2I(F).
\]

All terminal masks have complete uniform code cost am. The same private reserves and the one physical shared budget pay

\[
am+T+(3/\ln2)d\le4I(F).
\]

One fixed whole-set reference, explicitly defined below, has CE<=min(am+2,am+T+(3/ln2)d)<=3I(F). The terminal-inclusive physical-tree ledger is paid at rate4, separately from the stronger rate3 reference estimate.

For a=2 and unequal masks A_1=AND(z,w), A_2=OR(z,w), the root is connected and both shared influences equal delta, hence tend to zero. For every even m>=12, all three internal nodes are exact K4-bad. Nevertheless T/I tends to zero and CE/I tends to2. Thus low influence and badness at both candidate shared pivots do not by themselves prevent a paid stopping prefix.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Physical influence and genuine weak pivots

Flipping the value at the private origin adds two directed pivotal edges in each private direction because the paired quadratic equals+1 at the origin and at every adjacent point. A module activated at shared value b therefore has private influence m/2+2m delta. Averaging its activation gives J_j=m/2+2m delta p_j, and different private blocks cannot cancel each other's influences in the product. Hence J=sum J_j as displayed.

For a shared edge b,b' let s(b,b') be the number of masks whose value changes. Each changed module toggles precisely when its independent private block is at zero. Thus the product edge disagreement probability is

\[
d(b,b')=[1-(1-2\delta)^{s(b,b')}]/2.
\]

Average over the other shared bit to obtain each actual shared influence. Union bound gives d<=delta sum_j I(A_j as a Boolean sign). Each two-bit mask has I(A_j)<=4p_j: in each direction at most twice its support fraction is pivotal. Therefore d<=4delta P. In particular, for fixed a every shared influence is at most a delta and vanishes with m; no constant-influence shared pivot is assumed.

### 2. Reconstruct the accepted one-bit ownership interface

For arbitrary one-shared-bit modules with disjoint private cubes, character folding gives delta_j=min(d_j,1-d_j), q_j=1-2delta_j>=0. Their folded shared product has delta_*=(1-product q_j)/2; the original product has shared influence delta_* or1-delta_* and at least delta_*. For k_j=a_j b_j, Parseval/Poincare and the product-edge union bound give

4delta_j(1-delta_j)=Var(k_j)<=I(k_j)<=I(a_j)+I(b_j)=2J_j.

So J_j>=delta_j, and parity of independent disagreements proves 0<=sum delta_j-delta_*<=J-delta_*. The private-character fixture a_j=1,b_j=chi_{t_j} gives equality for every number of modules, establishing coefficient1 sharpness. Character shifts preserve Fourier entropy and pushed-forward complete CE.

For C_j=sum_U |U| ahat_j(U)bhat_j(U), a private derivative expansion gives I(D)=sum_j[J_j-Q_-j C_j]/2. Since kappa_j=(J_j-C_j)/2, this is t_j=(1-Q_-j)J_j/2+Q_-j kappa_j, with0<=t_j<=J_j. A normalized reference supplied for each child lifts through the mixture of child references and the actual parent conditional selector law. Log-sum gives root KL<=average child KL, hence root CE<=average terminal CE+Delta H. Inserting the reviewed B04-NODE-BOUNDARY@1 yields A019's stated sufficient allocation. This independently reconstructs A019-FOLDED-SEPARATOR-OWNER@1 at its exact scope, without a repair or use of its unproved optional scalar inequality. The present finite tests validate its derivative formula on a new restricted module family; they are not an exhaustive census of arbitrary modules. Verdict HOLDS; the universal allocation-existence line remains outside its statement.

### 3. A stricter half-private account for every actual shared edge

Fix one of z,w and a value of the other. Denote module endpoints by a_j,b_j. Let e_j indicate whether its mask changes and let Q_-j=(1-2delta)^{sum_{h!=j} e_h}. Its intrinsic derivative is0 or plus/minus P_j, so

\[
\kappa_j=m\delta e_j/2,\qquad
J_{j,edge}=m/2+m\delta(A_j(b)+A_j(b')).
\]

The independently reconstructed formula gives

\[
t_{j,edge}=(1-Q_{-j})J_{j,edge}/2+Q_{-j}\kappa_j.
\]

Here Q_-j is between0 and1 and kappa_j<=J_{j,edge}/2 (already true for delta<=1/2). Consequently0<=t_{j,edge}<=J_{j,edge}/2. Average over the other shared bit and sum the two query directions. This defines one owned account u_j, not a fresh private budget for each direction. Since each direction's averaged J_edge equals J_j, u_j<=J_j. Put T_private=2sum_j u_j<=2J.

The root z derivative contains one additional remaining-w energy E_zw. After z is restricted, the w derivatives have only private coordinates. Their weighted private energies give precisely the two edge averages just used. Equivalently Parseval gives

\[
T_{private}=2\sum_S |S\cap private|\,|S\cap\{z,w\}|\widehat F(S)^2,\qquad
T=T_{private}+2E_{zw}.
\]

Also E_zw<=min(Inf_z,Inf_w), so2E_zw<=d. Thus T<=2J+d<=2I. Exact bad-node filtering can only decrease this nonnegative bill. No assertion that those nodes are good is needed.

### 4. Retain every terminal and pay the shared cross energy once

At each of the four physical leaves use the normalized uniform reference on all am private Fourier bits, costing am, including the empty set. Assign each private block the single budget4J_j. Its terminal code and both directional boundary accounts cost m+2u_j<=m+2J_j; the remaining private reserve is at least2J_j-m=4m delta p_j. Summing gives reserve at least4m delta P.

The two-query influence telescope consumes exactly d, and the reviewed node inequality sums to

Delta_block H<=T+(3/ln2)d.

Write c=3/ln2. Since ln2>1/2, c<6. The extra shared/cross terms cost c d+2E_zw<= (c+1)d<7d. The single shared allocation4d leaves an additional need at most3d<=12delta P. The above private reserve is at least16delta P for m>=4. Therefore

am+T+c d<=4(J+d)=4I.

Every component is named: leaf cost m, owned private boundary2u_j, shared budget4d, and the residual point-edit reserve. No child entropy or KL rebate is spent a second time. The sufficient rate4 bound uses the reviewed node lemma; the exact T<=2I statement is self-contained.

### 5. One fixed normalized whole-set reference

For private Fourier set R and shared set L subset {z,w}, define actual row mass r(R)=sum_L Fhat(L,R)^2 and the fixed conditional nu(L|R)=Fhat(L,R)^2/r(R). Set

Q(L,R)=2^-am nu(L|R).

These conditionals are explicit functions of the four known leaf spectra: Fhat(L,R)=one-quarter sum_b chi_L(b) product_j[c_jhat(R_j)-2delta A_j(b)]. They are fixed for the function before sampling, not a choice of a new reference after observing a row. All private rows are positive: each terminal module coefficient is u times a sign or u times a sign minus2delta, nonzero for even m>=4. Reference normalization is exactly one. The zero true atoms are harmless and all branch, private, empty-sector and final shared ambiguity costs are included.

Its complete CE is am+B_block. Since B_block<=2 and I>=am/2, and am>=4,

CE<=am+2<=3I.

More substantively, entropy concavity of r=average actual terminal spectra gives H(r)>=average H(leaf). Hence CE<=am+H(F)-average H(leaf)<=am+T+c d<=4I: the owned physical ledger pays this same fixed reference. This concavity comparison is proved directly; neither A018's optional scalar inequality nor an oracle universal FEI reference is assumed. The rate3 bound is a width-two code bound, not advertised as a universal or new restricted FEI theorem.

### 6. Unequal AND/OR fixture: every shared query is bad and weak

Take a=2, A_1=zw and A_2=z OR w. Exactly one module changes on every shared edge, so Inf_z=Inf_w=delta, J=m+2m delta, I=m+(2m+2)delta. The unique GF(2) phase is the sum of the private quadratic phases plus zw P_1+(z+w+zw)P_2. The distinct highest monomials zw product t_1 and zw product t_2 connect all coordinates; they cannot cancel. Thus no nontrivial disjoint sign factor removes the shared interaction.

Write u=2^-m/2. The bent private coefficients are u e_j, e_j in {+1,-1}, with multiplicities N_+=(2^m+2^{m/2})/2, N_-=(2^m-2^{m/2})/2. For each private sign pair the four shared coefficients, in empty,z,w,zw order, are

\[
\delta e_1e_2-(3/2)\delta u e_1-(1/2)\delta u e_2+\delta^2,\quad
\delta u(e_1+e_2)/2-\delta^2,\quad
\delta u(e_1+e_2)/2-\delta^2,\quad
\delta u(e_1-e_2)/2+\delta^2.
\]

The closed spectrum gives E_zw=delta(1+delta)/2. The owned edge accounts give T_private=4m delta(1+delta), and therefore

\[
T=(4m+1)\delta(1+\delta).
\]

For equal private signs, the empty,z row weights are delta^2(1 minus/plus u)^4 and delta^3(1 minus/plus u)^2. Their ratio is at least1/(2delta), as (1-u)^2>=1/2 for m>=4. Keeping only the smaller-weight entropy term yields

\[
B_z\ge \delta(1-\delta)^2(m-1)/2.
\]

Indeed the two equal-sign classes have total smaller-sector mass delta(1-delta)^2/2, using the displayed multiplicities. On either z child exactly one module is a one-selector private point splice, while the other is an independent spectator. Each splice row has weights (u e-delta)^2 and delta^2, ratio at least1/(2delta), and there are1/delta rows. Therefore B_w,child>=delta(m-1). For even m>=12 both lower bounds exceed4delta; all three actual internal nodes are bad. This refutes the proposed *necessary* heuristic that a paid shared-first prefix requires a constant-influence or K4-good shared first pivot. It does not refute a sufficient good-pivot policy or universal coordinate selection.

Despite this exact badness,

T/I=[(4+1/m)delta(1+delta)]/[1+(2+2/m)delta] tends to0.

The complete reference is between2m and2m+2, and I/m tends to1. Thus CE/I tends to2. The registered limiting cost and boundary statements require only these squeeze bounds.

### 7. Honest universal gap and nonduplication

The first unsupported universal line is existence of a dimension-free separator/private-account policy with complete terminal references when a derivative changes an arbitrary terminal core, rather than a signed private point indicator with kappa<=J_edge/2. No such policy is proved. With k shared coordinates the present half-account only gives T_private<=kJ: it reuses the directional allowance k times, precisely the width problem exposed by A020. Moving from the two-bit test to arbitrary width by changing2 to k is not a universal closure.

The next mathematical target is to replace one point edit by an arbitrary child-certified private core and test whether kappa<=J_edge/2, or a signed terminal slack that replaces it, survives. This attacks the actual hypothesis enabling the paid ledger rather than collecting another bent-class example. A018's paired-row scalar target is preserved and is not repeated; A019/A020's own targets remain theirs.

# A016 stage 5: an entropy-zero terminal core can be charged repeatedly at bad selectors

Full FEI remains OPEN. No universal stopping-tree theorem is refuted.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

All physical bits are independent and fair, Boolean outputs are signs, entropy and complete reference CE use bits. For integers a>=1, M>=1 and even m>=6, set delta=2^-m. Take private gate u, private M-bit parity h(v)=(-1)^(sum v), and disjoint mask modules

\[
b_j(x_j,t_j)=c_j(t_j)-2x_j1_{t_j=0},\quad
c_j=(-1)^{t_{j,1}t_{j,2}+\cdots+t_{j,m-1}t_{j,m}},\quad
s=\prod_{j=1}^a b_j.
\]

Define F=1 when u=0 and F=h s when u=1. This is an actual Boolean family on n=1+M+a(m+1) coordinates. The prescribed bad-prefix tree queries x_1,...,x_a, then stops; all private data and u remain. Define T_bad as the reach-weighted sum of 2I(D) only at exact B>4Inf nodes. The claims are

\[
I_s=a[m/2+(m+1)\delta],\quad I_F=(M+I_s+1)/2,
\]

\[
T_{bad}=a(M+1/2)\delta+\frac{a^2m}{2}\delta+
\frac{a(a-1)(2m+1)}2\delta^2.
\]

The contribution from the same private parity coordinates alone is exactly aM delta. At EVERY reached selector node, Inf=delta/2 and B/Inf>=m-1>4. The finest disjoint sign factorization is connected at every such node, so optimal removal of independent physical factors changes none of this bill.

At fixed m>=6 and M=a^2,

T_bad/I_F ~ 2delta a -> infinity,
and the parity-only charge/I_F has the same asymptotic.

Querying u first instead is K2-good, with zero internal K4 bad-node bill. Both actual terminals have explicit complete references. One fixed normalized parent reference has

CE=2+a(m+1)/2<=4I_F.

Its CE/I_F tends to zero at M=a^2, fixed m. This is an obstruction to the specified fixed rule and to repeatedly paying its boundary from the same inherited core reserve, together with a paid alternative; it is not an FEI counterexample.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Consume the arbitrary-core interface at its stated scope

A019's core/edit identity is reconstructed independently. For arbitrary private sign g and region E, set b=g(-1)^1_E, D=g1_E and J=(I(g)+I(b))/2. A directed edge contributes its squared real difference divided by4 times the cube size. Internal pivotal edges contribute equally to J and I(D); external pivotal edges contribute to J alone; crossing edges have complementary child pivotal statuses, and their contributions cancel in2I(D)-J. Therefore

2I(D)-J=P_in-P_out.

This yields exactly the half-private iff condition, and every point edit satisfies it for every Boolean core. For disjoint modules, expanding the private cross-energy gives t_j=J_j,edge/2+Q_-j(P_in-P_out)_j/2, with Q_-j the product of other branch correlations. For density(E_j)<=1/2 these correlations are nonnegative; under P_in<=P_out the two shared directions give one account u_j<=J_j. The two-query bill is2sum u_j+2E_zw<=2J+d. A mixture of supplied four-terminal references, multiplied by the actual parent shared conditional law, is normalized; log-sum gives root KL<=average terminal KL. Thus the reviewed node inequality gives CE<=cbar+T+(3/ln2)d, exactly the claimed conditional scope. No terminal-reference supply, scalar subadditivity or universal recursive budget is inferred.

Verdict: A019-INTERNAL-PIVOTAL-EDIT-ACCOUNT@1 HOLDS at its stated scope, with its previously accepted node-boundary dependency retained for CE only. No substantive repair is supplied. The independent checker below covers every two-bit core/edit pair; the analytic reconstruction, not this small domain, establishes the full identity and conditional implication.

### 2. Exact gate spectrum keeps the core terminal free of entropy debt

Since h is a nonempty parity character, its mean is zero. Write chi_u=(-1)^u. Then

F=(1+chi_u)/2+(1-chi_u)h s/2.

The constant sector and the h sector have disjoint PRIVATE Fourier labels. The squared root spectrum has mass1/4 at empty and {u}, and for every mask Fourier set S two copies of s_hat(S)^2/4, with all M parity coordinates present and u absent/present. Hence

H(F)=2+H(s)/2.

The parity core has H(h)=0 and I(h)=M, so it carries genuine unused influence capacity, rather than a forgotten entropy bill. Direct edge averaging gives Inf_u=1/2, every parity coordinate influence1/2, every mask-coordinate influence half its influence in s. This proves I_F=(M+I_s+1)/2. For a point splice, private influence is m/2+m delta, and its selector influence is delta, hence I_s as stated.

### 3. Every chosen selector is exactly bad

One unqueried module's coefficients on private row T are c_hat(T)-delta and delta in the two selector sectors, with c_hat(T)=plus/minus2^-m/2. For m>=4 the first squared coefficient is at least delta/2, while the second is delta^2. Retaining only the latter's conditional entropy term gives at least delta^2(m-1) per row. There are1/delta rows, so the splice's B_x>=delta(m-1), Inf_x=delta.

At an actual selector prefix, s is still a disjoint product of reached module branches and unqueried modules. Tensoring these spectators does not change the queried module's B/Inf. The balanced private h also remains unchanged, so the disjoint gate split proves

B_x(F_prefix)=B_x(s_prefix)/2,
Inf_x(F_prefix)=delta/2.

Thus B/Inf>=m-1>4 for every even m>=6 at EVERY reached node, with no average-badness or h2 surrogate. The finite m4 controls below are also strictly K4-bad, but the registered all-parameter statement uses the simpler proved m>=6 hypothesis.

### 4. No physical spectator factor can be removed

The unique GF(2) sign exponent is

u[sum_i v_i+sum_j(private quadratic_j+x_j product_l(1+t_j,l))].

Every parity coordinate is linked to u by u v_i. An unqueried module has the uncancelled highest monomial u x_j product_l t_j,l. A reached x_j=0 module retains its paired terms u t_j,2r-1 t_j,2r, and every data coordinate is linked to u. A reached x_j=1 module retains the highest monomial u product_l t_j,l, which cannot cancel with a quadratic for m>=4. All remaining coordinates therefore lie in one ANF interaction component.

A Boolean sign factorization across a proper physical partition would split its unique phase into polynomials on separate coordinate blocks. These crossing monomials exclude that factorization. This establishes the finest factorization statement at every reached node, rather than merely at the root. Spectral sectors may be coded jointly even when no physical factor exists; the paid reference below does exactly that.

### 5. The same entropy-zero core is charged at every bad query

Let D_s be the queried selector derivative at a reached prefix. Its squared norm is delta. For the outer gated product,

D_F=1_{u=1}h D_s.

Dirichlet energy of products on disjoint physical blocks is additive with squared-norm weights. Since I(1_{u=1}h)=M/2+1/4 and its squared norm is1/2,

2I(D_F)=(M+1/2)delta+I(D_s).

Only the M parity coordinates contribute M delta to this bill. It is the SAME physical core, at every prefix and every selected selector. All nodes are bad, so exact bad-node localization discards none of that contribution.

At depth j, average over all2^j earlier selector assignments. Each reached private module has average influence m/2+m delta; each other unqueried module has influence m/2+(m+1)delta. The point-indicator derivative itself has energy m delta/2. Therefore

E I(D_s)=am delta/2+delta^2[(a-1)(m+1)-j].

Summing j=0,...,a-1 yields the registered exact T_bad formula. There is no probability reset: each depth is averaged with its actual fair reach weights. The same parity contribution sums to aM delta.

At M=a^2 and fixed m, I_F=a^2/2+O(a), T_bad=delta a^3+O(a^2), and its parity part is exactly delta a^3. Thus both bill/I ratios are asymptotic to2delta a. The parity core's one source budget4I(h)=4M cannot fund aM delta uniformly in a. A normalized terminal certificate with cost0 does not authorize creating a new4M reserve at each later bad query.

The internal-pivotal diagnostic is genuinely positive: for the private gated core g=1 on u0 and h on u1, edit E={u1}, P_in=M/2 and P_out=0, so2kappa-J=M/2. Unlike A020's parity-mask control, that correction now appears behind genuinely bad rare selector queries. The construction does not claim that every positive internal-pivotal correction must be charged; it specifies a policy for which the actual localized charge is unavoidable.

At each actual selector-first terminal, the selected mask modules have become specified bent or point-flipped m-bit branches. The gate/core split still applies. Assign a uniform reference to all am remaining mask-data bits, with the same two constant atoms and two parity-active copies. Its normalized complete cost is2+am/2 at EVERY leaf. Thus the bad-prefix terminal cost is explicitly retained, not treated as zero; adding its average only strengthens the divergence, and does not affect its leading2delta a asymptotic. The zero cost discussed above belongs only to the known parity core.

### 6. A private-gate-first prefix pays the same function completely

At u, Inf=1/2 and B<=1=2Inf, so u is K2-good and hence K4-good. Stop after this query. The u0 leaf is constant with complete point reference cost0. The u1 leaf is the parity character h times the disjoint product s; use the point reference at all M parity coordinates and the uniform reference on the a(m+1) mask bits. Its complete cost is a(m+1), including every terminal mask bit; its physical influence is M+I_s. The physical telescope is I_F=1/2+(M+I_s)/2.

Define one fixed parent reference: put mass1/4 at empty and {u}; on each mask set S put1/[4*2^{a(m+1)}] at the two active sets containing all parity coordinates and u absent/present. These two sectors are disjoint and the total reference mass is exactly1. The true active spectral mass is1/2, giving the COMPLETE root CE

2+a(m+1)/2.

For m>=6 this is <=4I_F: subtracting it from4I_F gives2M+a(m-1)/2+2a(m+1)delta>=0. Root gate code costs2, paid by4Inf_u=2, while average terminal cost is a(m+1)/2 and is paid from the remaining physical influence once. No terminal is free apart from its explicitly defined character/constant description. No external certificate for an arbitrary unknown core is assumed.

With M=a^2, CE/I_F~(m+1)/a tends to0. This dilution is stated honestly: the family has abundant influence and bounded entropy cost, so it is a stopping-rule falsifier, not a possible FEI counterexample. It is useful precisely because that abundance is still insufficient for repeated copies of the same reserve.

### 7. First unsupported universal line and mechanism change

The false diagnostic is 'a fixed bad-selector-first prefix can always be paid by the existing terminal-core reserve once per node, provided the core has a complete low-cost reference.' The quantified family above refutes it even for an entropy-zero core, with connected physical factorization and exact bad-node filtering retained. A019's proved one-/two-query sufficient statements are preserved: they do not assert an unbounded-depth reuse of that reserve. A020's zero-erasure caution is also preserved.

The first unsupported universal line is a selected gate/data policy that keeps one inherited signed terminal reserve through a nonbalanced data restriction when the actual conditional branch reference changes, rather than making a fresh copy of that reserve at the next selector. The constructed u-first policy works because a balanced core exposes a uniformly influential gate and disjoint constant/active rows. The next mechanism should preserve or pay that separation through a core reveal; another tensor-splice selector-first expansion would repeat a now-closed failure.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Bits are independent uniform physical 0/1 bits; signs are Boolean; entropy, cross-entropy CE and KL are in bits. Inherit the supplied A020 full root reference; after each query use its frequency marginal for BOTH physical children. No actual-spectrum reset, new activation lift, discarded terminal, or renewed bank is permitted. Test q then v0, and separately inspect the later physical leaf q0,v=0.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Independent source reconstruction and the one fixed root reference

The k-bit point indicator has Walsh coefficients delta everywhere. Each h_q child has mean +/-delta, main coefficient1-delta at{u}, and2^(k+1)-1 coefficients of magnitude delta. Their squared laws are equal, called P. In the parent, every u-absent child coefficient is transferred to queried frequency q PRESENT, while every u-present coefficient has q absent. Thus the positive empty unqueried row forces q present; the parent mean and full empty frequency mass are zero. The consistent incoming core reference is exactly

Q_h=(P_h+point_{q})/2.

This is A020's actual conditional lift of (P+point_empty)/2, reconstructed from signed Walsh coefficients. Its full empty-set reference is zero. With exact P_s uniform on four labels, the SAME full physical activation reference is

Q_F(gate,T)=[Q_h(core)*P_s(mask)+point_empty(T)]/4,

for BOTH gate frequencies. The true root conditional gate frequency is uniform, including the empty data row. This fully normalized reference contains its ghost mass and all physical labels.

The source posterior refund at either freshly lifted child is

D_child=[a log2(a/t)+(1+a)log2((1+t)/(1+a))]/2,
a=delta^2/4, t=(1+delta^2)/8.

Parent refund and core KL refund are zero. The formula and its positive limit (1/2)log2(9/8) independently establish the source forced-row divergence; finite checks additionally certify its stated1/32 lower bound on our frozen domain. Our independent review accepts the spectra, forced support, exact refund and divergent-ratio clauses plus reference eligibility. It does not purport to accept every other source clause (in particular its full K1 erasure proof or all-k1/32 estimate) merely from these checks. The source statement/hash is preserved.

### 2. Marginal transport is a full-code identity

For any Boolean f and supplied normalized Q with finite CE, query a physical bit i. Set Q'=sum_{i-frequency}Q, identical for both children. The exact Walsh identity gives average_b P_(f_b)(T)=sum_e P_f(e,T). Therefore

CE(f,Q)=average_b CE(f_b,Q')+L_i,
L_i=sum_(e,T) P_f(e,T) log2[Q'(T)/Q(e,T)]>=0.

This retains every atom and both terminal costs. The support of a positive child atom is covered because its averaged mass is positive. Conditional likelihoods have ratio>=1. Iterating on an adaptive tree gives CE_root=weighted sum L_i+average leaf CE; this is an accounting identity conditional on having the root reference, NOT a construction of a universal cheap reference. It does not imply child rate eligibility. Our checks reconstruct it from independent truth tables and actual restrictions, not from an author's checker.

### 3. First query, explicitly paid conversion, and second query

Let Q1 be the marginal of the FIXED Q_F after q. Its core marginal is (P+point_empty)/2. All inherited gate conditionals remain uniform. Fresh A020 child references instead use the actual gate conditional on their empty data row; their data priors are the same. Thus the only conversion is the complete conditional KL

E=CE(child,Q1)-CE(child,Q_fresh)
 =(1+mu^2)/2 * KL_Ber(p_gate ||1/2), mu=+/-delta/2.

Using KL<=chi-square/ln2 and ln2>1/2 gives 0<=E<4mu^2=delta^2. This conversion is retained as a terminal debit, not erased. The exact inherited first-query debit is

L_q=(1/2)log2[(9+delta^2)/8]
 +(delta^2/8)log2[(9+delta^2)/(1+delta^2)].

Only the empty unqueried core/mask row has both q-reference sectors; its two gate copies have true masses1/4 anddelta^2/16. In particular L_q tends to(1/2)log2(9/8), not zero. The original fresh-child insertion J equals L_q+E. Conservatively log(10/8)<1/2, log2(10)<4 and delta^2<=1/64 imply J<1/4+3delta^2/2<=35/128<5/8. Hence the previously booked first-query ceiling is still valid INCLUDING conversion.

Now expose v0 at each actual q child and inherit Q2=sum_(v0-frequency)Q1. Both queries have actual full-function influence delta/2; all four physical histories are retained. Partition second-query Fourier rows into: ordinary paired small rows, main-u pair, empty-core pair with/without the constant gate contribution. Ordinary pairs have equal reference masses, conditional charge1, and total true mass at most delta. For the main-u pair, absent-sector cost is at most delta^2 using log(1+t)<=t/ln2, and present-sector cost at most k delta^2. Empty-core absent-sector cost is at most2delta^2; present-sector cost at most(k+2)delta^2 because its largest likelihood ratio is below16/delta^2. Consequently, at EITHER q child,

L_v < delta+(2k+5)delta^2 <3delta <=3/8.

The strict middle inequality follows from (2k+5)2^-k<=11/8<2 for k>=3. This is a second debit from the SAME owner; the first fixed debit is never renewed.

The supplied child-core costs c_b=H_k+K_core satisfy K_core<=1 and H_k<4(k+1)delta, while I(h_b)=1+(k-1)delta. Here H_k=eta((1-delta)^2)+(2^(k+1)-1)delta^2(2k); its main term is<4delta by -(1-epsilon)ln(1-epsilon)<=epsilon and ln2>1/2, and its small terms sum to<4kdelta. Physical edges give the displayed influence. Their unused core surplus is S_b=4I(h_b)-c_b>3-8delta>=2. The private HALF reserve is thus>=1. Mask half-budget leaves1 after paying its exact code; gate profile is at most2 and any positive-mean gate shortfall is at mostdelta, paid by that mask remainder. The private half-reserve is untouched before insertion. One such averaged reserve pays J+L_v<5/8+3/8=1 (indeed<83/128 under our sharper first bound), with no renewal. The conversion E is included in J. Complete final CE is retained: by marginal transport its leaf average is at most the inherited q-child average. Thus this conservative allocation pays the converted terminal costs and both queries; it does not count a released terminal bill as a newly created bank.

### 4. Every complete two-query terminal is eligible, for all k

Q2's core masses are t=(1-2delta+2delta^2)/2 at{u}, (1/2+delta^2) atempty, and delta^2 at every other remaining core frequency. In particular t>=25/64. At v0=1, h=chi_u for BOTH q branches; its full activation cost is

CE=1+(1/2)log2(16/t)<4,
I=3/2.

At v0=0 the two child means are+/-2delta, the main coefficient is1-2delta, and each other core coefficient has magnitude2delta. The full inherited terminal cost has four disjoint groups: empty data row (true mass(1+delta^2)/2, code length<=2); main-u group (mass(1-2delta)^2/2, length<6); other nonempty core groups (mass2delta-4delta^2, length4+2k); shared-only nonempty group (mass3delta^2/2, length<=5). Hence

CE <4+4(k-1)delta+(9/2-8k)delta^2
 <=4+4(k-1)delta.

Its influence is 3/2+(k-2)delta minus/plus delta/2. Even the smaller rate4 budget exceeds the displayed upper bound by at least2-6delta>0. All four normalized full references therefore have CE<4I, uniformly over k>=3. Parent/root eligibility also holds: CE_root<=H_k/2+7/2<7/2+2(k+1)delta, versus4I_root=6+2kdelta. Each inherited q child costs at most7/2+2(k+1)delta+delta^2, whereas its smaller4I is6+(2k-3)delta. These costs are explicit complete codes, not root-only entropy estimates.

### 5. Exact boundary: eligibility is not inherited indefinitely

Continue erasing q and ALL v frequencies of this SAME root reference, without resetting it. The core marginal on u is ((1+delta)/2,(1-delta)/2). At the ACTUAL history q0,v=0, h=+1, F is1 atx0 and s atx1, with I=3/4. The inherited reference has empty data atoms (9+delta)/32 and shared-only atoms (1+delta)/32 at both gate frequencies. The true empty data row has mass5/8 and the remaining true mass is3/8. Therefore

CE=5-[5log2(9+delta)+3log2(1+delta)]/8.

For every k>=6 this exceeds4I=3. Equivalently (9+delta)^5(1+delta)^3<2^16. This polynomial is increasing in delta, and the endpoint delta=1/64 is certified by the exact integer inequality577^5*65^3<2^64. The limit excess is2-(5/8)log2(9)>0. This is a supplied-reference eligibility obstruction, not a failure of FEI or of the telescoping identity. Its physical history is rare and has an unused bank; no claim that the excess is unpayable is made.

The first unsupported universal line is that every transported child is automatically eligible at the SAME rate, or that any signed terminal deficit has an unspent branch-specific owner. The explicit later terminal disproves the former shortcut. Universal construction of a cheap initial Q and conservation of signed conditional banks for arbitrary cores remain OPEN.

# A016 stage 7: one original bank pays nonflat activation histories

Full FEI remains OPEN. The inherited-reference calculation extends from a point event to every activation A=1_{v=0}B(z), where v has k>=5 bits and B is ANY three-bit indicator. This includes unequal Fourier masses and unequal actual child spectra. After q, every adaptive order exposing the activation bits has expected signed rate-4 reserve >121/64. The SAME original half-core bank pays the booked insertion, conversion, all remaining query charges and final positive terminal deficits, leaving >5/128. This is a supplied-reference continuation, not a universal construction.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

All physical bits are independent uniform 0/1 bits, chi_b=(-1)^b, all logarithms are base two. For an arbitrary finite-bit indicator A of probability p<=1/8 put h=chi_u(1-A)+chi_q A and F=1 at gate x=0, hs at x=1. Here s is the two-bit sign point defect [-1,1,1,1]. Write J_A=4 sum_T |T| Ahat(T)^2, the physical total influence of the 0/1 indicator (flip-probability convention). The supplied root reference is Q_h=(P_h+point_q)/2 and Q_F(e,T)=[Q_h(T_core)P_s(T_mask)+point_empty(T)]/4 at BOTH gate frequencies. Every descendant inherits its frequency marginal. Expose q first, then every activation bit in any adaptive order, retaining u, x and the two mask bits. No reset or renewed bank is allowed.

Let P be the common squared spectrum of either h|q child and c=CE(P,(P+point_empty)/2). The general sufficient admission is c<=J_A+2. Under this admission the original bank S=2(1-p+J_A/2)-c/2 pays the complete ledger below and leaves >5/128. An unconditional instance of the admission is A=point_k B for every k>=5 and every three-bit B (including constants). The stage advances the saved signed-bank NEXT; the original addressing baseline is retired.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

1. General activation identity. At q fixed, h_q=chi_u off A and +/-1 on A. Its Fourier coefficients are (1-p) at {u}, +/-p at empty, and two copies of +/-Ahat(T) for each nonempty activation T. Thus the two children have the same squared law P. Their influence is 1-p+J_A/2. At the parent, q moves the u-absent coefficients onto q-present labels; no coefficient interferes. Hence CE(P_h,Q_h)=c, the root is balanced, and

C_root=c/2+3,   I_root=3/2+J_A/4.

The extra ghost point at q is retained. After q it is exactly point_empty. Fresh child activation references differ only on the empty-data gate conditional; the inherited conditional remains uniform. Their conversion E satisfies 0<=E<p^2 for p>0, and E=0 for p=0. Indeed with mu=p/2 its exact value is (1+mu^2)/2 times KL(Ber((1+mu)^2/[2(1+mu^2)]) || Ber(1/2)); chi-square/ln2 and ln2>1/2 give the bound. The denominator is strictly positive. Let J_first be the actual root-to-fresh-child debit. It is L_q+E, not a renewed reserve.

2. Any supplied finite-cost Q transports by Q'=sum_e Q(e,T). The actual Walsh restriction identity gives avg_b P_child(T)=sum_e P_parent(e,T), so

CE_parent=avg CE_child+sum_(e,T) P_parent(e,T) log(Q'(T)/Q(e,T)).

Every summand is nonnegative and support is preserved: a positive child mass has positive erased parent mass. On any finite adaptive tree these full charges telescope with the actual physical reaches. No arbitrary-probability surrogate or free terminal is substituted.

3. Complete endpoint depends only on p. Parseval gives sum Ahat^2=p. After q and all activation frequencies are erased, Q_core(empty)=(1+p)/2 and Q_core({u})=(1-p)/2. Every actual inactive history has core chi_u; the active histories have constants +1 and -1 with equal total weights p/2. Their complete inherited costs are

C_par=5-(1/2)log((9+p)(1-p)),
C_con=5-[5log(9+p)+3log(1+p)]/8,
C_end=(1-p)C_par+p C_con.

The corresponding influences are 3/2, 3/4 and 5/4. For 0<=p<=1/8, C_par>27/8, C_con>23/8, C_con<25/8, and therefore C_end>53/16=424/128. Exact endpoint checks suffice: 9^4<2^13, (73/8)^4<2^13, (9/8)^4<2; monotonicity covers the interval. C_par<5<6 and C_con<25/8<5 show that ONLY the positive constant history may have a deficit. Its full weighted positive deficit D=(p/2)(C_con-3)_+ obeys D<p/16<=1/128. This deficit is explicitly booked, even though the aggregate signed code budget already retains it.

4. One original bank. The fresh q-child activation has cost c/2+3-L_q-E, at most c/2+3-E, and influence 1+(1-p+J_A/2)/2 minus/plus p/4. Its complete rate-4 surplus is therefore 1+S minus/plus p+E+L_q, at least S+1-p. Thus S is an actual unused part of the supplied fresh-child budget; the mask/gate remainder pays the remaining profile and is not borrowed again. Let C_0 be the mean inherited cost after q. All later charges total C_0-C_end, irrespective of the adaptive exposure order. The total debit including the already-booked insertion is

J_first+(C_0-C_end)=C_root-C_end+E.

The exact remaining bank after paying the final positive deficit separately is consequently

S-[J_first+C_0-C_end]-D
 = C_end-c-1-2p+J_A-E-D.

Under c<=J_A+2, use C_end>424/128, 2p<=32/128, E<2/128 and D<1/128 to obtain a strict lower bound 5/128. The p=0 endpoint has E=D=0 and the same strict conclusion. This is an explicit single-owner allocation. It does not say each rare history is separately rate-4 eligible. The admission is a supplied-core code condition, not asserted for arbitrary cores.

5. Unconditional nonflat family. Take A(v,z)=1_{v=0}B(z), k bits v, r bits z, beta=E B, delta=2^-k, p=delta beta. Define L_B=sum_U Bhat(U)^2 log(1/Bhat(U)^2), omitting zeros. Exact tensor coefficients give

H(P)=eta((1-p)^2)-eta(p^2)+4kp+2delta L_B,
J_A=2kp+delta J_B,

where eta(t)=t log(1/t), with eta(0)=0. Every nonzero Bhat is an integer multiple of 2^-r; thus L_B<=2r beta. Also eta((1-p)^2)<3p for p>0 (ln2>2/3), while eta(p^2)>=0. It follows that H(P)-J_A<(2k+4r+3)p. Since c<=H(P)+1, a sufficient condition is (2k+4r+3)2^-k<=1. For r=3,k>=5 the left side decreases and is at most25/32. The p=0 case has H=J_A=0,c=1 and is admitted directly. This proves c<J_A+2 for EVERY three-bit B and all k>=5. The proof uses actual Boolean lattice coefficients, not an arbitrary spectral-law relaxation.

There is also a uniform intermediate signed reserve. H(P)<(4k+4r+3)p, so for r=3,k>=5, c<1+35/32=67/32 and C_root<259/64. Any partial adaptive exposure can be completed to the endpoint, giving expected remaining influence >=I_end=3/2-p/2 and expected CE<=C_root. Since p<=1/32,

E(4I_leaf-CE_leaf)>6-2p-259/64>=121/64.

Both signed terms E(4I-CE)_+ and E(CE-4I)_+ remain present; their difference is this reserve. Local nonconstant or constant eligibility is not required. This estimate applies to partial stopping partitions as well as fixed-depth prefixes, provided only activation coordinates are exposed after q.

6. Independent review consumed. A020's point-event all-prefix formulas Q_t, I_t and R_t are reconstructed from an independently written integer Walsh implementation at k=3,6,8, all t=0,...,k and both q signs, retaining active/inactive physical probabilities and both signed terminal terms. The all-k proof in its sections 1--4 is checked algebraically: the decreasing bound (2k+4)2^-k, the entropy upper bound using ln2>2/3, the k=3 rational417/128 endpoint and the stated terminal estimates all hold. The original bank margin >1/32 is accepted at its exact point-event scope. This review does not accept every statement of its broader source history or silently promote its Lean timeout. Its separate nonconstant penultimate claim is not needed for the extension here; no whole-report acceptance is inferred from the finite checks.

First unsupported universal line: an arbitrary Boolean activation/core must have a constructible supplied reference with c<=J_A+2, or an alternative paid source bank replacing that condition. For arbitrary A the entropy-minus-influence admission may fail; this stage neither assumes it universally nor proves full FEI. The new all-dimensional theorem is confined to padded fixed-width activations and the stated complete supplied reference.

# A017 / B04: recursive addressing with controlled input reuse

Run: `20261011T215725Z-fleet05-A017-recursive-addressing`. Full FEI remains OPEN. Local proof and exact self-check complete; independent acceptance, supervisor adoption, and mirror verification pending. The packet's construction and exact-ledger deliverables are complete at the stated scope.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For every finite n,a, binary linear map M:F2^n->F2^a, and degree-at-most-two binary polynomial q, consider the actual Boolean function F(x,y)=(-1)^q(x)y_(Mx), with uniform independent physical binary controls x and sign data y_z for each a-bit state. The same controls and data may be reused arbitrarily often in the state program M_t=A_t M_(t-1)+B_t and phase program q_t=q_(t-1)+p_t. Distinct terminal states still name distinct physical data bits. Entropy uses bits; influence is ordinary total uniform-cube influence. No fresh selector assumption is made between levels.

The exact result is H=2r+k and I=1+(c+b)/2+d, where r=rank M, k=rank of the polar form of q restricted to ker M, c counts nonzero address columns, b counts zero address columns with nonzero polar row, and d counts zero address columns with zero polar row and linear phase coefficient one. The spectrum has 2^(2r+k) atoms of equal squared magnitude 2^(-2r-k). It follows that H<=4(I-1-d)<=4(I-1).

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

Execution protocol `2026-10-11-execution-v2`, README, CURRENT_STATUS (2026-10-11T20:06:24Z snapshot), CLAIM_REGISTRY, OBSTRUCTION_REGISTRY, PACKET_STATUS, STREAM_NEXT_ACTIONS, AGENT_ASSIGNMENTS and the campaign plan were ingested. The A017 contract specifically requires proving reuse rather than silently applying disjoint composition. ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

The full proof is in `RECURSIVE_ADDRESSING.tex`. Its essential reconstruction is as follows. On each affine fiber x_z+K, the phase is a quadratic sign with the same polar rank k. A quadratic sign on a D-dimensional binary space has Fourier squared magnitudes either zero or 2^(-k): its autocorrelation vanishes off the radical of its polar form, and on the radical the phase difference is a linear character. Squared Fourier coefficients are the Walsh transform of that autocorrelation. The rank of an alternating form is even, by successively splitting nondegenerate two-dimensional pairs.

For a reachable label z, the signed indicator a_z(x)=1_(Mx=z)(-1)^q(x) has Fourier coefficients 2^(-r) times those fiber coefficients, up to signs. Ambient frequency restriction to K has 2^r lifts. Thus a_z has 2^(r+k) nonzero coefficients of squared magnitude 2^(-2r-k). There are 2^r reachable labels, and their data frequencies are distinct singleton coordinates y_z. This proves the exact flat spectrum and H=2r+k. History collisions are resolved in this signed indicator before squaring; there are no unaccounted cross-label collisions.

Flipping an active-address control changes the terminal data label, so its output disagreement is 1/2 even when the phase changes simultaneously. Flipping an inactive-address control changes only the phase, whose derivative is l_i+sum_j Q_ij x_j: influence 1/2 if nonconstant, otherwise l_i. Flipping a reachable y_z is pivotal with probability 2^(-r); the total data influence is one. These give the exact physical influence ledger.

Let V span the c active address directions and the b remaining directions with nonzero polar row. Both address map and polar form vanish on all other coordinate directions; M restricted to V retains rank r. The polar rank on ker M is therefore at most c+b-r. Hence 2r+k<=r+c+b<=2(c+b)=4(I-1-d). This uses no universal FEI premise and includes n=0, a=0, rank zero, and linear/constant phases.

At stage t, recompute these five statistics from M_t and q_t, including the changed kernel. Exact differences are Delta H=2 Delta r+Delta k and Delta I=(Delta c+Delta b)/2+Delta d. For n=a=m, cyclically adding x_j to state coordinate j makes M return to zero after every two cycles. With q=0, H=2t and I=1+t/2 on the first cycle, then H=2(2m-t) and I=1+(2m-t)/2 on the second. The tower may have arbitrarily many levels while the final function is y_0 at every even cycle. Fresh-stage accounting is false for this program.

The gate label is paid once: r bits for the reachable terminal label J and r+k bits for the conditional control-frequency support. This is an exact normalized spectral code of total length 2r+k. A stacked physical state transcript has entropy R=rank[M_1;...;M_t], and retains R-r_t bits given the final state. Those discarded transcript bits are not necessary gate labels for the final spectrum. The bases defining the code are fixed for the family; this is not a universal representation-description cost or a queue-reference theorem.

For a nontrivial infinite-dimensional regime, let q=sum_(j=1)^m x_(2j-1)x_(2j) and address h full pairs: H=2m+2h, I=1+m for 0<=h<=m. Quadratic fiber entropy remains when h<m. The unsigned/affine-phase case is established affine-cell addressing, not a new class. Moreover, the constant-four closure itself can be recovered by querying all c active physical controls first and applying baseline orthogonal-neighbor closure to the remaining quadratic phase restrictions, which independently satisfy H<=2I. What this stage adds is a closed exact rank ledger and cancellation accounting for the reused syntax, with a tighter 2r+k entropy calculation than that reorganization's 2c bound. Publication novelty is not asserted.

For arbitrary Boolean phase g, the exact surviving identity is H(g(x)y_(Mx))=2r+2^(-r)sum_z H(g restricted to x_z+ker M); influence is 1+c/2+sum_(i:M e_i=0) Inf_i(g). Entropy on the kernel is invariant under its choice of linear basis; influence is not. Substituting arbitrary oblique kernel influences for the physical budget is unsupported. The first unproved universal line is a dimension-independent payment of the average conditional fiber entropy by the displayed physical influence budget. At M=0 this is full FEI. No universal claim has been closed.

# A017 / B04 stage 2: point-flipped quadratic addressing

Run `20261011T234546Z-fleet05-A017-recursive-addressing`. Full FEI OPEN. Exact ledger and restricted stopping-tree proof complete locally; independent acceptance and supervisor transfer pending. B04 lead v2's assigned point-flipped-fiber target is resolved at the stated scope.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Let n>=1, M:F2^n->F2^a be any fixed linear address map, and q a quadratic polynomial with q(0)=0. With independent uniform binary controls x and distinct sign data y_z, let b=(-1)^q, g=b-2*1_(x=0), and F*=g(x)y_(Mx). This is a single genuine Boolean point flip. The exact entropy and physical influence formulas below hold even with linear terms in q. The stopping-tree theorem requires q to have zero linear term. Input reuse in any program computing M is allowed; the final data coordinates remain distinct. Entropy is in bits, physical influence uses the uniform cube, and I(D_i) for a real derivative means its Fourier Dirichlet energy, not a Boolean pivotal probability.

Resolved: exact H/I for every such quadratic/address pair; finite certificates through n=6 for the specified frozen families; the fixed-r limit for the standard matching quadratic and prefix address map; exact root physical boundary charges; and a nonreusing, terminal-inclusive tree with zero bad-node boundary charge at threshold K=2 and H(F*)<=4(I(F*)-1).

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

The updated BOARD.md (2026-10-11 23:39 UTC), B04 STREAM_NOTES.md v2 (23:25 UTC), own STATUS.md, and previous stage's full RECURSIVE_ADDRESSING.tex were read. ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Full derivation: `POINT_FLIPPED_FIBERS.tex`.

Put r=rank M, D=n-r and k=rank of the polar form of q on ker M. On the zero fiber the unflipped quadratic has nonzero Fourier amplitudes alpha=2^(-k/2). Their sum is b(0)=1, so their positive and negative counts are N_plus=(2^k+2^(k/2))/2 and N_minus=(2^k-2^(k/2))/2. There are N_zero=2^D-2^k zero coefficients. Flipping the origin subtracts epsilon=2^(1-D) from every fiber coefficient. Thus the exact post-flip spectrum consists of the masses (alpha-epsilon)^2, (alpha+epsilon)^2, epsilon^2 with those three counts, zero masses omitted. Call its exact entropy E_(D,k). Only label zero changed, giving

H(F*)=2r+k+2^(-r)(E_(D,k)-k).

Physically, active address controls still have influence 1/2. On an inactive control i, the point flip toggles the two directed edge endpoints 0,e_i. Its influence changes by (1-2*l_i)2^(1-n). Adding this to the previous exact ledger gives the complete influence. Reachable data influence remains 2^(-r) per label, summing to one.

For even n, standard q=sum x_(2j-1)x_(2j), and prefix address rank r, set t=r mod 2, delta=2^(-n), D=n-r. Then k=D-t and

I(F*)=1+n/2+2(n-r)delta.

When u=2^(1-D+k/2)<=1/2,

H(F*)=n+r-t+4delta(1-2^(-t))(D+t-2)+(4-12*2^(-t))delta/ln2+R,

with |R|<=16*2^r*delta^2/ln2. The proof expands (1-z)^2 ln(1-z) using its convergent series and bounds the weighted remainder by u^4/6+(2/3)alpha*u^3. This is a proved error bound, not a floating fit. Hence at fixed r, along even n to infinity, H/I tends to 2. Even r has a small negative entropy correction; odd r has a positive order-n*delta leakage term from the unpaired fiber coordinate. The exact histogram applies to the small dimensions where that Taylor condition fails.

Root physical boundary charges C_i=2I(D_i) for the same prefix/matching family are explicit. Write L=n-r and tau_i=1 when an unaddressed control's partner is also unaddressed. Each addressed control has C_i=(n+1)/2+2L*delta. Each unaddressed control has C_i=(n+1)/2+2delta(n+1+2tau_i). For data y_z, C_(y_z)=2^(-r)(r+k+2w(z))+1_(z=0)*4L*delta, where w=0 for even r and otherwise is the last address bit. Their total is n+4L*delta. The sum over all physical coordinates is n(n+3)/2+2delta[L(n+r+3)+2k], whose ratio to I grows linearly with n. All controls carry large unlocalized boundary energy, and data coordinates carry an additional total of order n. This does not obstruct FEI; it obstructs blindly paying the entire boundary moment.

The stopping rule avoids that charge. Query all c controls with nonzero M columns; each pivot has influence 1/2. After the address is fixed, nonzero physical assignments exclude the flipped point and yield quadratic-sign leaves times one data sign. On the all-zero branch, choose a control whose remaining quadratic polar row is nonzero. Its influence is 1/2+2^(1-d)>=1/2, with d remaining controls. Its one-child is a quadratic terminal; its zero-child preserves the same structure. Stop when no quadratic interactions remain: that last leaf is a point defect times a fixed data sign.

Every internal node therefore obeys B_i<=1<=2Inf_i. Its exact bad-node gate B_i>2Inf_i never fires, so the localized boundary bill is zero. Each one-bit entropy drop is at most two bits, hence at most 4Inf_i. Quadratic sign leaves satisfy H<=2I by polar rank. For a point defect on d controls, p=2^(-d), H=-2(1-2p)^2 log2(1-2p)+8p(1-p)(d-1), I=2dp, and H<=4I: the first term is <=4p/ln2<8p; d=0,1 have zero entropy. Multiplication by the retained independent data sign adds one influence and no entropy. Exact restriction telescoping pays each pivot once and retains all terminal costs, proving H(F*)<=4(I(F*)-1). This constant is sharp on the q=0,M=0 point-defect subfamily as n grows.

For prefix/matching, with s=k/2 phase pivots, the exact spent influence is P=r/2+2^(-r)(1-2^(-s))+2s*delta. The final point-defect leaf has d=n-r-s controls and weight 2^(-(r+s)); its weighted phase influence is 2d*delta, and its full entropy is retained. Most initial order-n entropy lives in the other quadratic terminals. The small pivot budget is not claimed to pay those terminals for free.

First unsupported universal line: arbitrary Boolean phases need not admit such a high-influence pivot, nor a known globally paid boundary bill leading to certified leaves. The quadratic interaction graph and known flipped point provide it here. Multiple defects with incompatible phase gradients may remove that property. The M=0 arbitrary-phase case is still full FEI; no general entropy has been discarded.
