# A018: shared-data collision law and posterior cancellation

Run: 20261011T221538Z-fleet05-A018-shared-data-collision-law. Stream B04. Packet A018.
Artifact complete locally; elementary proofs and exact author checks complete; independent acceptance pending. Full FEI remains OPEN.

## Exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Let a,n be arbitrary nonnegative integers, W a fresh physical selector block of a uniform binary coordinates, and y an independent uniform n-bit data vector. For every b in F_2^a, let g_b(y) be an arbitrary Boolean function on the same data cube. No balance, orthogonality, support separation, or data disjointness is assumed. Set F(b,y)=g_b(y), N=2^a, c_T(b)=hat(g_b)(T), and

R_T=N^{-1} sum_b c_T(b)^2.

For R_T>0 define

r_T(b)=c_T(b)^2/(N R_T),
nu_T(A)=(N^{-1} sum_b chi_A(b)c_T(b))^2/R_T.

These are normalized probability laws. Zero-mass T are omitted, including an empty T only when its mass actually vanishes. All entropies are in bits; physical influence uses Pr[F(z) != F(z with one bit flipped)]. Write

B_W=sum_T R_T H(nu_T),
M_W=a-sum_T R_T H(r_T),
Delta_W H=H(F)-N^{-1}sum_b H(g_b),
I_W=sum_{i in W} Inf_i(F).

The packet interface is

**Delta_W H=B_W+M_W, with 0<=M_W<=(2/ln 2) I_W.**

B_W is exactly H(S_W | S_data) for the parent spectral set. M_W is the mutual information of the physical branch label and data Fourier label under the auxiliary law P(b,T)=c_T(b)^2/N. It is not a conditional-label term that can be spent a second time.

The cancellation classification proved here is: M_W=0 iff all branches have identical squared Fourier coefficients at every T; B_W=0 iff every nonzero coefficient row c_T is a scalar multiple of one Walsh character of b. Therefore Delta_W H=0 iff every nonzero row is such a character. Common squared spectra alone are sufficient for complete cancellation when a<=1, but are insufficient when a>=2.

## Complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Collision and influence ledger

The exact coefficient is hat(F)(A,T)=N^{-1}sum_b chi_A(b)c_T(b). Parseval on the selector cube gives sum_A hat(F)(A,T)^2=R_T. Thus H(F)=H(R)+sum_T R_T H(nu_T), and I_W=sum_T R_T sum_A |A| nu_T(A).

For P(b,T)=c_T(b)^2/N, the branch marginal is uniform, the data marginal is R, and the posterior branch law is r_T. The chain rule gives

H(R)=a+N^{-1}sum_b H(g_b)-sum_T R_T H(r_T).

Subtract the branch average from the parent entropy to get Delta_W H=B_W+M_W. Also I_data(F)=N^{-1}sum_b I(g_b), by conditioning physical edge differences on b. Consequently total physical influence is I(F)=I_W+average I(g_b).

### 2. Posterior payment, with an elementary proof of the constant

Use natural-log entropy Ent(h)=E[h ln(h/Eh)] on a uniform cube. For real numbers s,t,

Ent_{one bit}((s,t)^2) <= (s-t)^2/2.

To prove it, replacing s,t by their absolute values can only reduce the right side. Normalize (s^2+t^2)/2=1 and interchange them so s^2=1+u, t^2=1-u, 0<=u<=1. The left side is ((1+u)ln(1+u)+(1-u)ln(1-u))/2; the right side is 1-sqrt(1-u^2). Their difference vanishes at zero and has derivative u/sqrt(1-u^2)-artanh(u), which is nonnegative since its own derivative is (1-u^2)^(-3/2)-(1-u^2)^(-1)>=0. Extend to u=1 by continuity, and rescale. If both entries vanish both sides are zero.

For a nonnegative h on a product cube,

Ent(h) <= sum_i E_{outside i} Ent_i(h).

Here is a direct finite proof avoiding an unproved tensorization invocation. Normalize Eh=1 and put P(z)=2^{-a}h(z). Then Ent(h)=ln 2 (a-H(P)). The ith conditional contribution is ln 2 (1-H_P(Z_i|Z_{-i})). The chain rule and conditioning inequality give H(P)>=sum_i H_P(Z_i|Z_{-i}), proving the assertion. Zero conditional masses contribute zero. The general case follows by homogeneity.

For each nonzero T let phi_T(b)=c_T(b)/sqrt(R_T), so E phi_T^2=1. Then

a-H(r_T)=Ent(phi_T^2)/ln 2
<= (2/ln 2) sum_i E[(D_i phi_T)^2]
= (2/ln 2) sum_A |A| nu_T(A),

where D_i phi=(phi(b)-phi(b with bit i flipped))/2. The last equality is Walsh Parseval. Multiply by R_T and sum T. Nonnegativity follows either from H(r_T)<=a or from M_W=I_P(branch;T). This proves the full posterior bound for arbitrary overlapping Boolean branches. The row estimate itself permits real rows, but a universal estimate for B_W does not follow from it.

### 3. Exact cancellation and the role of signs

Because H(r_T)<=a and all positive weights R_T are retained, M_W=0 iff each r_T is uniform. That is iff c_T(b)^2 is independent of b for every T, equivalently all branch squared spectra coincide.

Because entropy vanishes exactly for a point mass, B_W=0 iff each nu_T has exactly one occupied A. Inverse Walsh then gives c_T(b)=alpha_T chi_{A_T}(b), alpha_T!=0. This in turn forces r_T uniform, so B_W=0 implies M_W=0. Since both terms are nonnegative, Delta_W H=0 iff B_W=0 iff all nonzero coefficient rows have this character form. There need not be any affine relation between A_T and T; Boolean realizability of the given branches is still part of the hypotheses.

Identical branches give c_T(b)=hat(g)(T), r_T uniform, nu_T concentrated at A=empty, I_W=0, and H(F)=H(g). For a=1 every sign pattern on two entries is a constant times one of the two Walsh characters, so common squared spectra suffice; this recovers the archived isospectral splice rather than claiming it as new.

For arbitrary a, common squared spectra allow writing c_T(b)=alpha_T h_T(b), with h_T Boolean on the selector cube. In that case

M_W=0, B_W=sum_T alpha_T^2 H(h_T), I_W=sum_T alpha_T^2 I(h_T),
H(F)=H(common squared data spectrum)+B_W.

Thus the posterior label term cancels the entire literal a-bit alphabet cost, but the sign phases may still produce spectral entropy. Relative to the incoherent reference H(R)+a, coherence changes entropy by B_W-a<=0; relative to the branch-average baseline it adds B_W+M_W>=0. Statements about coherence 'adding entropy' must specify which baseline is being compared.

The actual Boolean example a=2, g_b(y)=(-1)^{b_1 b_2}y has identical branch squared spectra, M_W=0, and a single data row whose Walsh spectrum is uniform on all four A. Hence B_W=Delta_W H=2, I_W=1, I_data=1. All branch entropies are zero. For general a and any Boolean h, branches g_b(y)=h(b)y give H(F)=H(h), I_W=I(h), I_data=1. A uniform B_W<=K I_W on all common-spectrum branches would already imply full FEI for arbitrary h. This is the source-credited X005 guard, not a new counterexample to FEI.

### 4. Required bent-splice obstruction

Let m>=2 be even, delta=2^{-m}, and b(y)=(-1)^{y_1y_2+...+y_{m-1}y_m}. Its coefficient c_T has magnitude sqrt(delta); this follows by taking the product of the exact two-bit transform. Set b'(y)=b(y)-2 1_{y=0}. Both are Boolean, and the one-bit selector F between them has I_W=delta.

In every data fiber the two parent coefficients are c_T-delta and delta, so e_T=delta^2 and R_T=(c_T-delta)^2+delta^2. Write u=sqrt(delta)<=1/2. Then

R_T/e_T >= delta^{-1}(1-2u+2u^2) >= 1/(2delta).

Binary entropy obeys R_T h_2(e_T/R_T)>=e_T log_2(R_T/e_T). Summing all 2^m fibers gives

B_W >= delta(m-1), and Delta_W H/I_W >= B_W/I_W >= m-1.

The inference is valid whether or not e_T is the smaller mass. M_W still has the proved constant posterior payment; it cannot remove the nonnegative rare logarithm in B_W. Therefore no finite universal K satisfies either B_W<=K I_W or Delta_W H<=K I_W for all Boolean fresh-selector splices. This is a reconstruction of the archived guard; it does not refute total-influence FEI, good-block selection, or a paid stopping tree.

### 5. Exact open line and the universal interface

For any proper block W, a proposed next step needs to pay B_W=H(S_W|S_{-W}) using a nonreused physical budget or select W so that this payment is possible. The statement 'there exists a usable proper block or physical stopping tree with cumulative B_W cost <=K I(F)' is unproved here. Simply charging each node's B_W to that node's I_W is refuted by Section 4.

For a finite physical stopping tree whose internal nodes query disjoint-from-their-own-ancestor blocks, the entropy chain rule telescopes exactly:

H(root)=sum_internal pi_v(B_{W_v}+M_{W_v})+sum_leaves pi_leaf H(leaf).

Physical influence decomposes exactly as I(root)=sum_internal pi_v I_{W_v}(node)+sum_leaves pi_leaf I(leaf). Thus all posterior terms together cost at most (2/ln 2) times the selector portion of that decomposition. If a separate certificate pays sum_internal pi_v B_{W_v}<=K_B sum_internal pi_v I_{W_v} and all leaves have H<=C_leaf I, then H(root)<=max(K_B+2/ln 2,C_leaf)I(root). That certificate is an additional hypothesis, not a universal result. Overlapping numerical influence banks cannot be used twice. Zero-cost or common-spectrum nodes do not certify arbitrary leaves.

## Exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For arbitrary fresh uniform a-bit selectors x, independent uniform data y, and Boolean branches g_x(y) on one common cube, put F(x,y)=g_x(y), s=I_sel(F), and Delta=H(F)-average_x H(g_x). All H and h_2 use bits. With the normalized stage-1 row laws, Delta=M_W+B_W and B_W=H(S_W|S_data). The first target is the universal inequality

Delta <= min(a,(2/ln2)s)+a h_2(s/a) <= s log_2(e^3 a/s).

At a=0 both sides mean zero. At s=0 the right side is its continuous zero extension, avoiding an undefined 0 log 0 expression.

The second target is the even-m bent one-point splice, s=delta=2^{-m}, u=sqrt(delta):

Delta=delta(m+3/ln2)-(19/(6ln2))delta^2+O(delta^3),
[(2/ln2)delta+h_2(delta)]-Delta=(8/(3ln2))delta^2+O(delta^3).

Disjoint tensor powers scale Delta and selector influence additively. Sharpness means the gap is o(s) along these actual Boolean families; it does not assert an exact pointwise supremum for every pair (s,a).

The third target concerns every Boolean f on n>=1 uniform coordinates and every physical coordinate i. Write g=f|_{x_i=0}, h=f|_{x_i=1}, D=(g-h)/2, d=Inf_i(f). For this trinary derivative use the spectral energy

I(D)=sum_T |T| hat(D)(T)^2 = sum_{S contains i} (|S|-1) hat(f)(S)^2.

Then Delta_i H <= (3/ln2)d+2I(D). The derivative's I is an L2 Dirichlet energy, not the edge-disagreement influence of a trinary-valued function.

## Sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

## Complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Selector logarithmic envelope: HOLDS

Let Z_j indicate selector coordinate j in the parent spectral sample. By the entropy chain rule and conditioning,

B_W=H(S_W|S_data) <= H(S_W) <= sum_{j in W} h_2(P(Z_j=1)).

Walsh Parseval gives P(Z_j=1)=Inf_j(F). Concavity of h_2 therefore gives B_W<=a h_2(s/a). This argument uses neither orthogonality of branches nor a disjoint-support mixture. Stage 1's posterior bound and the alphabet bound give M_W<=min(a,(2/ln2)s); add them.

For 0<t<=1, -(1-t)ln(1-t)<=t, hence h_2(t)<=t log_2(e/t). Thus

min(a,(2/ln2)s)+a h_2(s/a)
<= (2/ln2)s+s log_2(ea/s)
=s log_2(e^3 a/s).

At s=0 every queried influence is zero and F is independent of the selector, so Delta=B=M=0. The a=0 case is also exact. All units and constants agree with B04-SEL-LOG-SHARP@1.

### 2. Bent-splice formula and sharpness: HOLDS

Take b(y)=(-1)^(y_1y_2+...+y_{m-1}y_m) on even m>=2 data bits, and b'=b-2*1_{y=0}. The exact two-bit transform tensorizes: hat(b)(T)=e_T u, e_T in {+1,-1}, u=2^{-m/2}. Its signs have multiplicities

N_e=(2^m+e 2^{m/2})/2, equivalently N_e u^2=w_e=(1+eu)/2.

Parseval gives the uniform squared spectrum of b, H(b)=m. For the parent splice the two coefficients at data set T are e_T u-u^2 and u^2. For b' the coefficient is e_T u-2u^2. The selector differs only at the flipped data vertex, so s=u^2 exactly. Summing these signed coefficient histograms before squaring gives

Delta=m u^2-2 sum_e w_e(1-eu)^2 log_2(1-eu)
          +sum_e w_e(1-2eu)^2 log_2|1-2eu|.

At m=2, e=+1, the latter term is zero by z^2 log|z| -> 0 as z -> 0; do not evaluate log(0) numerically. This is the standard spectral-entropy zero convention. The parent coefficient calculation is independent of the lead's simplification and agrees with its stated A+B formula.

For an analytic remainder proof work in natural units. Define L(z)=(1-z)^2 ln(1-z). On |z|<1 its coefficients are

c_1=-1, c_2=3/2, c_k=-2/[k(k-1)(k-2)] for k>=3.

This follows by multiplying the absolutely convergent series ln(1-z)=-sum_{k>=1} z^k/k by 1-2z+z^2. The weighted sum sum_e ((1+eu)/2)L(lambda e u) contains only even powers. Its u^(2j) coefficient is c_(2j) lambda^(2j)+c_(2j-1) lambda^(2j-1). Combining lambda=1 with multiplier -2 and lambda=2 with multiplier +1 gives

Delta ln2 = m delta ln2+3delta-(19/6)delta^2-(61/30)delta^3+O(delta^4).

In particular the first two claimed terms hold. For a direct rigorous remainder through order delta^2, m>=4 implies delta<=1/16 and |2u|<=1/2. For j>=3 the absolute combined coefficient is at most 2*4^j (use |c_k|<=1 for k>=3). Consequently

|Delta ln2 -m delta ln2-3delta+(19/6)delta^2|
<=2 sum_{j>=3}(4delta)^j
<= (512/3)delta^3.

This proves an explicit uniform O(delta^3) error rather than a numerical series fit. Also

h_2(delta)ln2=delta ln(1/delta)+delta-delta^2/2+R,
|R|<=delta^3/[6(1-delta)].

Subtracting yields the claimed (8/3)delta^2 natural-unit sharpness gap, with a uniform O(delta^3) error. Its sign is strictly positive for all sufficiently large even m; the finite endpoint signs checked here are separately certified. Since the gap/delta tends to zero, for every epsilon>0 a large even m violates an envelope lowered everywhere by epsilon*s. Tensor products on disjoint (selector,data) blocks satisfy Delta_t=t Delta_1 and s_t=t delta; the a=t envelope is also t times the one-bit envelope. No pointwise optimality away from this sequence is claimed.

### 3. Derivative-boundary payment: HOLDS

The one-bit selector envelope gives

Delta_i H <= (2/ln2)d+h_2(d)
<= (3/ln2)d+d log_2(1/d).

Let A=supp(D)={y:g(y)!=h(y)}, so E[1_A]=d and D takes only 0,+1,-1. I(D)>=I(1_A): on an edge with just one active endpoint the derivative squared differences are identical; on an edge with two active endpoints the indicator has zero difference whereas D has nonnegative squared difference.

The sharp support-isoperimetric factor follows from the same elementary product-entropy tensorization used in stage 1, applied to the indicator itself. On each one-bit fiber with one active endpoint,

Ent_i(1_A)=(ln2)/2=2 ln2 E_i[(D_i 1_A)^2],

and on constant fibers both sides are zero. Tensorize and sum to obtain

d ln(1/d)=Ent(1_A)<=2 ln2 I(1_A)<=2 ln2 I(D).

Therefore d log_2(1/d)<=2I(D), giving exactly

Delta_i H <= (3/ln2)d+2I(D).

This reconstructs the edge-isoperimetry step directly, with no outside Harper theorem dependency. Walsh expansion of f=(g+h)/2+(-1)^{x_i}(g-h)/2 shows hat(D)(T)=hat(f)(T union {i}) and proves the claimed spectral expression for I(D). At d=0, D=0 and Delta_i=0. At d=1, the support logarithm vanishes; all steps remain valid.

For the bent splice D is one signed point indicator on m bits, so I(D)=m delta/2. Its node bound is delta(m+3/ln2). Section 2 gives Delta/node-bound ->1, verifying first-order sharpness. The bound's derivative term cannot be discarded even though it is small in total mass.

### 4. What the review does not prove: first unsupported line

The lead's open statement remains unproved: there exists a terminal-certified adaptive physical stopping tree for every Boolean f in which

sum_{v: exact B_v>K d_v} pi_v * 2 I(D_v) <= K' I(f)

for fixed dimension-independent K,K', without spending a descendant/terminal influence reserve twice. All three accepted claims are local; they do not supply this selection and cumulative payment theorem. Replacing exact B_v by h_2(d_v) is invalid for the proposed localization: odd-function roots, including majority, can have exact B_v=0 even when the binary upper bound is positive. A fixed query order can charge the second spectral moment, which is not an absolute multiple of the first moment. This remains an honest C019 gap, rather than a consequence of the newly reviewed node lemma.

The board's A016 tensor-spectator overcharge and A017 point-surgery tests are relevant safeguards. Next work should remove exact spectators before boundary pricing and retain the actual terminal entropy; merely paying every node's full I(D_v) would repeat the known excess.

# A018 stage 3: shared-separator collision, data dependence and owned influence

Run 20261013T222945Z-fleet05-A018-shared-data-collision-law, B04/A018. Full FEI remains OPEN. A016's connected-spine control independently HOLDS at its stated scope. A new exact paired-row/reference interface is proved below; an uncorrected entropy-subadditivity strengthening remains open outside the explicitly certified finite domain.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Let r>=1, let z be one uniform physical bit, and let y_1,...,y_r be mutually disjoint uniform private input blocks, independent of z. Let f_j(z,y_j)=g_{j,z}(y_j) be arbitrary Boolean signs; no balance, correlation or spectral separation is assumed. Set F(z,y)=product_j f_j(z,y_j). The same physical z occurs in every factor. All entropies, cross-entropies CE and KL divergences use bits. I is Boolean physical influence; a trinary derivative's I is its squared-Fourier/Dirichlet energy.

For module j, write P_{j,b}(T)=hat(g_{j,b})(T)^2, R_j=(P_{j,0}+P_{j,1})/2, B_j=H(S_z^{(j)}|S_{y_j}^{(j)}), and M_j=H(R_j)-(H(g_{j,0})+H(g_{j,1}))/2. For the actual shared product set

R(T_1,...,T_r)=[product_j P_{j,0}(T_j)+product_j P_{j,1}(T_j)]/2,
B_*=H(S_z^F|S_y^F), M_*=H(R)-average_b sum_j H(g_{j,b}),
TC=KL(R || product_j R_j)=sum_j H(R_j)-H(R).

The universal exact identities proved here are

H(F)-sum_j H(f_j)=B_*-sum_j B_j-TC,
M_*=sum_j M_j-TC,
0<=TC<=sum_j M_j, and H(F)<=sum_j H(f_j)+1.

For finite-cost normalized complete module references Q_j(A,T), let q_j(T)=sum_A Q_j(A,T), tau_j(A|T)=Q_j(A,T)/q_j(T), and k_j=E_{R_j} KL(nu_j(.|T)||tau_j(.|T)), where nu_j is the actual module spectral conditional selector law. Define the shared reference Q_*(A,T)=nu_*(A|T) product_j q_j(T_j), with an arbitrary fixed conditional law on zero true rows. Then

CE_*(F)=sum_j CE_{Q_j}(f_j)+B_*-sum_j B_j-sum_j k_j,
CE_*(F)-H(F)=TC+sum_j KL(R_j||q_j).

This is a complete reference accounting identity, not a free use of unknown entropy: B_* is explicitly retained and every marginal/conditional reference law is fixed for the function. Finite CE hypotheses avoid undefined infinity subtraction. Uniform or exact spectral references used in tests are fixtures, not supplied universal FEI certificates.

The physical owner correction is also exact. With d_j=Inf_z(f_j) and d_*=Inf_z(F),

d_*=[1-product_j(1-2d_j)]/2,
I(F)=sum_j average_b I(g_{j,b})+d_*,
sum_j I(f_j)-I(F)=sum_j d_j-d_*=:L>=0.

Thus module budgets count the same physical z repeatedly; a valid transfer must retain L or a proved exclusive reserve. No universal payment for L, B_* or TC is asserted here.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Independent A016 reconstruction: HOLDS at stated scope

A016's family uses one common bit z, private selectors x_j and m-1 private data bits t_j, a>=1, even m>=4:

c_j=(-1)^(t_{j,2}t_{j,3}+...+t_{j,m-2}t_{j,m-1}),
f_j=(-1)^(z t_{j,1})c_j-2x_j 1{z=0,t_j=0}, F=product_j f_j.

Let delta=2^{-m}. At z=0 the private module is psi=c_j-2x_j 1{t_j=0}; at z=1 it is the character (-1)^(t_{j,1})c_j. Direct edge differences give

J_0=(m-2)/2+2(m-1)delta,
L_0=J_0+2delta=(m-2)/2+2m delta,
Inf_z(F)=[1-(-2delta)^a]/2,
I(F)=a[(m-1)/2+m delta]+Inf_z(F).

For example, fixing x=1 flips one point of the m-1-bit private c_j extension, creating an extra 4delta influence in each data direction; fair averaging of x gives J_0. The common-z branch ratio has independent factors of mean -2delta, giving the displayed d_z. These normalizers were independently verified physically.

The unique multilinear ANF exponent is a sum of each module's z t_1, its private quadratic pairs, and x_j(1+z) product_l(1+t_{j,l}). At the root its uncancelled highest monomial connects all coordinates to z. After a selector prefix, each earlier x=1 branch still has a monomial z product_l t_l of degree m>=4, so its data remain connected to z. Each earlier x=0 branch detaches exactly its m-2 private quadratic coordinates into independent pairs, retaining z t_1. All remaining selectors still connect their entire private block to z. ANF uniqueness proves that these graph components are the finest possible partition into independent sign factors. The m=2 cancellation endpoint remains excluded exactly as in A016.

At the next queried selector, D is its m-coordinate signed point indicator times the other modules' z=0 values. Its norm is delta. The point indicator contributes m delta/2 energy; each other retained private block contributes delta times its z=0 energy. Averaging all prior selector assignments gives the raw bill

T_raw=a m delta+delta a(a-1)[m-2+(4m-2)delta].

Only the earlier x=0 quadratic pairs can be removed. At prefix depth j their expected removed energy is j(m-2)/4, multiplied by delta in D. Summing 2I(D) gives exactly

T_factor=a m delta+delta a(a-1)[3(m-2)/4+(4m-2)delta].

The exact-B filter survives: in queried data rows with its t_1 Fourier bit absent, the z=1 coefficient is zero. The two parent selector-sector coefficients are (eu-delta)hat(H_0)(R) and delta hat(H_0)(R), u=sqrt(delta). Their row mass divided by delta^2 hat(H_0)^2 is at least 1/(2delta). There are two Fourier z choices and 2^{m-2} private quadratic indices. Parseval of the actual Boolean H_0 yields

B_v >=delta(m-1)/2, Inf_v=delta

at every selector-first descendant. For any fixed K, choosing even m with (m-1)/2>K makes all those nodes bad. T_factor is quadratic in a whereas I(F) is linear: factor removal cannot repair that fixed rule. This is not a counterexample to universal adaptive selection.

Querying z first instead has d_z>=3/8 and B_z<=1<4d_z. Both leaves are explicitly paid: I_0=aL_0, I_1=am/2; uniform private masks cost am<=4I_0 and the character-flat leaf costs a(m-2)<=2I_1. Thus the chosen tree has zero internal K4 bad-node bill without treating leaves as free.

The stronger complete reference uses Q0=2^{-am}, Q1=2^{-a(m-2)} on the exact character-flat support, and Q(A,R)=(Q0(R)+Q1(R))/4. Summing both A gives normalization one. That support's true parent data mass is alpha=(1+delta^a)/2 because the other leaf puts mass delta^a there. Consequently

CE_Q=am+2-alpha log2(1+4^a)<=a(m-1)+2.

With X=a(m-1)>=3, I(F)>=X/2+3/8 and X+2<=(8/3)(X/2+3/8). Thus CE_Q<=(8/3)I(F), including root, posterior and terminal costs. This is a full independent reconstruction of A016's critical claim, supplemented by distinct ANF/derivative/reference code. No mathematical repair to that version is supplied.

### 2. Exact shared-label data law

For each private Fourier set T_j let a_j=hat(g_{j,0})(T_j), b_j=hat(g_{j,1})(T_j). The two parent coefficients are

hat(F)(0,T)=[product_j a_j+product_j b_j]/2,
hat(F)(1,T)=[product_j a_j-product_j b_j]/2.

Their squared sum is R(T) as stated above. Summing over other T coordinates shows its jth marginal is R_j, using Parseval for every branch. Thus H(F)=H(R)+B_* and H(f_j)=H(R_j)+B_j. Subtraction immediately proves

H(F)-sum_j H(f_j)=B_*-sum_j B_j-TC.

All signs are retained in the coefficients before squaring. For one selector bit, exchanging the signs of one row changes which sector is occupied but not its conditional entropy. Zero rows are omitted from conditional entropies and may carry arbitrary fixed conditionals in references.

The physical branch label b is uniform in the auxiliary law P(b,T)=product_j P_{j,b}(T_j)/2. Given b the labels T_j are independent. Its mutual information with T is M_*, and its mutual information with T_j is M_j. The entropy chain rules give M_*=sum_j M_j-TC. Nonnegativity of KL and mutual information gives TC>=0 and TC<=sum_j M_j. Since B_*<=1, B_j>=0 and TC>=0, the universal corrected bound H(F)<=sum_j H(f_j)+1 follows. No novelty claim is made for this elementary shared-bit overlap bound; the important delivered object is its exact residual.

### 3. Complete reference transfer with the missing debt visible

For each finite-cost normalized Q_j, the usual conditional decomposition is

CE_{Q_j}(f_j)=E_{R_j} log2(1/q_j(T_j))+B_j+k_j,
D(P_{f_j}||Q_j)=D(R_j||q_j)+k_j.

Fix nu_*(A|T) to the actual parent conditional law on positive true rows and choose a fixed arbitrary distribution on zero true rows. Then Q_*(A,T)=nu_*(A|T) product_j q_j(T_j) has total mass one. This is the function-specific compatible lift, not a sampled-row-dependent choice of a different reference. Its expected complete cost is

CE_*(F)=B_*+sum_j E_{R_j} log2(1/q_j(T_j))
=sum_j CE_{Q_j}(f_j)+B_*-sum_j B_j-sum_j k_j.

Subtract H(F)=sum H(f_j)+B_*-sum B_j-TC and use the module debt decomposition:

CE_*-H(F)=TC+sum_j D(R_j||q_j).

Equivalently the joint data mismatch to product_j q_j equals TC plus the marginal divergences. The conditional module debts k_j are erased only by explicitly matching the new conditional selector reference; they cannot simultaneously be spent as terminal reserves elsewhere. Even with exact module marginals q_j=R_j, the product-data lift leaves debt TC. This is the precise distinction between zero marginal error and a paid joint shared-separator description.

### 4. Exclusive physical owner correction

Let E_j(y_j)=1{g_{j,0}(y_j)!=g_{j,1}(y_j)}. The shared-z output changes iff sum_j E_j is odd. These E_j are independent on their private blocks, with means d_j. Hence d_*=[1-product(1-2d_j)]/2. Conditioning on z gives every private influence as the corresponding average module influence. Therefore I(F)=sum private averages+d_*, whereas sum I(f_j) uses sum d_j for that same coordinate.

L=sum d_j-d_*=E[sum E_j-(sum E_j mod2)]=2E[floor((sum E_j)/2)]>=0.

It is not legitimate to combine module-level C I(f_j) certificates and silently replace sum I(f_j) by I(F). Likewise TC<=sum M_j<=(2/ln2)sum d_j is a bound in a repeated-module selector budget, not in the single physical d_* budget. Private inherited CE and any paid terminal deficits must be retained explicitly. This is where a source-owned influence reserve remains necessary even if the entropy-subadditivity proposal turns out to be true.

### 5. First unsupported line, isolated as a paired-row inequality

The bounded tests prove H(F)<=H(f_1)+H(f_2) for all ordered modules with up to two private bits each. No all-dimensional proof is supplied for

B_* <= B_1+B_2+TC.

This is exactly the missing line in the uncorrected entropy-subadditivity proposal; no counterexample was found in the frozen domain. It can be reformulated for posterior biases U_j=(P_{j,0}-P_{j,1})/(P_{j,0}+P_{j,1}), sampled independently under R_1,R_2, with E U_j=0. Write e(u)=h2((1+sqrt(1-u^2))/2). Then the target is

E[(1+U_1 U_2)e((U_1+U_2)/(1+U_1 U_2))]
<=E e(U_1)+E e(U_2)+E[(1+U_1 U_2)log2(1+U_1 U_2)].

Zero-weight rows 1+U_1 U_2=0 contribute zero. The scalar reformulation itself is proved by the exact product row law. A proof for every zero-mean bias law would suffice; failure for an arbitrary real bias law would still require a Boolean realization before refuting the Boolean target. No non-Boolean relaxation is used to claim FEI progress.

Even proving this optional inequality would not pay the universal C019 tree: the one physical influence loss L and complete terminal costs remain. The next useful step is a signed paired-row bound inserted against an exclusive shared-coordinate reserve, not another assertion that spectral modules are independent.

# A018 stage 4: actual Boolean failure of shared-row subadditivity

Run 20261014T022025Z-fleet05-A018-shared-data-collision-law, B04/A018. Full FEI remains OPEN. The optional paired-row entropy-subadditivity line from stage 3 is FALSE, even for two Boolean point-defect modules sharing one bit. The previous exact TC/reference interface remains valid. The new obstruction has a pure-integer certificate and an all-dimensional rare-event extension; its complete reference is nevertheless paid at rate4 from a single allocation of the module terminal slacks. A016's new two-weak-separator theorem independently HOLDS at its stated scope.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

All physical bits are independent fair bits. For two mutually disjoint private blocks y_1,y_2 of sizes n_1,n_2, and one common physical bit z, define f_j(z,y_j)=-1 exactly when z=0 and y_j=0, and +1 otherwise. Let F=f_1 f_2 and G=H(F)-H(f_1)-H(f_2). Entropies, KL and complete reference CE are in bits; G_nat=ln(2)G uses natural units. Physical influence is edge disagreement probability. No balance or orthogonality assumption is made.

We refute H(F)<=H(f_1)+H(f_2), equivalently the optional B_*<=B_1+B_2+TC in the previous report. We also refute any dimension-free G<=K L on these actual Boolean modules, where L=I(f_1)+I(f_2)-I(F) is the lost shared physical influence. This is not a refutation of the exact identity H(F)-sum H(f_j)=B_*-sum B_j-TC, of A019's ownership theorem, or of FEI.

For equal private sizes m and delta=2^-m, we prove

G_nat=delta^2[(4m-24)ln2+6]+R,
|R|<=delta^3[(16m+48)ln2+1000], for all m>=4.

Consequently G>0 for every m>=12 and

G/L=2m+3/ln2-12+O(m 2^-m) -> infinity.

Despite this loss-price obstruction, H(F)<=CE_Q(F)<=4I(F) for every m>=12, for the explicitly normalized conditional-product reference Q described below. Every module terminal cost and the joint-data TC bill are retained; the two module slacks are allocated once.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Boolean realization and physical ownership

Write a=2^-n_1 and b=2^-n_2. At z=0 each module is a private one-point defect h_j; at z=1 it is constant1. Its branch spectra are h_jhat(empty)=1-2a_j and h_jhat(T)=-2a_j for all nonempty T. The module itself is a one-point defect on n_j+1 bits, with empty coefficient1-a_j and every nonempty coefficient -a_j.

Every private coordinate of f_j has influence a_j. Private influence is unchanged by multiplying the other sign module. The shared product changes on exactly one of the two private-origin events, so

Inf_z(F)=a+b-2ab,
I(F)=(n_1+1)a+(n_2+1)b-2ab,
L=2ab.

These are physical edge identities, independently checked against the actual Walsh spectrum.

The posterior-bias realization is especially simple. For equal m, let eta=(1-(1-2delta)^2)/2=2delta(1-delta). Under the module's data marginal, U=1 on a nonempty private Fourier set (total probability eta), and U=-eta/(1-eta) on the empty set (probability1-eta). Therefore E U=0 exactly. At m=5 this is U=1 with probability31/512 and U=-31/481 with probability481/512. Thus failure of the averaged scalar proposal is realized by Boolean functions, not just a normalized real relaxation.

### 2. Eleven-bit certificate with no logarithm approximation

Take n_1=n_2=5. Each module has 64 truth values, negative only at its all-zero physical point, and Fourier integer transform histogram {62:1,-2:63}. The shared parent has 2048 actual truth values and integer transform histogram

{1924:1,-124:1,-60:124,4:1922}.

The parent normalization is 1924^2+124^2+124*60^2+1922*4^2=2048^2. Expanding the entropy gives

G_nat=[491071 ln31-231361 ln481-27900 ln15-262144 ln2]/131072.

The exact integer comparison

31^491071 > 481^231361 * 15^27900 * 2^262144

proves G>0. code/verify_integer_witness.py executes it directly, writes the two integer byte hashes and bit lengths, and independently checks the displayed Parseval count and 961*511=491071. Actual truth tables, a recursive Walsh implementation, a separate iterative Walsh implementation, physical edges and every branch-product coefficient are checked by code/check_boolean.py. Thus the certificate is source-complete and does not depend on a floating entropy sign. The first discovered Boolean witness was the unequal (4,6) fixture, also eleven-dimensional; the equal (5,5) witness provides the simpler integer certificate.

### 3. Exact all-m spectrum and collision asymptotic

Now set both private sizes to m and N=1/delta. Shared parent coefficients have the following four classes, with their multiplicities:

empty: c_0=1-2delta+2delta^2, once;
shared-only: -2delta(1-delta), once;
exactly one private block nonempty: -delta(1-2delta), 4(N-1) times;
both private blocks nonempty: 2delta^2, 2(N-1)^2 times.

These follow by taking the half-sum/half-difference of the private branch-product spectrum and the constant branch spectrum. All multiplicities sum to2N^2, and the squared masses sum to1. Each module has empty squared mass (1-delta)^2 and2N-1 other squared masses delta^2.

Let x=delta. Collecting logarithms in H(parent)-2H(module) gives the exact natural-unit expression

G_nat=(-4x^2+16x^3-8x^4)ln x
       +(-24x^2+48x^3-24x^4)ln2+A(x),

A(x)=-2(1-2x+2x^2)^2 ln(1-2x+2x^2)
     +[4(1-x)^2-8x^2(1-x)^2]ln(1-x)
     -8x(1-x)(1-2x)^2 ln(1-2x).

For 0<x<=1/16 we show |A(x)-6x^2|<=1000x^3. The bound is deliberately loose and fully explicit. For 0<=t<1,

|ln(1-t)+t+t^2/2|<=t^3/[3(1-t)].

In the first term put t=2x-2x^2<=1/8. The log remainder is <=4x^3; multiplication by2(1-t)^2 contributes <=8x^3. The polynomial replacing that term has coefficients

[0,4,-16,24,4,-64,96,-64,16].

Beyond degree2 their absolute sum is268, giving a total residual <=276x^3. In the second term the polynomial multiplier is [4,-8,-4,16,-8]; the log remainder at t=x is <=x^3. Its absolute coefficient sum is40. Multiplying its quadratic log approximation gives coefficients [0,-4,6,8,-14,0,4], whose degree>=3 absolute sum is26. This contributes <=66x^3. In the third term the multiplier is [0,-8,40,-64,32]. The log remainder at t=2x is <=4x^3. The multiplier's absolute coefficient sum is <=144x, so its error is <=576x^4<=36x^3. Its quadratic log approximation has coefficients [0,0,16,-64,48,64,-64]; the degree>=3 absolute sum is240. This contributes <=276x^3. The sum618x^3 is below1000x^3; the degree<=2 terms sum to6x^2. These polynomial products and coefficient sums are independently checked with exact rationals.

The remaining logarithmic residuals are bounded by16x^3 ln(1/x) and48x^3 ln2, proving the announced R bound. For m>=12, ln2 lies in (2/3,1), so

G_nat/delta^2 >= 2m-6-2^-m(16m+1048) > 2m-7 >0.

The quantity2^-m(16m+1048) decreases with integer m>=12 and at12 equals1240/4096<1. This proves positivity for every m>=12 without large truth tables or precision extrapolation. Dividing by L=2delta^2 proves G/L=2m+3/ln2-12+O(m2^-m), hence no finite loss-only price is possible. The entropy-to-total-influence ratio stays bounded; this family is not an FEI counterexample.

### 4. Complete conditional-product reference and one-use slack payment

For each module use its explicitly known squared spectrum as a finite normalized reference Q_j; its CE equals H(f_j). Let R_j be its data marginal and nu_* the actual parent selector conditional. Define

Q_*(A,T_1,T_2)=nu_*(A|T_1,T_2) R_1(T_1)R_2(T_2).

All private rows have positive true mass in this family, so no unsupported atom is excluded. Summing conditionals gives normalization1. The exact stage3 identity, or direct entropy expansion, yields CE_*=H(F)+TC. This is an explicit reference for a specified family; it supplies no oracle reference for arbitrary Boolean terminals.

Within each private block the nonempty data Fourier label is uniform, in both the module marginal and the shared joint law. Dependence therefore reduces exactly to the binary markers X_j=1{T_j nonempty}. Their marginal probability is eta=2delta(1-delta), and their joint law satisfies P(X_1=X_2=1)=2eta^2. The joint Bernoulli table has covariance eta^2, so its chi-square divergence from the product marginals is eta^2/(1-eta)^2. Using ln t<=t-1 gives

TC_bits<=eta^2/[(1-eta)^2 ln2]
        <=4delta^2/[(1-2delta)^2 ln2]<8delta^2,

since delta<=1/16, ln2>2/3, and6*(64/49)<8. This retains, rather than discards, the joint-data reference debt.

For m>=12 the proven remainder gives G<=4m delta^2: the normalized residual is <1 in natural units, and (7/ln2)-24<0. Also

H(f_j)=-(1-delta)^2 log2((1-delta)^2)+(2delta-delta^2)2m
      <=4m delta+(2/ln2)delta<4m delta+3delta.

The empty-sector estimate uses -ln(1-delta)<=delta/(1-delta). Thus each module's rate4 slack S_j=4I(f_j)-H(f_j) is at least delta. For the compatible parent reference the exact required payment is

S_1+S_2 >= 4L+G+TC.

The right side is <=(4m+16)delta^2, while the left is >=2delta. At m=12, (4m+16)delta=64/4096<2, and this expression decreases for integer m>=12. Hence CE_*<=4I(F), with each module slack used once. This includes both terminal costs, the lost physical influence and the complete TC debt. The failure of G<=K L does not imply failure of this stronger signed-slack insertion.

### 5. Independent review of A016's two weak separators

A016-TWO-WEAK-SEPARATORS-OWNED-PAYMENT@1 HOLDS at its stated scope; no substantive repair is supplied. Reconstruct its arbitrary-mask modules c_j-2A_j(z,w)1{t_j=0} directly. For each physical shared edge, a changed module derivative is a signed point indicator with kappa_j=m delta/2; unchanged modules have kappa_j=0. Its edge-average private energy is J_j,edge=m/2+m delta(A_j(b)+A_j(b')). With Q_-j=(1-2delta) to the number of other changed masks, product expansion gives

t_j,edge=(1-Q_-j)J_j,edge/2+Q_-j kappa_j<=J_j,edge/2.

Averaging the two edges in each of two directions defines one account u_j<=J_j; it does not assign a new J_j for each direction. Parseval identifies T_private=2sum u_j and the extra root mixed energy2E_zw. Since2E_zw<=d, T<=2J+d<=2I. Filtering exact bad nodes decreases this nonnegative bill.

Retain terminal uniform code am. Allocating4J_j to module j pays m+2u_j and leaves at least2J_j-m=4m delta p_j. The reviewed one-node constant c=3/ln2<6 adds c d; the mixed term adds at most d. After shared allocation4d the remaining need is <3d<=12delta sum p_j, whereas the unused private reserve is >=16delta sum p_j for m>=4. This proves the full rate4 ledger without spending a private block twice. The explicit conditional shared reference with uniform private marginal costs am+B_block<=am+2<=3I; entropy concavity also bounds it by the same terminal-inclusive ledger.

For unequal AND/OR masks, expanding their four Walsh coefficients gives exactly the source's displayed four sign classes, E_zw=delta(1+delta)/2 and T=(4m+1)delta(1+delta). The distinct highest ANF monomials connect both private blocks to z,w and cannot cancel. Equal private-sign rows have root weight ratio >=1/(2delta) and total smaller-sector mass delta(1-delta)^2/2, proving B_root>=delta(1-delta)^2(m-1)/2. Each child is a private point splice with B_child>=delta(m-1). At m=12 both exceed4delta; the lower factors increase thereafter. Finally T/I->0 and the normalized reference squeeze2m<=CE<=2m+2 proves CE/I->2. These all-parameter reconstructions supplement the independent finite checks; they do not infer the theorem from fixtures.

### 6. Changed gap and exact first unsupported line

The optional stage3 averaged scalar line and Boolean shared-bit entropy subadditivity are now refuted. No proved A018/A019 dependency is refuted. The current exact interface survives and explains the sign of the excess. A universal G<=K L repair is also impossible.

For general supplied finite complete module references Q_j, define S_j(K)=K I(f_j)-CE_j and k_j=E_Rj KL(nu_j||tau_j). The exact parent lift obeys

CE_*-K I(F)=K L+B_*-sum B_j-sum k_j-sum S_j(K).

The first unsupported universal line is existence of an admissible module/pivot policy whose SAME owned slacks satisfy sum(S_j+k_j)>=K L+B_*-sum B_j, while retaining arbitrary terminal CE and avoiding repeated use of any private/core reserve. Neither a loss-only bound nor the false entropy-subadditivity line supplies it. NEXT targets this signed insertion with a non-flat arbitrary certified terminal core, using the eleven-bit point product as a mandatory positive-excess control and A016's weak-and-bad two-pivot family as the paid control. # A018 stage5: one physical branch label and arbitrary certified terminal cores

Run 20261014T051305Z-fleet05-A018-shared-data-collision-law, B04/A018. Full FEI remains OPEN. A joint physical-branch reference closes the constant-terminal shared fanout with arbitrary supplied non-flat core references, preserving any core rate at least the existing fine profile constant Gamma in(4.872,4.873). The scalar optimum belongs to A060; it is independently reconstructed here and not claimed as a new class or constant. The substantive reference result is a single-use physical budget and exact empty-row KL refund. Replacing a product prior by the joint prior is not a universally nonnegative credit; an eligible six-bit counterexample is certified. A019's internal-pivotal edit theorem independently HOLDS at full stated scope.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

All physical bits are independent fair bits, signs are Boolean, and H, CE and KL use bits. For any r>=1 and arbitrary Boolean private cores g_j(y_j) on mutually disjoint private cubes, define G=product_j g_j and F(z,y)=G(y) at z0, constant1 at z1, with one common fresh physical bit z. Constants and zero true Fourier rows are allowed. Each core comes with a supplied normalized complete finite-cost spectral reference Q_j, cost c_j=CE_Qj(g_j). No universal availability of c_j<=C I(g_j) is assumed.

Put mu_j=E g_j, mu=product mu_j, P_j=ghat_j^2, P=product P_j, Q=product Q_j, R=(P+delta_empty)/2, q=(Q+delta_empty)/2, and let nu_F(A|T) be the actual parent conditional shared Fourier law, fixed from the two known branch spectra. On zero true rows choose any fixed normalized conditional. The normalized parent reference is Q_F(A,T)=nu_F(A|T)q(T).

Let eta(x)=-x log2 x with eta(0)=0 and

Psi(mu)=eta((1+mu)^2/4)+eta((1-mu)^2/4)+(1-mu^2)-eta(mu^2)/2.

Then

H(F)=H(G)/2+Psi(mu),
I(F)=sum_j I(g_j)/2+(1-mu)/2,
CE_QF(F)=sum_j c_j/2+Psi(mu)-D_ref,
D_ref=sum_j KL(P_j||Q_j)/2-KL(R||q)>=0.

The refund is exactly the physical-label posterior KL, concentrated in the empty private row:

D_ref=(1+mu^2)/2 * KL_Ber(mu^2/(1+mu^2) || Q(empty)/(1+Q(empty))).

For Gamma=max_{0<d<=1}[4-4d+Ppair(d)/d], Ppair(d)=eta((1-d)^2)+eta(d^2)-eta((1-2d)^2)/2, A060's existing fine profile result gives 4.872<Gamma<4.873 and Psi(mu)<=Gamma(1-|mu|)/2. A self-contained simpler fallback is Psi(mu)<=gamma_0(1-|mu|)/2 with gamma_0=4+5/(8ln2)<5.

If every supplied c_j<=C_j I(g_j), the same normalized parent reference obeys CE_QF(F)<=max(Gamma,C_1,...,C_r) I(F). Private inputs are paid at their physical half-branch weight, and the single actual shared influence is paid once. This conditional reference transfer supplies no arbitrary Boolean core certificate and no universal nonconstant-fallback theorem.

Only A018 stage2 Fraction log definitions are imported; their main search is excluded. No A019/A060 checker or source utility is imported. Source receipts bind available bytes at stage close; mutable board/rolling hashes do not imply immutable start snapshots.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Independent full-scope A019 reconstruction

For a=g, b=g(-1)^1_E and D=g1_E, partition each directed private edge using the real Dirichlet normalization (h(x)-h(x^i))^2/(4*2^n). An internal pivotal edge contributes equally to J=(Ia+Ib)/2 and kappa=I(D); an external pivotal edge contributes to J only. On a crossing edge one sign child is pivotal and the other is not, so J has twice the derivative contribution. Crossing contributions cancel in2kappa-J. Thus exactly2kappa-J=P_in-P_out, and half-private iff P_in<=P_out. Point edits have kappa=n2^-n/2; if r original incident directions were pivotal, J=I(g)+2^-n(n-2r)>=n2^-n because I(g)>=2r2^-n. No flat spectrum is required.

For an arbitrary shared edge and independent private modules, write C_j=sum |T| ahat_j(T)bhat_j(T), q_j=E a_j b_j and Q_-j=product_{h!=j}q_h. Product cross-energy is sum C_j Q_-j, so

I(D_product)=sum_j[(1-Q_-j)J_j,edge/2+Q_-j kappa_j]
           =sum_j[J_j,edge+Q_-j(2kappa_j-J_j,edge)]/2.

For an unchanged mask, the effective edit is EMPTY: kappa=0 and correction=-J, not0. For changed edits of density<=1/2, q_j>=0. Under P_in<=P_out every edge account is <=J_j,edge/2. Averaging both directions of the two-shared-bit prefix uses one account u_j<=J_j. Parseval gives T_private=2J+W, with the stated signed edge-average W, and adds the root mixed charge2E_zw. Since2E_zw<=d, W<=0 impliesT<=2J+d<=2I. The two-bit source witness g=[1,1,-1,1], E={0,2} has J1,kappa3/4,q0 and refutes the unconditional half-private extension.

For arbitrary supplied four-leaf references, their physical mixture with the actual parent conditional is normalized, and log-sum bounds its data KL by the physical average child KL. Hence CE_root<=cbar+Delta_block H. The previously independently accepted B04-NODE-BOUNDARY@1 on the prescribed two-query tree gives CE<=cbar+T+(3/ln2)d; the stated conditional max(C+2,1+3/ln2) bound follows. This reconstructs every clause at its stated scope without repair. It does not infer a free terminal reserve or a necessary entropy obstruction from W>0.

### 2. One joint physical-label reference

For T nonempty, the constant branch has coefficient0 and the parent's two shared coefficients are Ghat(T)/2. Their squared masses are P(T)/4 each. On T empty the coefficients are(mu+1)/2 and(mu-1)/2. Summing these entropy packets gives H(F)=H(G)/2+Psi(mu). The private spectral influence is I(G)/2; physical shared disagreement is d=(1-mu)/2. Independence of private blocks makes H(G)=sum H(g_j) and I(G)=sum I(g_j).

The data law is R=(P+delta_empty)/2. Form q=(Q+delta_empty)/2 using the SAME one physical label. Q_F=nu_F q is normalized because every conditional sums to1, including zero true rows. Finite supplied CE ensures that q is positive on every positive true row, so the parent CE is finite. Its exact debt is KL(R||q), since its selector conditional matches the true conditional.

Apply the KL chain rule to the tagged law of (b,T), with b fair and branches P,delta_empty, and reference branches Q,delta_empty. Its joint KL is KL(P||Q)/2; its marginal data KL is KL(R||q). Their difference is posterior-label KL D_ref>=0. At every nonempty row the actual and reference label are both branch0, giving no posterior cost. At the empty row the actual label probability is mu^2/(1+mu^2) and the reference probability is Q(empty)/(1+Q(empty)). This proves the displayed exact binary refund formula, not just its log-sum upper bound. If mu=0 and Q(empty)=0, both Bernoulli parameters are0 and the cost is0; finite CE excludes the unsupported positive-true/zero-reference case.

Now CE_QF=H(F)+KL(R||q)=sum c_j/2+Psi-D_ref. This retains every terminal CE and uses a single shared physical prior. It does not assume cheap arbitrary terminals or erase a remaining nonconstant fallback.

### 3. Identify and consume A060's fine profile

Write d=(1-mu)/2. Then Psi(mu)=4d(1-d)+Ppair(d), so Psi(mu)/d is exactly A060's G(d). The symmetry Psi(mu)=Psi(-mu) means that values d>1/2 cannot exceed their folded ratio at1-d. No context-dependent character folding is performed or used to reduce the actual physical influence.

Here is a different strict-concavity proof and a self-contained fallback bound. For 0<d<1/2, expand

(1-d)^2 ln(1-d)=-d+3d^2/2-sum_{k>=3}2d^k/[k(k-1)(k-2)].

It follows that

ln2*G(d)=4ln2+d(3-4ln2-2ln d)
        -sum_{k>=3}(2^(k+1)-4)d^(k-1)/[k(k-1)(k-2)].

All coefficients of the subtracted series are positive. Its second derivative together with -2/d is strictly negative, proving G''<0. Series differentiation is valid on compact subintervals of(0,1/2); endpoint values follow by continuity. The coefficient k3 is2. Discarding all higher negative terms and using the tangent of -dln d at d=1/4 gives

ln2*G(d)<=4ln2+1/2+d-2d^2<=4ln2+5/8.

Indeed -dln d<=1/4+(2ln2-1)d, and5/8-(1/2+d-2d^2)=2(d-1/4)^2. Thus gamma_0=4+5/(8ln2)<5 is a safe elementary bound, not a new sharp constant.

For the existing sharp Gamma, directly differentiate Ppair or the equivalent series. G'(1/8)>0 is certified by7^126>3^60*2^256, and G'(1/4)<0 by3^5<2^8. Strict concavity gives one root and the global maximum there; symmetry handles d>1/2 and the limit at0 is4. Our own24 rational derivative bisections and a concave tangent at the lower bracket certify4872/1000<Gamma<4873/1000. This uses A018's independent80-bit rational-log arithmetic and the series proof rather than A060's source checker/integral proof. Any dyadic d is realized by an actual Boolean core with that negative fraction. Continuity and dyadic density prove necessity of the fine profile supremum. The scalar optimization was already done by A060; this is a scoped review and its reference application.

### 4. Preserve the supplied rate with one physical reserve

Let C=max(Gamma,C_1,...,C_r). Since D_ref>=0,

CE_QF<=sum_j c_j/2+Gamma(1-|mu|)/2
      <=C[sum_j I(g_j)/2+(1-mu)/2]=C I(F).

Each supplied core cost is paid at its actual half-branch probability. The selector term uses only its own physical influence, once. No additional rate2 is charged per merge, no module slack is assumed absent its certificate, and no loss-only bound for the previously refuted shared-product excess is invoked. Repeating this construction within the restricted constant-terminal/disjoint-private grammar preserves the same C, PROVIDED references are supplied at its terminal cores and the construction is used with the actual functions. This is a closure/application of known composition calculus, not a universal reduction that manufactures terminal references.

The previous eleven-bit positive-excess control survives: using exact point-core references, the new q equals actual R, so its complete CE is H(F). The separately lifted module-marginal product reference costs H(F)+TC. The difference is exactlyTC; G=H(F)-sum H(f_j)>0 remains positive. Thus the signed collision is not eliminated by asserting entropy subadditivity. The reference uses one physical branch label rather than r auxiliary ones.

### 5. Changing arbitrary supplied priors is signed

For comparison define q_sep(T)=product_j[(Q_j(T_j)+1{T_j=empty})/2] and q_joint=(product_j Q_j+delta_empty)/2. Both lifted parent references have the same true conditional selector law. Therefore

CE_sep-CE_joint=E_R log2(q_joint/q_sep).

For exact core references this equalsTC>=0. For arbitrary supplied references its sign is not guaranteed, even when the references satisfy a valid core rate. Take private point-defect cores of dimensions3 and2, whose means are3/4 and1/2 and influences3/4 and1. Give them uniform core references, with costs3 and2 respectively; both satisfy c_j<=5I_j. The six-bit parent has I=19/16, and exactly

CE_sep-CE_joint=[128ln2+73ln11-127ln3-80ln5]/(128ln2)<0,

proved without approximation by2^128*11^73<3^127*5^80. The parent rate guarantee from the DIRECT joint-reference proof remains valid; it does not rely on labeling this change a refund. This is not a negative D_ref: that posterior KL is always nonnegative, while the comparison of two different data priors is a separate signed quantity. Neither may be spent twice.

### 6. Exact first unsupported universal line

This transfer requires one actual branch to be constant. Replacing it by an arbitrary nonconstant fallback makes the physical-label posterior likelihood depend on every spectral row, not just(mu^2,Q(empty)). The first unsupported line is a noncircular bound for that full signed row transfer, using the SAME supplied core references and one genuinely unused child/physical reserve after a real core restriction. Terminal CE remains part of the bill. Mean-only profiles and the false shared entropy-subadditivity or loss-only prices cannot replace it.

NEXT makes one nonconstant fallback/actual core-data exposure, carries the joint reference and rowwise likelihood statistic, and tests whether an owned certified child surplus pays its signed change. The A019 internal-pivotal correction and A020 raw-overcharge control must both remain in the ledger. A free reset to an exact unknown core spectrum is not authorized by this conditional transfer.

# A019: exact low-energy collision payment for rare input translations

Run: 20261011T223425Z-fleet05-A019-orthogonality-extensions. Stream B04; packet A019. Full FEI remains OPEN. This is a narrow structural extension of the existing collision interface, with a complete analytic proof and exact author validation; independent review of the extension is pending. No literature priority claim is made.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Identify the uniform sign cube with F_2 coordinates, with characters chi_T(y)=(-1)^(T dot y). Let n>=0, r>=0, g:F_2^n->{-1,1} be arbitrary. For each j=1,...,r choose a positive integer k_j, a vector v_j in F_2^n, a point z_j in F_2^(k_j), and a fresh selector block W_j of k_j coordinates. The W_j are pairwise disjoint and independent of y. Translation vectors may coincide, overlap, or be linearly dependent; g need not be balanced, quadratic, or have disjoint Fourier supports. Define

A_j(w_j)=1{w_j=z_j}, tau(w)=sum_j v_j A_j(w_j), F(w,y)=g(y+tau(w)).

All sums in input arguments are mod two. Put p_T=hat(g)(T)^2, delta_j=2^(-k_j), rho_j=Pr_y[g(y)!=g(y+v_j)]=sum_{T:T dot v_j=1}p_T, and a=sum_j k_j. Entropies are in bits, influence is physical edge disagreement. Write E_k for the Fourier entropy of h_k(w)=1-2*1{w=z} on k bits:

E_k=-(1-2delta)^2 log2((1-2delta)^2)+4delta(1-delta)(2k-2), delta=2^(-k),

with 0 log 0=0. The exact conclusion is

H(F)=H(g)+sum_j rho_j E_(k_j),
I_data(F)=I(g), I_W(F)=sum_j rho_j (2 k_j delta_j),
Inf_i(F)=2 delta_j rho_j for each selector i in W_j,
M_W=0, B_W=Delta_W H=sum_j rho_j E_(k_j)<=4 I_W(F).

Consequently a certified H(g)<=C I(g) transfers to H(F)<=max{C,4} I(F). No theorem about arbitrary g's entropy is assumed or concluded. A one-block dictator core with rho=1 has I_W/a=2^(1-k)->0 and B_W/I_W=E_k/(2k2^(-k))->4. Thus 4 is the optimal uniform collision constant for this translation family, even in the low-disagreement regime. This is not an application of a uniform positive neighboring-disagreement bound.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. General disjoint-module translation identity

The useful algebra works for arbitrary Boolean activation functions A_j on disjoint W_j. Put h_j=(-1)^A_j. Fix w; a change of variable y'=y+tau(w) gives

hat(g(.+tau(w)))(T)=hat(g)(T)(-1)^(T dot tau(w))
=hat(g)(T) product_{j:T dot v_j=1} h_j(w_j).

All branches have the same squared spectrum p, including the empty Fourier row. Therefore R_T=p_T, r_T is uniform on all 2^a branch labels whenever p_T>0, and M_W=0. For each positive-mass T the selector phase is an actual Boolean function

h_T(w)=product_{j:T dot v_j=1} h_j(w_j).

Because those factors depend on disjoint physical blocks, its Fourier spectrum is their tensor product, with unused blocks carrying the empty Fourier atom. Thus

H(h_T)=sum_{j:T dot v_j=1} H(h_j),
I(h_T)=sum_{j:T dot v_j=1} I(h_j).

Equivalently, the exact parent coefficient is hat(g)(T) times the product of the selected module Fourier coefficients, and is zero if an unselected module has a nonempty selector frequency. Conditional on T, squared selector coefficients are precisely that normalized product distribution. Zero p_T rows are omitted, not assigned artificial conditional laws. Hence directly by the entropy chain rule and mean cardinality,

H(F)=H(g)+sum_j rho_j H(h_j),
I_W(F)=sum_j rho_j I(h_j), I_data(F)=I(g),
B_W=sum_j rho_j H(h_j), M_W=0.

Parseval gives the stated equality between rho_j and physical translation disagreement: E[g(y)g(y+v_j)]=sum_T p_T(-1)^(T dot v_j)=1-2rho_j. Flipping selector i in module j changes tau by v_j exactly when A_j changes. Conditional on every w, y+tau(w) is still uniform. This proves the finer identity

Inf_i(F)=rho_j Inf_i(h_j).

Coincident v_j do not cause unaccounted coefficient collisions: a fixed T selects physical tensor factors on labeled disjoint W_j. It is the physical disjointness of W_j, not any linear independence of v_j, that is used.

### 2. Rare-point module spectrum and constant 4

For h_k=1-2*1{w=z}, the empty coefficient is 1-2delta; every nonempty coefficient is -2delta chi_A(z). Their squared masses are t=(1-2delta)^2 and 4delta^2 repeated 2^k-1 times. Their sum is one. This yields E_k above, including k=1, where h_k is a character and E_1=0. Each physical coordinate is pivotal at two directed endpoints of one edge, so Inf_i(h_k)=2delta and I(h_k)=2kdelta.

For 0<=t<=1, -t ln t<=1-t, with the endpoints interpreted continuously. Here 1-t=4delta(1-delta). Therefore

E_k/(2kdelta)
<= (1-delta)[4-(4-2/ln 2)/k] <4.

The strict final inequality follows from ln 2>1/2 and k>=1. The first inequality is valid also at t=0. This is a complete analytic all-k bound; finite parameter checks are not the dimension bridge.

For k>=2, delta<=1/4 and t=1-u with u=4delta(1-delta). A useful explicit expansion bound is

0<=u+(1-u)ln(1-u)<=u^2/(2(1-u)).

Indeed the left side is sum_{l>=2}u^l/[l(l-1)], so its coefficients are positive and at most 1/2. Consequently

0<=4delta/ln2-[-t log2 t]
<= [4delta^2+u^2/(2(1-u))]/ln2.

After dividing the exact E_k by 2kdelta this proves, with a bounded remainder,

E_k/(2kdelta)=4-(4-2/ln2)/k+O(delta),

and in particular convergence to 4. No unsupported exchange of n, r, k or core parameters is needed; the exact weighted identities hold simultaneously for all of them. If I_W>0, B_W/I_W is the weighted average of E_(k_j)/(2k_j delta_j), with weights rho_j 2k_j delta_j/I_W. For I_W=0 the exact debt is zero.

### 3. Unbounded low-disagreement and depth regimes

Take n=1, g(y)=(-1)^y, r=1, v=1. Then F=(-1)^(y+A_k(w)), all 2^k selector labels choose between g and its sign flip, and every selector coordinate has disagreement 2^(1-k). These branches share every data Fourier index. The selector entropy increment divided by selector influence tends to 4, whereas I_W/a tends to zero. This is an actual Boolean family, not an arbitrary amplitude relaxation.

For unbounded module count, take any r, all k_j=K and v_j=1 with the same dictator core. Then I_W/a=2^(1-K), B_W/I_W=E_K/(2K2^(-K)); there is no arity loss and repeated translations are allowed.

For unbounded nesting depth, start with a certified core g_0. At step l, use fresh disjoint rare-point selector modules to translate arbitrary coordinates of the preceding entire data function g_(l-1), including its old selectors if desired. For each step H(g_l)<=H(g_(l-1))+4 I_new and I(g_l)=I(g_(l-1))+I_new. Telescoping yields

H(g_L)<=H(g_0)+4[I(g_L)-I(g_0)].

Hence the same max{C,4} constant holds at all finite depths. This allows sharing all old data coordinates across branches but requires fresh physical selector coordinates at each step. It is not a result for selectors reused as current data without this independence. No positive lower bound on disagreement is used.

### 4. Proper-block stopping certificate and singleton guard

One can query each entire W_j as a block. At every branch the queried translation merely shifts the remaining core, so its squared spectrum and all rho_l stay the same. Eliminating W_j decreases entropy by rho_j E_(k_j) and influence by rho_j 2k_j delta_j. The terminal function is a translate of g; it has entropy H(g) and influence I(g). Thus this is a finite physical block tree with

sum_v pi_v B_(W_v)<=4 sum_v pi_v I_(W_v), sum_v pi_v M_(W_v)=0,

and the complete terminal cost retained. For n>=1, querying all remaining selectors is itself a proper block; alternatively individual complete modules are proper except a degenerate no-data situation where the function is constant. This is a C019 certificate on the class, with no reused physical influence.

The constant 4 must not be applied to every singleton query. For g a dictator and one three-bit module, H(F)=E_3, the two singleton children have entropies 0 and E_2=2, and Inf_i(F)=1/4. Therefore Delta_i H/Inf_i=4(E_3-1)>4. Exactly E_3=4-(9/8)log2 3>2 since 3^9<2^16. The full three-bit block still obeys B_W<=4 I_W. This guard shows why the block certificate is the correct payment granularity.

### 5. Imported bent-splice falsifier

Let m>=2 be even, delta=2^(-m), b(y)=(-1)^(y_1 y_2+...+y_(m-1)y_m), b'=b-2*1{y=0}. A one-bit selector between b and b' has I_sel=delta. The two-bit product transform gives c_T=hat(b)(T)=+/-sqrt(delta). Parent coefficients on the two selector sectors are c_T-delta and delta, so R_T=(c_T-delta)^2+delta^2 and minority-sector mass e_T=delta^2. Since sqrt(delta)<=1/2,

R_T/e_T>=1/(2delta).

The elementary h2(x)>=x log2(1/x) gives B_sel>=sum_T e_T log2(R_T/e_T)>=delta(m-1). Thus B_sel/I_sel>=m-1. M_sel is nonnegative, so the entire increment has the same divergent lower bound. This is the source-credited inherited falsifier, not a new discovery.

The present structural hypothesis excludes this family at an exact line: branch magnitudes must be identical. Here hat(b')(T)=c_T-2delta and its squared magnitude equals c_T^2 only if c_T=delta, impossible for 0<delta<1 and c_T=+/-sqrt(delta). An input translation cannot implement this point surgery. In contrast, rare translations can have small physical disagreement while preserving every magnitude. Common magnitudes alone remain insufficient for a universal constant, because branches h(w)(-1)^y encode arbitrary Boolean h; our disjoint point-module factorization supplies the additional paid structure.

### 6. First unproved universal line

There is no unproved line in the stated translation-class theorem. The universal extension is not proved: an arbitrary Boolean function need not admit a proper-block restriction tree whose selector coefficient rows factor into these disjoint rare-point phases and whose remaining terminal entropy is already certified. Allowing arbitrary row phases would recover the full FEI problem. Allowing overlapping selector modules invalidates the product-law proof and requires a new collision calculation. The singleton guard also prevents silently refining the block certificate at the same constant.

# A019 stage 2: one shared selector bit, exact collision and a good-node tree

Run 20261012T031916Z-fleet05-A019-orthogonality-extensions, B04/A019. Full FEI remains OPEN. This report continues the already reviewed translation mechanism under the stream lead's direction. It proves an arbitrary-activation transfer, extends it to unbounded star overlap through one shared physical selector, and gives a terminal-inclusive selector-first tree with zero localized bad-node boundary bill at threshold 3 for point modules. New extension review is pending; no literature priority claim is made.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

All cubes are uniform and physical; inputs are written in F_2, entropy uses bits, and influence of Boolean signs is edge disagreement. Dirichlet energy of a real derivative D is I(D)=sum_U |U| Dhat(U)^2, not a trinary edge-disagreement probability.

Let g:F_2^n->{-1,1} be arbitrary, x a fresh physical bit, and Z_1,...,Z_r mutually disjoint fresh private blocks, independent of the data y and x. For arbitrary Boolean activations A_j:Z_j->{0,1}, vectors v_j in F_2^n, and orientations s_j in {0,1}, set

F(x,z,y)=g(y+sum_j v_j 1{x=s_j} A_j(z_j)).

There is no bound on r, n, private block dimensions, or overlap/dependence between the vectors v_j. Define h_j=(-1)^A_j, m_j=E h_j, rho_j=Pr[g(y)!=g(y+v_j)], and p_T=ghat(T)^2. For each T let J_T={j:T dot v_j=1},

alpha_T=product_{j in J_T:s_j=0}m_j, beta_T=product_{j in J_T:s_j=1}m_j,
d_T=(1-alpha_T beta_T)/2,
I_priv=(1/2)sum_j rho_j I(h_j), I_x=sum_T p_T d_T.

Empty products equal one; zero p_T rows are omitted. The full selector block is W={x} union all private coordinates. Exact identities and bounds are

M_W=0, I_W=I_priv+I_x, I_data=I(g),
B_W=H(F)-H(g)=(1/2)sum_j rho_j H(h_j)+sum_T p_T kappa(alpha_T,beta_T),
B_W <= (1/2)sum_j rho_j H(h_j)+I_priv+(3/ln2)I_x,
B_x(F) <=3 I_x,
2 I(D_x F)=I_priv+2 sum_T p_T |T| d_T.

The kappa function is defined explicitly below. Applying the boundary lemma to Boolean selector phases, instead of to F as a whole, removes the final data-core term from the entropy-decrement upper bound. This is an exact removal of that term, not an assumption that g's entropy is already paid.

If H(h_j)<=C_act I(h_j) for all j, then

B_W<=max(C_act+1,3/ln2) I_W,
H(F)<=max(C_core,C_act+1,3/ln2) I(F)

provided separately that H(g)<=C_core I(g). For private point activations A_j=1{z_j=z_j*}, C_act=4 is the inherited module bound, so B_W<=5 I_W, irrespective of r and how small neighboring disagreement becomes.

For those point modules, query x first, then private selector bits, stopping at a translate of g. Every queried selector node satisfies exact B_i<=3 Inf_i, including all restrictions and zero-influence nodes. Thus the lead's localized boundary bill over selector nodes with B_i>3 Inf_i is exactly ZERO, without reusing any influence. Terminal entropy remains H(g) and terminal influence I(g). A majority core and arbitrary finite fresh nesting give unbounded terminal-certified examples. Constant 5 is a proved upper bound, not an optimality assertion.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Inherited arbitrary-activation disjoint transfer

Without x, let F_0(z,y)=g(y+sum_j v_j A_j(z_j)) on disjoint Z_j. Translation gives the data coefficient row

c_T(z)=ghat(T) product_{j:T dot v_j=1} h_j(z_j).

Its squared magnitude is p_T at every z. Conditional selector Fourier spectra tensorize across disjoint private blocks. Consequently, exactly

B_W=sum_j rho_j H(h_j), M_W=0,
I_W=sum_j rho_j I(h_j), I_data=I(g).

Thus certified constants C_core and C_act transfer at max(C_core,C_act). The coefficient proof and empty-row conventions are already in A019 stage 1, section 1; arbitrary activation does not supply a certificate H(h_j)<=C_act I(h_j) automatically. Taking g a dictator, r=1 and unrestricted A_1 recovers arbitrary Boolean selector phases, so that missing certificate is full-FEI strength. This paragraph completes the lead's requested transfer statement without claiming a new general theorem about those phases.

### 2. One shared bit: exact phase coefficients and empty-sector correction

For the star-overlap F in the target, the row phase h_T is Boolean. Its x=0 child is

u_(0,T)=product_{j in J_T:s_j=0} h_j(z_j),

and its x=1 child is u_(1,T) with s_j=1. The two children depend on disjoint private coordinates. Their means are alpha_T,beta_T, their entropies sum to sum_(j in J_T) H(h_j), and their influences sum to sum_(j in J_T) I(h_j).

For a nonempty private frequency belonging to child b, the two parent x coefficients are +c_U/2 and +/-c_U/2. Frequencies from different children cannot collide except at the empty private frequency. There the two amplitudes are

a=(alpha+beta)/2, b=(alpha-beta)/2.

Let eta(t)=-t log2 t, with eta(0)=0, and write

kappa(alpha,beta)=2-alpha^2-beta^2+eta(a^2)+eta(b^2)
                         -(eta(alpha^2)+eta(beta^2))/2.

Then H(h_T)=(H(u_0)+H(u_1))/2+kappa(alpha,beta). The term 2-alpha^2-beta^2 comes from splitting the nonempty private masses into two parent atoms of one-quarter child mass. This identity uses the actual signed coefficients before squaring; it is not an incoherent mixture assertion. The exact parent spectrum is ghat(T) times the selector phase transform. Hence M_W=0, H(F)=H(g)+sum_T p_T H(h_T), and the target collision identity follows.

The conditional selector x influence is d_T=(1-alpha beta)/2, because the product of the child means is their correlation. Private influence sums at the parent are half the sums of the child's private influences. Averaging over T gives I_x and I_priv as stated; data influence remains I(g). This proves every influence normalizer directly in physical coordinates.

### 3. Boundary payment with the data-core term removed

B04-NODE-BOUNDARY@1, independently reconstructed by A018, says for any genuine Boolean h and split i,

Delta_i H(h)<=(3/ln2)Inf_i(h)+2 I((h_0-h_1)/2).

For the Boolean phase h_T above, the two children have disjoint private Fourier supports apart from the empty frequency. That frequency has zero weight in Dirichlet energy. Therefore

I((u_0-u_1)/2)=(I(u_0)+I(u_1))/4.

Apply the local lemma to h_T, then average with the fixed p_T. This gives

H(F)-average_x H(F_x)
<= (3/ln2)I_x+I_priv.

The branch-average entropy is H(g)+(1/2)sum_j rho_j H(h_j); adding gives the complete B_W bound. If each module certificate uses C_act, this becomes (C_act+1)I_priv+(3/ln2)I_x. Since I(F)=I(g)+I_priv+I_x, the full closure follows with the certified core constant.

For comparison, the actual derivative D_x F has coefficient energy

I(D_x F)=sum_T p_T[I(D_x h_T)+|T| d_T].

Thus the raw node-boundary charge on F includes the additional nonnegative 2sum_T p_T |T|d_T. Our conditional-row proof does not charge that term. It is allowed because h_T is genuinely Boolean in this translation class, unlike a general arbitrary coefficient row. For arbitrary shared-data branches, normalizing a row need not produce a Boolean phase, and this substitution cannot be assumed. The terminal H(g) has not been converted into a spendable influence bank.

For self-contained dependency reconstruction, the local lemma follows from Delta<=h2(d)+(2/ln2)d, h2(d)<=d log2(1/d)+d/ln2, and d log2(1/d)<=2I(D). The last inequality comes from entropy tensorization for the indicator of supp(D); on a boundary edge D has squared change at least that of the indicator. This is the accepted A018 proof, not a new boundary result.

### 4. Exact root erasure, including the initially missed term

Define Phi(A,B)=(A+B)h2(B/(A+B)), zero when A+B=0. The exact x erasure in a row is

b(alpha,beta)=1-(alpha^2+beta^2)/2+Phi((alpha+beta)^2/4,(alpha-beta)^2/4).

The first term is essential: all nonempty private frequencies carry an equiprobable x-frequency bit. The initial helper omitted this term and failed its exact identity assertion. A dictator core with one one-bit point activation gated by x already has B_x=1, whereas the incomplete formula returns 1/2. No published claim used that incomplete formula; out/PILOT_FAILURE.json and code/failed_star_v1.py preserve it. The proposed B_x<=2I_x was also false: alpha=1,beta=1/2 gives B_x=(5/8)h2(1/10)+3/8>2*(1/4). The repaired uniform bound is 3, proved next.

### 5. Scalar root bound b(alpha,beta)<=3(1-alpha beta)/2

This holds for all alpha,beta in [-1,1], not just dyadic means. Replacing beta by -beta interchanges the two empty-sector masses and leaves b invariant. It suffices to prove the bound for d=(1-|alpha beta|)/2 in [0,1/2]; the original d is at least this value. Put e=(alpha-beta)^2/4 after choosing same signs, q=(alpha^2+beta^2)/2=1-2d+2e. The identity

(1-alpha beta)^2-(alpha-beta)^2=(1-alpha^2)(1-beta^2)>=0

gives e<=d^2. At fixed d, b=2d-2e+Phi(q-e,e) is increasing in e, because its derivative is log2(q^2/[4e(q-e)])>=0 (extend zero endpoints by continuity). Thus

b<=2d(1-d)+Phi((1-d)^2,d^2).

Use the elementary binary bound h2(t)<=2sqrt(t(1-t)). For completeness, write t=(1+u)/2. The difference sqrt(1-u^2)-h2((1+u)/2) is zero at u=0,1; its derivative changes sign once, because R(u)=sqrt(1-u^2)atanh(u)/u decreases from 1 to 0. Indeed R'/R=[u/atanh(u)-1]/[u(1-u^2)]<0. Hence the difference is nonnegative. For d>=1/4 this yields b<=4d(1-d)<=3d.

For 0<d<=1/4, use Phi(A,e)<=e log2((A+e)/e)+e/ln2 and q<=1. The displayed upper bound divided by d is at most

U(d)=2(1-d)+d[2 log2(1/d)+1/ln2].

Its derivative is 2log2(1/d)-2-1/ln2>0 on this interval because ln2>1/2. Therefore U(d)<=U(1/4)=5/2+1/(4ln2)<3. The case d=0 has b=0. Averaging the scalar bound over p_T proves B_x(F)<=3I_x. No bound on core influence, bias or dimension was used.

### 6. Point-module hereditary singleton payment and the two-level tree

After fixing x, active modules have disjoint private blocks, and inactive modules are irrelevant. If A_j is a point indicator, any private restriction either kills that event, makes its translation fixed, or leaves a point indicator on fewer free coordinates. Earlier fixed translations preserve every p_T. Thus the disjoint phase identity applies at every such descendant, and each private node's B_i and Inf_i are the same rho-weighted scalar point-module quantities.

For a point sign h_m on m>=2 remaining coordinates, let delta=2^-m. On the queried coordinate's empty residual frequency the masses are (1-2delta)^2 and 4delta^2; on each other residual frequency they are both 4delta^2. Hence

B_i(h_m)=4delta-8delta^2+Phi((1-2delta)^2,4delta^2), Inf_i(h_m)=2delta.

Since the empty pair's total is at most 1,

B_i/Inf_i <=2+delta[4m-8+2/ln2]<3.

The last line uses ln2>1/2 and m-1<=2^(m-2), valid for all m>=2 by induction. For m=1 the point sign is a character and B_i=0. Killed or fixed events give zero. Tensor products of disjoint phases show that conditioning on other module Fourier labels leaves this same module law, so the weighted B_i<=3Inf_i transfers to the physical parent without a core charge.

Query the shared bit first, then the private selector coordinates; stop before querying any core coordinate. This is the lead's two-level organization: shared-bit split, followed by private-module subtrees, with each complete-module query also available as a block. Root B_x<=3I_x and every subsequent selector node has B_i<=3Inf_i. Consequently

sum_(selector nodes with B_i>3Inf_i) pi_v 2I(D_v)=0,
sum_selector pi_v Inf_i=I_W(F), E_leaf H=H(g), E_leaf I=I(g).

It is important that the zero statement uses exact B, not h2(Inf), and that it is restricted to the queried selector portion with certified terminal core required for full closure. The full entropy is independently paid at H(g)+5I_W by section 3. No descendant or terminal influence is spent twice.

### 7. Majority cores, low disagreement, and nesting

For odd n, take g(y)=sgn(sum_i(-1)^(y_i)). If a vector v flips t coordinates, the exact translation disagreement is

rho(v)=2^-n sum_(i=0..t,j=0..n-t) binom(t,i)binom(n-t,j)
                      1{|t-2i|>|n-t-2j|}.

The two compared sums are A+B and -A+B; odd n excludes ties, proving the formula. Therefore all star influences and exact entropy expressions can be computed with finite integer sums without enumerating all selector branches. Threshold Theorem A certifies H(g)<=C_reg I(g), C_reg<8.937 and >5, so the star extension preserves C_reg uniformly in n,r and widths. This uses a baseline certified leaf, not a new proof of majority FEI.

For any nonconstant core and v with rho(v)>0, one private point module of width k with the extra common x is just a point-triggered translation on k+1 controls. Its neighboring disagreement tends to zero, while the exact phase spectrum still applies. Multiple compatible and incompatible orientations share x, and the result holds for unbounded r; no lower disagreement bound is assumed.

Each finite fresh nesting level gives H(g_l)<=H(g_(l-1))+5[I(g_l)-I(g_(l-1))], so constants remain max(C_base,5). An input translation of a lower-level star changes point targets and shared orientations but preserves this allowed class. Consequently, for point modules the same outer-to-inner selector-first singleton tree has zero bad-selector bill at threshold 3 at every level, ending at the certified base core.

### 8. Exact first unsupported universal line

The derived star theorem is complete. The universal extension is unproved: for arbitrary Boolean F, there need not be a proper-selector block with common squared data spectra whose normalized rows are Boolean phases, nor a physical separator after which those phases have disjoint private supports. The exact row-wise removal of the data-core term fails at this unproved Boolean-phase admission line. Arbitrary activation phases also need their own FEI/hereditary certificate, and arbitrary g remains a full terminal obligation. The bent splice changes branch magnitudes and therefore still excludes a universal selector-only payment. We import that existing falsifier; its lower-bound computation is not repeated this stage.
