## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

1. **Fixed-reference transport.** For any supplied finite-cost Q and physical query i, write Q'=sum_e Q(e,T). Parseval applied to the two actual restrictions gives avg_b P_(f_b)(T)=sum_e P_f(e,T). Hence

CE(f,Q)=avg_b CE(f_b,Q')+L_i,
L_i=sum_(e,T) P_f(e,T) log2[Q'(T)/Q(e,T)]>=0.

Support is valid because a positive child atom has positive averaged mass. Q'(T)>=Q(e,T)>0 on each positive summand. Thus no denominator vanishes and the nonnegative code charges telescope. This is the scoped A016 identity, reconstructed from actual Walsh transforms; it is not a universal construction of Q.

2. **All prefixes with the same Q.** After q and t v queries, put z=2^t d^2. On remaining core labels (u followed by k-t v bits), the core reference marginal is

Q_t(empty)=(1+z)/2,
Q_t({u})=(1-2d+z)/2,
Q_t(T)=z/2 otherwise.

It is normalized, strictly positive for k>=3, and the full reference still has the displayed uniform gate conditional and the same mask reference. Exactly one v prefix is active, with probability 2^-t. There h_q=chi_u off the remaining all-zero v event and (-1)^q on it; every inactive prefix is exactly chi_u. This identity, not an approximate symmetry, reduces the full history calculation to the active and inactive cases for both q signs.

Let C_t be the full expected CE after those t v queries, and I_t the full expected remaining physical influence. Direct physical edges give

I_root=(3+kd)/2,
I_t=3/2+(k-t-1)d/2.

The removed influence is (t+1)d/2. If L_[t]=C_root-C_t is the complete cumulative code charge, the signed reserve is exactly

R_t=4I_t-C_t=R_root-[2(t+1)d-L_[t]].

For the common child spectrum P, set c=CE(P,(P+point_empty)/2). Its entropy is H_k=eta((1-d)^2)+(2^(k+1)-1)d^2(2k), and its KL is at most 1. Since H_k<4(k+1)d, C_root=c/2+3 and R_root>5/2-2d. Since L_[t]>=0,

R_t>5/2-(2k+4)d>=5/4

for all k>=3 and 0<=t<=k. The endpoint inequality uses that (2k+4)2^-k decreases for k>=3. No terminal deficit is discarded: if D_t=E(CE-4I)_+ and N_t=E(4I-CE)_+, then N_t-D_t=R_t. The checker retains both separately at every depth. This is an expected signed certificate; it does not assert that every history has its own nonnegative local reserve.

3. **Final terminal costs and positive deficit.** After all v queries, the core marginal is ((1+d)/2,(1-d)/2). At every inactive history the core is chi_u, with complete terminal cost

C_par=5-(1/2)log2[(9+d)(1-d)].

At the two active histories h=+1 and h=-1, both complete terminal costs are

C_con=5-[5log2(9+d)+3log2(1+d)]/8.

Their influences are respectively 3/4 and 5/4; the inactive influence is 3/2. Therefore C_end=(1-d)C_par+d C_con. We have C_par>27/8 and C_con>23/8, by 9^4<2^13, (73/8)^4<2^13 and (9/8)^4<2. Thus C_end>53/16. Also C_con<25/8 because log2(9+d)>3. Only the positive constant history can have a terminal deficit, and its weighted positive deficit satisfies

D_end=(d/2)(C_con-3)_+<d/16<=1/128.

The A016 sign C_con>3 for k>=6 follows from (9+d)^5(1+d)^3<2^16, monotonicity in d, and the exact endpoint 577^5*65^3<2^64. All costs retain the unused ghost atoms in the original reference.

4. **Original half-core bank, no renewal.** The stage5 named reserve is S=2I(h_q)-c/2=2+2(k-1)d-c/2. Let J=C_root-average fresh child CE be the exact already-booked first insertion, and E=C_0-average fresh child CE the conversion to the inherited marginal. As A016 derives, 0<=E<d^2: only the empty-data gate conditional changes, and its conditional KL is bounded by chi-square/ln2 with ln2>1/2. The first debit obeys J<5/8. Carry this exact J forward rather than pretending that the full conservative ceiling was an actual expense.

The sum of subsequent query charges is C_0-C_end. Consequently the total debit, INCLUDING conversion, is

J+(C_0-C_end)=C_root-C_end+E.

The remaining SAME reserve is therefore

S-[J+C_0-C_end]=C_end-[c+1-2(k-1)d]-E.

For a uniform all-k bound, ln2>2/3 gives

H_k<(4k+3)d-(2k+3/2)d^2.

Indeed eta((1-d)^2)<(2d-d^2)/ln2 and the remaining entropy is exactly (4kd-2kd^2). Thus

c+1-2(k-1)d<2+(2k+5)d-(2k+3/2)d^2<=417/128.

At k=3 the last expression is 417/128. At k>=4 its upper bound 2+(2k+5)2^-k is decreasing and at most 45/16<417/128. Combining C_end>424/128 and E<2/128 leaves more than 5/128. Paying D_end<1/128 separately still leaves more than 1/32. This is one explicit allocation of disjoint expenses from the original S; no bank is regenerated at a descendant. It is separate from, and consistent with, the full signed physical-influence reserve R_t.

5. **New nonconstant local-eligibility falsifier.** At the actual history q=0 and v_1=...=v_(k-1)=0, the remaining core is the two-bit point defect chi_u off v_k=0 and +1 on it. It is nonconstant. Its full activation has I=11/8, while its inherited full reference costs

C_near=6+k/4-[17log2(18+d)+3log2(2+d)+4log2(2-3d)]/32.

For k>=8, d<=1/256. The exact integer inequalities (4609/256)^4<2^17 and (513/256)^4<2^5, together with 0<2-3d<2, imply that the bracket is strictly below 80. Hence C_near>7/2+k/4>=11/2=4I. The denominator 2-3d is positive throughout. Thus even keeping both physical branches nonconstant does not guarantee inherited rate-4 eligibility. The history has probability d after q is included. Its cost grows with k but its weighted contribution is of order kd, consistent with the signed reserve. This does not refute FEI or the supplied-reference telescoping identity.

**First unsupported universal line:** an arbitrary Boolean core must admit a constructible initial reference and a conserved source bank that cover these signed profile costs under a proper query, without knowing its Fourier entropy. This stage proves that conservation on the fixed rare-query family; it does not supply the arbitrary-core premise. A local eligibility requirement is now falsified even before the constant endpoint.

SOURCE agents\A021\runs\20261011T214020Z-fleet05-A021-rare-core-asymptotics\REPORT.md
# A021 / B05: uniform rare-core asymptotics and control-first debt

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For every balanced Boolean seed b on n independent uniform sign bits, every integer t>=1 and k>=1, put h=H(b), i=I(b), g_t=product of t disjoint copies of b, q=2^{-k}, A_k=indicator of the all-positive control point, and F_{k,t}=1+A_k(g_t-1). Dimension is k+nt. All H, KL, and logarithms are in bits; c=1/ln(2). The two parameters k,t remain independent throughout the identities and remainder estimates. The choice of control point changes Fourier signs only. The validation uses point zero in bit coordinates, equivalently the all-positive point after encoding.

Target: obtain exact entropy/influence/variance and control-first mismatch-KL debt, uniform remainders, and conditional necessary tests for an entropy-dominating influence-bounded global potential. This packet does not attempt a universal potential construction. Full FEI remains OPEN.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Exact spectrum and all entropy terms

Tensor independence gives H(g_t)=th, I(g_t)=ti, and E g_t=0. For a data set T and control set U, the indicator coefficient is q times a sign epsilon_U. Consequently:

- empty T,U: coefficient 1-q, probability (1-q)^2;
- empty T, nonempty U: coefficient -q epsilon_U, probability q^2, multiplicity 2^k-1, total mass q-q^2;
- nonempty T and arbitrary U: coefficient q epsilon_U ghat_t(T), probability q^2 ghat_t(T)^2, total mass q.

These masses sum to one. No incoherent approximation or Boolean realizability assumption enters. Summing -p log p over the three classes gives

    H(F)=qth+B_k(q),
    B_k(q)=(4q-2q^2)k+b(q),
    b(q)=-2(1-q)^2 log(1-q).

In detail, the data-class entropy is qth+2kq, pure-control entropy is 2k(q-q^2), and constant-atom entropy is b(q). Every data influence is q times its core influence. A control bit is pivotal if the other k-1 controls match and g_t=-1, probability 2^{-(k-1)}/2=q. Hence

    I(F)=q(ti+k), E F=1-q, Pr(F=-1)=q/2,
    V=Var(F)=2q-q^2.

The spectral empty/nonempty split can also be retained exactly:

    H(F)=h_2(V)+V H(S | S nonempty),
    H(S | S nonempty)=[qth+B_k(q)-h_2(V)]/V.

This prevents losing normalization or the constant atom by replacing full entropy with a conditional spectrum. For any 0<v<1,

    h_2(v)=v log(1/v)+cv-cv^2/2+R_v,
    -cv^3/[6(1-v)] <= R_v <= 0.

Proof: -(1-v)ln(1-v)=v-sum_{j>=2}v^j/[j(j-1)], and the tail coefficients are at most 1/6. Substitution v=V is uniform in t; here V<=3/4.

### 2. Uniform expansion, with no restriction on tensor growth

Expanding the convergent logarithmic series for q<=1/2 gives

    b(q)=2cq-3cq^2+R(q),
    R(q)=-4c sum_{j>=3} q^j/[j(j-1)(j-2)],
    -2cq^3/[3(1-q)] <= R(q) <= 0.

The coefficients follow by multiplying sum q^j/j by 2(1-2q+q^2). All terms in R are negative, and their absolute coefficients are at most 2/3. Therefore, for every t,k,

    H(F)=q[th+4k+2c]-q^2[2k+3c]+R(q),
    I(F)=q(ti+k).

This is an absolute error bound independent of t, h, i, and seed dimension. In particular it does not require qt to be small. Let

    R0=(th+4k+2c)/(ti+k).

Then the exact ratio satisfies

    R0 - q(2k+3c)/(ti+k) - 2cq^2/[3(1-q)(ti+k)]
       <= H(F)/I(F) <= R0 - q(2k+3c)/(ti+k).

Since a balanced Boolean seed has i>=1 (Parseval on nonempty sets), these are uniform also over all balanced seeds. The correction is bounded independently of t by q(2+3c/k)+2cq^2/[3(1-q)k].

For a fixed seed, k->infinity and t/k->lambda in [0,infinity) imply

    H(F)/I(F) -> (lambda h+4)/(lambda i+1).

For t/k->infinity the limit is h/i. For t/k->0 it is 4. No convergence of t/k is needed for the uniform displayed interval. In the core-dominant small-influence regime k=o(t), qt->0, one has I(F)->0, minority probability ->0, and H(F)/I(F)->h/i. Example: t=k^2, q=2^{-k}.

For arbitrary nonconstant, possibly unbalanced f, use g_t=z product_{j=1}^t f_j instead. Then the same proof substitutes H(g_t)=tH(f), I(g_t)=tI(f)+1. Taking t=k^2 preserves H(f)/I(f), while influence and minority probability vanish. This reproves the baseline equivalence along a different explicit sequence; no new restricted endpoint is claimed.

### 3. Exact magnitude-mismatch/KL debt on a control split

At an active node with s>=1 controls remaining, set u=2^{-s}. Its children are constant 1 and an activation with s-1 controls and mask probability p=2u. The balanced core is unchanged. Denote the child Fourier coefficients a_W,b_W, packet law Q_W=(a_W^2+b_W^2)/2, magnitude mismatch m_W=(|a_W|-|b_W|)^2/4, mu=sum m_W, and nu_W=m_W/mu.

The all-empty packet has

    Q0=1-2u+2u^2, m0=u^2.

Every other nonzero packet has a_W=0, so m_W=Q_W/2. Thus mu=u and the remaining mismatch mass is u-u^2. This includes s=1, where the active child is the balanced core and Q0=1/2. Magnitude mismatch coincides with signed mismatch here because the empty child coefficient 1-2u is nonnegative and all other constant-child coefficients vanish. That coincidence is family-specific; magnitude mismatch and disagreement cannot be interchanged for general splits.

The full residual from the source theorem is therefore exactly

    J_s=mu[log(1/mu)-D_2(nu||Q)]
       =sum_W m_W log(Q_W/m_W)
       =u-u^2+u^2 log(Q0/u^2).

The KL credit is, explicitly,

    D_2(nu||Q)=s-1-u[2s-1+log Q0].

It is independent of t and of the balanced seed. The conditional packet entropy is exactly

    L_s=2(u-u^2)+Q0 h_2(u^2/Q0).

For s>=2, Q0>=1-2u and |log Q0|<=2cu/(1-2u)<=4cu. This yields uniform expansions

    J_s=u+(2s-1)u^2+E_s,       |E_s|<=4cu^3,
    L_s=2u+(2s-2+c)u^2+F_s,   |F_s|<=5cu^3.

For the second bound, write L_s=2(u-u^2)+u^2 log(Q0/u^2)+c u^2 minus a nonnegative tail bounded by c u^4/[2(Q0-u^2)]<=c u^4. Both errors in fact are nonpositive. The s=1 formulas are kept exact: J_1=1/2, L_1=1. They are not forced into a small-u expansion.

The physical entropy decrement is also exact and independent of t:

    Delta_s=B_s(u)-B_{s-1}(2u)/2, with B_0(1)=0.

At s=1 the active leaf's core entropy cancels separately and Delta_1=B_1(1/2)=2. This identity comes directly from Section 1 and needs no imported inequality. The source theorem's additive 3c mu term is a distinct, already linearly paid allowance; it is not part of J_s, and J_s is not the blind scalar mu log(1/mu).

For completeness its uniform small-u expansion, s>=2, is

    Delta_s=4u+(2s-4+3c)u^2+G_s,
    0<=G_s<=8cu^3/[3(1-2u)]<=16cu^3/3.

Indeed G_s=R(u)-R(2u)/2=4c sum_{j>=3}(2^{j-1}-1)u^j/[j(j-1)(j-2)] is nonnegative and at most |R(2u)|/2. Thus the entropy decrement, packet entropy and mismatch debt are each expanded uniformly in both parameters, with the final balanced-core split kept exact.

### 4. Exact control-first tree, nonreuse, and uniform tree asymptotics

Query each control, stopping immediately on the first mismatch. The active node with s controls remaining has physical reach probability w_s=2^{-(k-s)}=q/u. Inactive leaves are constant; the single fully active leaf is g_t and has reach q. The queried influence budget is exactly

    sum_s w_s mu_s=kq.

The unqueried leaf influence is qti, so I(F)=kq+qti: no unit is reused. Entropy telescopes exactly as

    H(F)=sum_s w_s Delta_s+qth=B_k(q)+qth.

Its complete mismatch debt is

    J_tree=sum_s w_s J_s=q[k+A_k],
    A_k=sum_{s=1}^k 2^{-s}[2s-1+log(1-2^{1-s}+2^{1-2s})].

For s=1 the bracket is zero. For s>=2, Q0>=1/2, so 2s-1+log Q0>=2s-2>=0; also log Q0<=0. Thus 0<=A_k<=sum_{s>=1}2^{-s}(2s-1)=3. The increasing sequence has a limit A in [0,3], and its tail obeys

    0<=A-A_k<=sum_{s>k}2^{-s}(2s-1)=(2k+3)2^{-k}.

Hence, uniformly in t,

    q(k+A)-q^2(2k+3) <= J_tree <= q(k+A),
    J_tree <= q(k+3) <= 4kq.

This closes the control-only mismatch payment for this family. If the source inequality Delta<=J+3c mu is used, the full upper ledger includes both J_tree+3ckq AND qth. The linear term is paid from queried control influence; terminal entropy requires a separate core payment from qti.

In contrast, the blind rarity charge sums to

    sum_s w_s mu_s log(1/mu_s)=q k(k+1)/2.

The exact weighted KL credit is the difference between this quantity and J_tree. Dropping it creates a factor of order k even on this ordinary balanced-core activation. This is not the sparse-bent surgery no-go and does not decide what tree is optimal for that different family.

### 5. Necessary potential tests, stated with their quantifiers

For any C and any functional P satisfying H(f)<=P(f)<=C I(f) on all these activations, necessarily

    P(F)/q >= th+4k+2c-q(2k+3c)+R(q)/q,
    C(ti+k) >= th+4k+2c-q(2k+3c)+R(q)/q.

Thus for every admitted seed, C>=h/i in the core-dominant regime and C>=4 in the control-dominant regime. A functional vanishing faster than qth when k=o(t) and h>0 cannot dominate entropy, even though I and minority probability vanish. The exact induction slack is

    C I(F)-H(F)
      =q[t(Ci-h)+(C-4)k-2c+q(2k+3c)]-R(q).

For C=h/i<4 and core-dominant t, the ratio still approaches C but the displayed finite-family slack is negative for all sufficiently large k. Limit ratio preservation must not be mistaken for inheritance of an exact finite constant.

For the specific tree potential T=J_tree+terminal_entropy, the terminal term is exactly qth. Its asymptotic coefficient is (lambda h+1)/(lambda i+1) for finite lambda=t/k, not the coefficient 4 of the actual entropy. If a proposed bound T<=K I is asserted for all these cores, it necessarily has K>=max(1,h/i). This is a conditional test of this explicit potential, not an assertion that every imaginable debt has that coefficient. Adding the separately paid 3c control allowance changes the selector coefficient to 1+3c and leaves the hidden-core obstruction unchanged.

Uniform FEI for this fixed-seed activation class follows, without any mismatch theorem, from b(q)<=2cq:

    H(F)/I(F) <= max(h/i,4+2c/k) <= max(h/i,4+2c).

The hidden seed ratio is fully retained. With arbitrary cores this is not an absolute bound, since a uniform h/i bound is exactly the missing FEI obligation.

First unsupported line toward full FEI: a dimension-independent bound on terminal H(g)/I(g) for arbitrary Boolean cores, or a new structural admission rule plus valid terminal payment. No such bound or rule is established here.

A node is strictly K-bad when B_i>Kd_i. For a complete physical restriction tree, define Q_bad,K=sum_{bad v} w_v c_v, with actual uniform-cube reach probabilities w_v. Parameters t,k remain independent until a limit is specified. All new computations stay at dimension <=13, and the all-parameter conclusions rest on the proofs below.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Repair: positive entropy remainder and negative decrement remainder

Put c=1/ln2, q=2^-k. Direct multiplication gives, for j>=3,

    1/j-2/(j-1)+1/(j-2)=2/[j(j-1)(j-2)]>0.

Therefore the correct uniform identity is

    b(q)=-2(1-q)^2 log2(1-q)=2cq-3cq^2+R(q),
    R(q)=+4c sum_{j>=3} q^j/[j(j-1)(j-2)],
    0<R(q)<=2cq^3/[3(1-q)] for 0<q<1.

For balanced seed b, h=H(b), i=I(b), its t-fold disjoint tensor g_t, and F=1+A_k(g_t-1), the exact spectrum from stage 1 still yields

    H(F)=q[th+4k+2c]-q^2(2k+3c)+R(q), I(F)=q(ti+k).

Let Ra=[th+4k+2c-q(2k+3c)]/(ti+k). The corrected interval is

    Ra <= H(F)/I(F) <= Ra+2cq^2/[3(1-q)(ti+k)].

The slack is q[t(Ci-h)+(C-4)k-2c+q(2k+3c)]-R(q): the remainder lowers slack. For a control entropy decrement at u=2^-s, s>=2,

    Delta_s=4u+(2s-4+3c)u^2+G_s,
    G_s=R(u)-R(2u)/2,
    -8cu^3/[3(1-2u)] <= G_s < 0.

Indeed the series coefficient is 4c(1-2^{j-1}), negative for each j>=3. The s=1 endpoint remains Delta_1=2. Exact spectra, influences, KL debt, tensor-ratio limits and the claim A021-CONTROL-FIRST-KL-LEDGER v1 remain unchanged. The stage-1 finite checker did not test this series sign. Two nominal independent acceptances repeated the same arithmetic error; they do not validate the false interval.

Historical artifacts are preserved. CORRECTION.md identifies affected source passages and consumers; replacement A021-UNIFORM-RARE-CORE-EXPANSION v2 is in this run. Claude B05 contributed the sign repair and cannot independently accept that repaired version. Rational interval certificates at q=2^-s for s=1..12 independently verify positive entropy remainders; s=2..12 certificates verify negative decrement remainders.

The direct bound b(q)<=2cq survives independently: -(1-q)ln(1-q)<=q, then multiply by 2(1-q). No surviving sufficiency bound is justified by discarding a falsely negative remainder.

### 2. Three identities for tracking actual bad nodes

If f is split into children a,b, the queried-coordinate Fourier erasure is

    B_i(f)=sum_W (ahat(W)^2+bhat(W)^2)/2
                     *h2((ahat(W)-bhat(W))^2/[2(ahat(W)^2+bhat(W)^2)]).

Zero packets contribute zero. This formula remains valid with signed child coefficients; h2 is symmetric if opposite signs change the conditional probability to its complement.

First, disjoint multiplication by another sign factor preserves B_i and d_i in the queried factor. Its independent squared spectrum is a tensor factor in the conditional entropy, and its square cancels in the children product.

Second, suppose both core children have mean zero and let F=1+A_k(g-1). Then

    B_i(F)=q B_i(g), d_i(F)=q d_i(g),
    c_i(F)=q c_i(g)+kq d_i(g).

Proof of erasure: every nonempty residual-data coefficient in each child is multiplied by q and repeated across 2^k control sets; packet entropy is homogeneous of degree two, giving q times the core packet sum. Empty data rows have equal coefficients -q for nonempty control sets and 1-q for the empty control set, so contribute zero. Mean-zero children are essential to this cancellation.

Proof of odd-face charge: the actual activated children product is 1+A_k(g_i^+ g_i^- -1). Its data influence is q I(g_i^+g_i^-). Its each control influence is 2q Pr(g_i^+g_i^-=-1)=2q d_i(g), giving the formula after division by two.

Third, all control nodes of a balanced-core activation are K=3-good. At an active node with s controls left, u=2^-s,

    B_control=L_s=2(u-u^2)+Q0 h2(u^2/Q0), Q0=1-2u+2u^2,
    d_control=u.

For s=1, L_s/u=2. For s>=2, the exact binary-entropy expression gives

    L_s/u <= 2+u(2s-2+c) <= 2+(2+c)/4 < 3.

To justify the first bound, use log Q0<=0 and
(Q0-u^2)log2[Q0/(Q0-u^2)]<=c u^2. The sequence 2^-s(2s-2+c) decreases for s>=2, so its maximum is at s=2; c<2 follows from ln2>1/2. A point-defect node on r>=2 bits has exactly the same B/d as this balanced-control node with s=r-1, while r=1 is a character with B=0. Therefore point-defect controls are also 3-good, uniformly in dimension. This direct estimate does not use the incorrectly signed stage-1 expansion.

### 3. A fixed balanced six-bit core and a finite exact leaf certificate

Let b4(x)=(-1)^(x1*x2+x3*x4) on four bits, b4' its value flip at x=0, a(y,x)=b4 for y=0 and b4' for y=1, and b(z,y,x)=z a(y,x), with fresh uniform sign z. This is balanced. Its influence is

    I(b)=1+I(a)=1+2+5/16=53/16.

Indeed I(b4)=2; the zero flip toggles one edge in each of four coordinates, so I(b4')=2+8/16; the selector influence is d=1/16. Averaging the data influences and adding d gives I(a)=37/16.

At the selector, the 16 bent Walsh coefficients are +/-1/4, with 10 positive and 6 negative. The flip subtracts 1/8 from every coefficient. Child packet masses are 5/128 (ten packets) and 13/128 (six packets); each mismatch mass is 1/256. Thus

    B_y=(10[10 log2 10-9 log2 9]+6[26 log2 26-25 log2 25])/256,
    256 ln2 (B_y-4/16)=192 ln2-180 ln3-200 ln5+156 ln13>0.

The last strict sign has a rational atanh certificate in out/validation.json. Balancing by z changes no selector erasure or influence. The actual children product is b4*b4', a point defect on four bits, so its odd-face charge is 4/16.

Every coordinate of every physical restriction of b4 or b4' has B_i<=4d_i. This is a finite proof obligation, closed by 216 split certificates: each of the two functions has 4*3^3=108 pairs (partial assignment, live coordinate). Output out/seed_split_certificates.json records every partial assignment, queried original coordinate, integer-Walsh denominator, exact influence, prime-log vector and rational sign enclosure. All 216 natural numerators of B_i-4d_i are nonpositive. No larger census or numerical extrapolation is used.

### 4. Exact complete-tree classifications for every t,k

Take t disjoint copies of b above, g_t=product b_j, and k fresh point controls, F=1+A_k(g_t-1). We use two fully specified complete orders:

    CF: controls; all t selectors; all 4t bent-data bits; all t balancing signs.
    DF: all t selectors; all 4t bent-data bits; all t balancing signs; controls.

On either order, K=4-bad nodes are exactly the selector stages, with branches stopped whenever the function is already constant.

For CF, the control nodes are good by Section 2. They leave one live core branch of probability q. At each selector stage the disjoint-factor identity leaves B_y and d unchanged, so every selector node is bad. After selectors have been queried, each factor is z_j times either bent child. During the bent-data queries the four-bit restriction certificate makes every node good, with disjoint factors affecting neither B nor d. The remaining signs form a character and have B=0. Hence

    Q_bad,4(CF)=qt/4.

For DF, unqueried balancing signs make both children mean zero throughout the selector and bent-data stages. Thus B and d scale by q exactly. Every selector stage stays bad, and its node odd-face charge is q(4+k)/16. Each stage's physical reach probabilities sum to one, so the total selector charge is qt(k+4)/16. All bent-data nodes remain good by exact scaling and the finite certificate. With at least two balancing signs left, each queried sign has B=0; the last sign produces a point defect on k+1 bits, whose B/d is less than 3 by Section 2. After the signs, the function is either constant or a point defect on the controls, again good. Consequently no hidden bad stages remain and

    Q_bad,4(DF)=qt(k+4)/16.

Together with I(F)=q(53t/16+k),

    Q_bad,4(CF)/I(F)=4t/(53t+16k)<=4/53,
    Q_bad,4(DF)/I(F)=t(k+4)/(53t+16k).

Taking t=k^2 gives the DF ratio asymptotic to k/53, despite I(F)->0 and Pr(F=-1)=q/2->0. CF remains paid. This is an obstruction to an arbitrary-order claim, not to the existence of a paid order.

### 5. Exact adaptive interleaving condition

The fixed six-bit witness also permits a policy-level identity. During a prefix, query only controls and previously unqueried selector bits, stopping on an inactive control. After every selector has been queried, finish by querying the bent-data bits, balancing signs, and any remaining controls. Let r_j be the number of controls still unrevealed just before the j-th selector query on the hypothetical branch where all controls are active; the rule may depend on earlier selector outcomes, so r_j is a random variable under uniform selector histories. Selector labels may also be chosen adaptively, provided each copy's selector is used once.

All nonselector nodes of this policy are good by the preceding proof. The exact localized charge is

    Q_bad,4(policy)=q/16 [4t+sum_{j=1}^t E r_j].

For a selector history with j-1 earlier selectors and k-r controls fixed active, its physical weight is 2^{-(j-1)}2^{-(k-r)}. Multiplying by its activation factor 2^-r leaves q 2^{-(j-1)}. Its unactivated charge is (4+r)/16. Summing over fair histories gives the formula, without charging inactive branches or assuming spectral independence of physical histories.

For any claimed constant K', the exact necessary and sufficient condition within this policy class is

    sum_j E r_j <= 16 K'(53t/16+k)-4t.

Thus when t>>k, a uniformly paid policy must keep the average number of unrevealed controls at bad selector queries bounded independently of k. CF has r_j=0; DF has r_j=k. This specifies the missing scheduling work instead of replacing it by another scalar rarity bound. An adaptive finite certificate (t=2,k=1) queries the control before the second selector only on the first-selector zero branch: sum E r_j=3/2 and Q_bad=19/64. All twelve deterministic prefix orders in the two three-coordinate cases were checked exactly as well.

### 6. EVERY-tree localized payment fails at every fixed threshold

The fixed K=4 example extends to any finite K>=0 without a new dimensional search. Choose an even m>=4 such that m-2>K; let bent_m be the product of m/2 quadratic two-bit bent factors, flip its value at zero, splice with a selector y, and balance with z. Put delta=2^-m. Each Walsh coefficient c_T of the bent function is +/-sqrt(delta); the flipped coefficient is c_T-2delta. Thus each mismatch packet has mass delta^2 and

    Q_T=(c_T^2+(c_T-2delta)^2)/2>=delta/4,
    B_y >= sum_T delta^2 log2(Q_T/delta^2)
        >= delta(m-2)>K delta.

The core selector is genuinely bad, with influence delta, core child-product charge m delta, and seed influence i_m=1+m/2+(m+1)delta. The fresh balancing sign keeps both children mean zero at every selector-first stage of the activated t-fold tensor, even after earlier selectors have been fixed. Therefore, for the designated complete DF tree,

    Q_bad,K(DF) >= qt delta(m+k),
    Q_bad,K(DF)/I(F) >= t delta(m+k)/(t i_m+k).

Set t=k^2 and let k grow, holding this m fixed. The lower bound diverges linearly in k. Hence for every fixed finite K,K' there are actual Boolean balanced-core activations and complete physical trees with Q_bad,K>K'I. This rules out a payment quantified over EVERY complete tree for any fixed threshold, not merely K=2 or K=4.

For K>=3 these examples have a paid alternative: CF has good control nodes, and the remaining localized charge is at most q Q(g_t)=qt Q(seed), with Q(seed) finite since m is fixed. The equality Q(g_t)=tQ(seed) follows because mixed two-faces between disjoint factors have corner product +1. This prevents promoting the bad DF ledger into an existential-tree refutation or an FEI counterexample.

The face normalization itself is directly checked: Q(f)=2^(1-n) times the odd-two-face count, and Q(f)=I(f_i^+f_i^-)/2+(Q(f_i^+)+Q(f_i^-))/2. A two-face involving i corresponds to an edge cut by the children product; other two-faces stay in one child. The frozen full-tree checks verify that both orders spend the same total Q while locating different fractions at bad nodes. Tree independence of TOTAL face mass does not imply tree independence of its BAD-node part.

First unsupported universal line: construct, for every Boolean f, a realizable policy whose bad-node odd-face mass is <=K'I(f) at an absolute sufficiently large K, together with a nonreusing terminal entropy payment if the policy stops early. Our adaptive formula settles that condition only on the explicit fresh-control witness; overlapping controls and arbitrary cores remain unpaid.

    sum_pairs [(a^2+b^2)ln(a^2+b^2)-a^2 ln(a^2)-b^2 ln(b^2)]
       -K sum_pairs b^2 ln2,

divided by the squared cube size. Zero terms are omitted. Logs are factored into a rational prime-log vector, and signs are enclosed with atanh series, not inferred from floating values or from unproved logarithm independence. Normalize an argument to 2^e r, 1<=r<2, and use ln r=2 sum y^{2j+1}/(2j+1), y=(r-1)/(r+1)<=1/3. The omitted positive tail is bounded by 2y^{2N+1}/[(2N+1)(1-y^2)]. Negative coefficients reverse interval endpoints. Reciprocal logs and all signed interval arithmetic preserve ordering. Empty vectors prove exact equality. The analytic large-parameter bridge is Sections 4-6; the finite jobs do not extrapolate signs into larger dimensions.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

DERIVATION.md proves, for every live point-selector query with underlying
branches G,H and c=1/ln2,

    B_i/d <= max{4,c+log2(8 eta/gamma+4)}+2c/sqrt(e).

The proof separates the full constant core Fourier row, bounds its cost
uniformly, and controls nonconstant packet mass by 8eta+4rho^2 gamma.
The remaining-selector nonempty sector is exact, not a marginal entropy
proxy. It follows that eta<=gamma/5 gives strict 6-goodness and eta<=gamma
gives strict 7-goodness at EVERY layer, with no lower bound on gamma.

Define P(theta) as the supremum of B_i/d over actual Boolean branch pairs
in every dimension and all point-control widths with eta/gamma<=theta.
For every real theta>=8,

    log2(theta)-3 <= P(theta) <= log2(theta)+307/45.

Thus P(theta)/log2(theta) tends to one. The lower bound uses actual balanced
bent/one-vertex-flip branches: eta=1/2, gamma=2^-m, B/d>=m-2. This
extracts the necessary leading logarithm for any scalar contrast-only
price; a global potential avoiding that price needs an explicitly owned
rebate. Rarity width itself does not add a logarithm to this interface.

For S,W,U,V independent positive point blocks of ANY positive widths and
ANY independent Boolean core g, set A=WUV+S(U OR V-WUV), F=1+A(g-1).
Expose S, then the surviving conjunction or union. The separator's ratio
eta/gamma is <=1/5; the success-branch union ratio is <=1. Therefore this
actual stopping prefix has EXACTLY ZERO strictly 7-bad control charge,
uniformly even for highly biased cores and vanishing branch disagreement.
With pi=EA and a_g=1-Eg, its exclusive accounts are

    I_ctrl=a_g J(A), E I_terminal=pi I(g), E H_terminal=pi H(g),
    H(F)<=(7+2/ln2)a_g J(A)+pi H(g).

The sharper constants give a prefix rate <10. If H(g)<=C0 I(g), the
completed bound is H(F)<=max(10,C0)I(F). The arbitrary-core terminal
ratio is explicitly inherited, not proved or discarded. The earlier
balanced one-bit theorem is independently accepted at the entropy/face
scope stated in REVIEW_A023.md; no substantive repair was supplied.

First unsupported universal line: choose an actual policy for every
Boolean f whose large-contrast switches and complete terminal entropy
are paid from one nonreused physical influence reserve. No universal
bounded-contrast representation is shown. Arbitrary-width small-gamma
threshold-4 payment also remains open; the new closure uses threshold 7.

## proof dependencies and changed downstream conclusions

The new criterion, contrast asymptotics and arbitrary-core application
are self-contained proofs. Source ideas are credited to A025's exact
selector-sector split and A023's overlap family; their full-price failure
and single-bit theorem remain intact. Standard Parseval, binary entropy
log-sum, the physical influence telescope and JS<=2d/ln2 are the elementary
interfaces used, with the latter's scalar proof supplied below.

For clarity, JS<=2d/ln2 follows from
[(1+x)ln(1+x)+(1-x)ln(1-x)]/2<=1-sqrt(1-x^2) on [-1,1].
The derivative comparison is artanh(x)<=x/sqrt(1-x^2), proved by
differentiating on [0,1) and using equality at zero. Apply to each pair
of squared child coefficients, then use (|u|-|v|)^2<=(u-v)^2 and
sum(u-v)^2=4d. Thus no unpaid JS premise is hidden in the terminal ledger.

The small-gamma obstruction is resolved for bounded inactive-branch
contrast at threshold 7. It is NOT resolved for arbitrary branch pairs
or relabeled threshold 4. A023 v2 receives a scoped independent entropy/
face review in a local copy; its earlier unbalanced-core two-event claim,
full retained-KL computation and arbitrary nested reservations are not
accepted by this review. Source files and shared registries are untouched.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

POINT_SURGERY.tex contains the complete derivation. For D flipped vertices, the actual Boolean function is g=f(1-2 1_D). Its Fourier coefficients are a_S-2t_S, with t_S=2^-n sum_(v in D) f(v)chi_S(v). A fair selector has midpoint mass (a_S-t_S)^2, selector mass e_S=t_S^2, and packet mass q_S=(a_S-t_S)^2+e_S. Therefore

J=sum e_S log2(q_S/e_S)=mu[log2(1/mu)-D2(E||q)],

L=J+R, 0<=R<=mu/ln2, mu=|D|2^-n, E=e/mu.

These are exact signed packets and exact logarithmic terms, including H(parent)=H(q)+L and I(parent)=mu+(I(f)+I(g))/2. The true entropy decrement is L+JS of the child spectral laws.

For a one-point flip, E is uniform on every Fourier label. Every coefficient ratio r_S=a_S/[2^-n f(v)chi_S(v)] is an even integer. Consequently the smaller selector-fiber mass is e_S for every seed; physical mismatch equals spectral minority mass. For adjacent flips, E is uniform on exactly one half-cube, and the essential cross term in e_S is 2 delta^2 epsilon (-1)^(j in S). The change to total influence is the sum over external boundary edges; the internal common edge contributes zero. Simply adding two original-table point-flip influence changes would need a -4 delta f(v)f(v^j) correction in coordinate j. Adjacent coefficient ratios can be odd, so minority and physical mismatch need not agree.

Bent one-point ratios are n+log2(e)+O(2^-n/2); adjacent ratios, divided by their own mismatch mass 2 delta, are n-2+log2(e)+O(2^-n/2). Both follow from exact sign multiplicities and an explicit uniform 12/|r| estimate. This recovers the known bent logarithmic obstruction at the correct local scope.

The quantitative tensor result is: for a fixed m-bit Boolean seed h, let rho be its occupied Fourier-label fraction and lambda the uniform-on-support mean log2 of its absolute integer Walsh sums. After flipping one point of h^(tensor t), with delta_t=2^-mt,

|L_t/delta_t - 2(1-rho^t) - rho^t(2t lambda+log2 e)| <= 12(rho/2)^t.

All nonzero integer Walsh sums are even and have magnitude >=2. Tensor ratios multiply, the occupied fraction is rho^t, and zero packets contribute exactly 2 to the cost/mismatch average. Applying the explicit estimate to occupied ratios proves the formula for every t>=1 and every flipped vertex. Thus positive zero fraction gives L_t/delta_t ->2, whereas full support gives linear growth 2t lambda+log2 e, lambda>=1. The theorem concerns a fixed seed, not an arbitrary dimension-dependent seed sequence. Bent core plus character padding has a separate two-parameter uniform formula in POINT_SURGERY.tex.

First unsupported universal line: there exists a physical stopping policy for arbitrary Boolean input with sum_v pi_v J_v plus all recombination, linear remainders and terminal entropy bounded by K I(input) for one absolute K without reusing ancestral influence. No such inequality is inferred here. The support dichotomy supplies exact stress bills for testing it.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

ODDFACE_GAP.tex gives the proof. For an actual sign table g on m bits and h=g(1-2 1_D), let s=|D|, delta=2^-m, and E(D) count internal unordered edges. Then d=s delta and c_D=delta(ms-2E(D)). With signed half-difference t_T=delta sum_D g(v)chi_T(v), midpoint u_T=(ghat(T)-t_T)^2 and selector mass e_T=t_T^2, q_T=u_T+e_T. The parent erasure is B=sum q_T h2(e_T/q_T), and h2(d)-B is the selector/residual spectral mutual information M. The exact slack decomposes as

c_D+log2(e)d-B = delta[s log2(s)-2E(D)] + [d+(1-d)ln(1-d)]/ln2 + M.

All three terms are nonnegative. An elementary coordinate-splitting induction proves the edge-isoperimetric term without an imported theorem. The scalar term has positive series sum_(j>=2) d^j/[j(j-1)ln2], between d^2/(2ln2) and d^2/[2(1-d)ln2]. One point and an adjacent pair have zero geometric excess; separated points have geometric excess d. These facts do not remove spectral mutual information. Adjacent interference and physical/minority normalization are preserved.

For quadratic bent core b on k>=4 even data bits with beta=2^-k/2, p=2^-r controls, delta=2^-k, and arbitrary flip pattern D, define S_D=sum_D b(v), A_D=sum_(v in D,i: v^i not in D) b(v)b(v^i). For the actual activated selector F,

I(F)=p[k/2+r(1-beta)+delta(A_D+rS_D+s)],
c_D=p delta[(k+r)s-2E(D)].

With alpha=s delta<1/2, c_D/I(F)<=2alpha/(1-2alpha), uniformly in r and all even k>=4. This is a selector-root bound only; it is not spent again at descendants.

For constant-child splits, direct Fourier algebra gives B=2d(1-d)+Psi(d^2,(1-d)^2), where Psi is binary entropy times its packet mass. Hence B/d<=2+log2(e)/sqrt(e)<3. Every control-first query of a one-point activation has a constant child, regardless of the core, so these queries add no threshold-4 bad-node credit. Their active core ledger is scaled by p, with its terminal cost retained. The complete exact k=4,1<=s<=4 finite core certificate has zero bad-node credit for data-then-selector. Therefore control-data-selector has zero bad-node credit for every integer r>=0 on that declared family. This all-r conclusion follows from the transfer proof and finite exact core certificate, not an extrapolation from r=0,2,4.

The census also detects an actual descendant cost for data-first order. For D3={2,4,6} and D4={2,4,6,11}, after x0=0 (physical probability 1/2), the next x1 split differs at one residual vertex. It has d_v=2^-(r+3) and c_v=(r+3)d_v. Its limiting normalized erasure has the exact prime-log expression

(112 log2(2)-54 log2(3)+80 log2(5)-98 log2(7)+26 log2(13))/8,

strictly between 4157/1000 and 4158/1000. A monotonicity comparison of the single constant packet proves B_v/d_v at least this limit for every r>=1; thus the node is bad throughout that infinite range. Root influences are exactly p(35+15r)/16 and p(34+14r)/16, respectively. The full data-first bad-node ratio is consequently at least (r+3)/(35+15r) or (r+3)/(34+14r), tending to 1/15 or 1/14. These are lower bounds; the all-r proof does not require that all other descendants are good. They remain bounded and do not refute the localized bridge.

The exact maxima in the frozen domain are:

| r | single flip: selector-first | single flip: control-first | three flips: data-first | four flips: data-first |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 4/29 at {15} | 4/29 at {15} | 0 | 0 |
| 2 | 6/53 at {7} | 4/53 at {7} | 1/13 at {2,4,6} | 5/62 at {2,4,6,11} |
| 4 | 8/75 at {7} | 4/75 at {7} | 7/95 at {2,4,6} | 7/90 at {2,4,6,11} |

The omitted entries are zero: all two-point patterns in all four orders; single flips under data-first; multiple flips under selector-first/control-first; and all patterns under control-data-selector. The maximum over all 30,192 declared ledgers is 4/29. The maximum, over patterns, of the minimum over the four orders is exactly zero. Tie counts and every pattern's certified root gap are retained in the JSON records. This identifies finite maximizers, not a maximizer over unbounded k or all adaptive orders.

First unsupported line for the universal route: for some absolute K,Kprime and every Boolean f, a physical stopping tree has bad-node odd-face credit <=Kprime I(f) with complete terminal cost and no reused influence. The present root estimate does not prove that line; the data-first descendants explicitly show why root payment alone is insufficient.

# exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

A023 / B05, run 20261011T221608Z-fleet05-A023-amortized-rarity-budget. All cubes are uniform, all functions are Boolean signs, and entropy/KL/logarithms are in bits. Set c=1/ln(2). For any Boolean core g, r>=1 fresh controls, p=2^-r, and a=1-Eg in [0,2], define F=1+A_r(g-1), where A_r specifies one physical control point. The stopping tree queries these controls in a fixed order, stops that component on the first mismatch, and otherwise stops at g after r matches. It is physical, adaptive through this stopping rule, deterministic, and finite.

Write J_v=mu_v[log2(1/mu_v)-D2(nu_v||Q_v)], with mu_v the MAGNITUDE mismatch (minority fiber) mass, Q_v the average child squared Fourier law, and nu_v its normalized minority law. Zero mismatch means zero cost. The first target is the complete bound

    sum_v w_v J_v <= K rpa,   K=1+c+8c/e < 7,
    sum_v w_v (J_v+3c mu_v) + pH(g) <= 12rpa+pH(g).

The terminal term is retained. The influence partition is exactly I(F)=rpa+pI(g). A second, direct calculation gives H(F)<=pH(g)+8rpa.

The unconditional nontrivial family is every finite disjoint product of these activations whose core is a fair selector between an arbitrary bent function on even k>=4 bits and its flip at an arbitrary one vertex. Widths r, bent dimensions k, chosen vertices, and number of components are all independent and unbounded. For this family control-first J+terminal<=7I, the full sufficient mismatch ledger including 3c mu is <=12I, and H<=8I. Selector-first J+terminal<=8I and its full sufficient ledger is <=13I. This includes the original B05 quadratic witness with r=2k. No assertion of universal tree selection or full FEI is made.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

## 1. Node definitions and the complete entropy allowance

For child coefficients x_T,y_T, let Q_T=(x_T^2+y_T^2)/2, e_T=(x_T-y_T)^2/4 and m_T=(|x_T|-|y_T|)^2/4=min(e_T,Q_T-e_T). Put d=sum e_T, mu=sum m_T<=d, L=sum Q_T h2(e_T/Q_T). The exact decrement is Delta H=L+JS(x^2,y^2). The signed logarithmic allocation is J^sgn=sum e_T log2(Q_T/e_T); the required minority allocation is J=sum m_T log2(Q_T/m_T). Each is its own normalized KL expression; they must not be interchanged at a sign-changing endpoint.

Elementary entropy expansion gives J<=L and L<=J^sgn+c d. The first follows because the other half of binary entropy is nonnegative; the second follows from -(1-t)ln(1-t)<=t. Separately L<=J+c mu. The Hellinger inequality JS<=2c mu gives Delta H<=J+3c mu. For completeness: write two probabilities as s(1+t),s(1-t). The nats JS contribution is s[(1+t)ln(1+t)+(1-t)ln(1-t)]/2, at most s(1-sqrt(1-t^2)). The difference starts at zero and has derivative t/sqrt(1-t^2)-artanh(t)>=0, since this derivative's derivative is nonnegative. Summing gives JS_nat<=sum (|x_T|-|y_T|)^2/2=2mu. Thus no unreviewed restriction theorem is needed for this allowance.

## 2. Arbitrary-bias control tree and its retained KL payment

At the unique active node with s remaining controls set u=2^-s and d_s=u a. Its children are 1 and 1+A_(s-1)(g-1). The physical reach probability is w_s=p/u. The empty packet has Q0=1-2d_s+2d_s^2 and signed mismatch e0=d_s^2. In every other packet one child coefficient is zero, so e_T=m_T=Q_T/2, and the total such mismatch is d_s-d_s^2. Hence, for all s including the last one,

    J_s^sgn=d_s-d_s^2+d_s^2 log2(Q0/d_s^2).

If s>=2, the active child's mean 1-2ua is nonnegative; consequently magnitude mismatch agrees with signed mismatch. For s=1 and Eg<0 the empty minority is (1-a/2)^2 rather than a^2/4. We retain the genuine minority cost and only use J_s<=L_s<=J_s^sgn+c d_s. This handles g=-1, g=+1, zero coefficients, and the last-layer sign change. For a=0 the whole tree is constant and all costs are zero.

Since Q0<=1 and 0<d_s<=1,

    J_s^sgn <= d_s+2d_s^2 log2(1/d_s).

Every summand 2^-s(s-log2 a) is nonnegative for a<=2. The infinite sums sum 2^-s=1 and sum s2^-s=2 therefore imply

    sum_s w_s J_s^sgn
      <= p a r + 2p a^2 sum_(s=1)^r 2^-s(s-log2 a)
      <= p a r + 2p a^2(2-log2 a).

Differentiation on 0<a<=2 gives max a(2-log2 a)=4c/e, attained at a=4/e. Combining with J_s<=J_s^sgn+c d_s and sum w_s d_s=rpa proves

    sum_s w_s J_s <= p a[r(1+c)+8c/e] <= K rpa,
    K=1+c+8c/e<7.

Also sum w_s mu_s<=rpa, so the full allowance is at most (K+3c)rpa<12rpa. The remaining expected entropy is exactly pH(g), with expected influence pI(g). Influence is partitioned exactly because each queried control has influence pa and every data influence is p times the core's influence.

These constants require no floating sign calculation. The atanh series gives ln2>2(1/3+1/81)=56/81, and the exponential series gives e>65/24. Thus c<81/56 and c/e< (81/56)(24/65). Substitution proves K<7 and K+3c<12 by rational arithmetic.

## 3. First-revelation ledger and no reuse across descendants

Give this control block one identifier when its first control is queried. Its account holds exactly B_ctrl=rpa, consisting of the distinct physical control-coordinate influences. Its single bill is the complete expected sum of the J_s charges; descendants add subcharges to that bill and cannot open a second account for the same control event. It is closed when the block first becomes known inactive (first mismatch) or known active (r matches). The core account is pI(g), belongs only to the terminal core, and pays pH(g). The two accounts are disjoint; neither is pledged to both a parent and a descendant. This is a first-revelation payment in the sense of the first physical revelation process of a declared event, not an oracle revelation of an unqueried event.

KL credit is generated at the node where its actual (nu_s,Q_s) pair is defined, and consumed once in that node's J_s=mu_s log(1/mu_s)-mu_s D2(nu_s||Q_s). We neither transfer a KL value to another reference law nor claim that the same event earns a fresh credit at every descendant. The paid quantity is the completed block bill, with all these distinct conditional subbills included exactly once. The logarithm is not discarded: the formula in section 2 bounds the post-KL bill explicitly.

On the positive-mean core subfamily (a<=1), mu_s=d_s and the unrebated weighted rarity sum is exactly

    p a [r(r+1)/2+r log2(1/a)].

The weighted KL credit is this expression minus the exact signed J_tree. Thus its quadratic-in-r part is created and consumed within this one control-event account. At a>1 only the last node needs the separate minority endpoint above. This last correction is why simply extending A021's balanced formula by renaming mu would be invalid.

## 4. Direct full-entropy bound for biased activation

Let m=Eg=1-a and use 0log0=0. The empty Fourier coefficient of F is 1-pa; at empty data/nonempty controls it is -pa times the point character; at nonempty data it is p ghat(T) times that character. Summing the actual squared spectrum, with signs retained, gives

    H(F)=pH(g)+2rp a(2-pa)
       +p m^2 log2(m^2)-(p-p^2)a^2 log2(a^2)
       -(1-pa)^2 log2((1-pa)^2).

This is valid for m negative and at all constant endpoints. The m term is nonpositive. The last term is at most 2cpa, by -ln(1-t)<=t/(1-t). For a>=1 the a term is nonpositive; for 0<a<1 its upper bound is 2pa[a log2(1/a)]<=2cpa/e. Therefore

    H(F)<=pH(g)+[4+2c(1+1/e)]rpa < pH(g)+8rpa.

The strict numerical constant follows from c<81/56 and e>65/24: 2c(1+1/e)<7209/1820<4. This improves the conservative mismatch-ledger constant but does not replace the requested J_tree certificate. If H(g)<=C0 I(g), then H(F)<=max(C0,8)I(F); the full sufficient mismatch ledger is <=max(C0,12)I(F). Arbitrary cores still require an actual terminal certificate.

## 5. A complete unbounded family: activated arbitrary bent point-surgery selectors

Let b be any bent function on even k>=4 bits, v any vertex, delta=2^-k, s=b(v), b'=b-2s1_{x=v}, and let t choose b or b' fairly. Define g(x,t) as this selector core, and F=1+A_r(g-1), with arbitrary r>=1. Bent flatness gives I(b)=k/2. Flipping v changes influence in coordinate i by 2delta b(v)b(v^i), since precisely one undirected edge changes status. Consequently

    I(g)=delta+(I(b)+I(b'))/2 >= k/2-(k-1)delta >=7k/16.

Every distribution on the core's 2^(k+1) Fourier labels has H(g)<=k+1<=5k/4. Thus H(g)/I(g)<=20/7<3, a direct terminal certificate. Each child's H is at most k; no threshold or read-once membership is assumed. The exact mean is Eg=Eb-delta s, with |Eb|=sqrt(delta); hence 11/16<=a<=21/16.

The control-first tree therefore has

    J_tree+pH(g) <=7rpa+3pI(g)<=7I(F),
    J_tree+3c sum w_s mu_s+pH(g)<=12I(F),
    H(F)<=8I(F).

The rare point-surgery selector is not queried on inactive paths. On the active leaf its entire entropy, including the surviving kdelta selector logarithm, is paid once by the core account pI(g). That account is not also used for a separate selector bill. In particular r=2k and the quadratic b at v=0 is exactly the accepted B05 sparse-bent obstruction, with its local bad ratio fully absorbed in this complete tree.

## 6. Selector-first complete tree and comparison

Query t at the root, then query controls in each child, stopping on their first mismatch, with terminals b or b'. Let a_*=(1-Eb+1-Eb')/2=a and Ibar=(I(b)+I(b'))/2. Root disagreement is d=pdelta. Because the root children differ at exactly one point of the (r+k)-cube, every Fourier ratio is an even integer: an integer Walsh sum of 2^(r+k) signs is even. Thus the signed mismatch e=d^2 is the minority in every packet, mu=d. Its exact bill is sum_T e log(Q/e), and its KL representation implies

    J_root<=d log2(1/d)=pdelta(r+k).

The two child control-tree bills average to at most 7rpa. Their expected terminal entropy is p[H(b)+H(b')]/2<=pk. Since a>=11/16>=delta, the r part of J_root is <=rpa. Since Ibar>=k/2-kdelta>=7k/16, its k part is <=(16delta/7)pIbar, and terminal entropy is <=(16/7)pIbar. Therefore, for k>=4,

    J_selector-first+terminal <=8rpa+(17/7)pIbar<=8I(F),
    I(F)=rpa+pIbar+pdelta.

The full 3c mu allowance is <=3c(rpa+pdelta), so full sufficient ledger<=13I(F). Influence accounts here are distinct: root selector pdelta; queried controls rpa; terminal data pIbar. The selector bill uses additional control/data allowances once; the stated constants include these extra debits. No assertion that selector-first is optimal is made. Both actual complete trees are linearly paid although the isolated selector is not paid by its own influence with a universal constant.

For the original positive quadratic b at zero, Ibar=k/2+kdelta, Eg=sqrt(delta)-delta, and I(g)=k/2+(k+1)delta. These identities are checked exactly. Terminal-entropy placement differs: control-first leaves the full selector core; selector-first retains the root selector bill and leaves its two children. The entropy difference is exactly the core's own restriction decrement, multiplied by p, so neither tree silently omits it.

## 7. Multiple events and descendants: disjoint product extension

For any finite number M of independent components of section 5, let f=product_(j=1)^M F_j. Process components in fixed order, querying a component's controls until it fails or activates, leaving its certified core unqueried; continue until every component is resolved. The tree has depth at most sum r_j. Its leaves are products of the active cores and constant signs. A core is activated with probability p_j. Entropy and influence of disjoint products add by tensor Fourier factorization.

At a node querying component j, child Fourier coefficients are x_T b_R,y_T b_R. Their Q and m are multiplied by b_R^2; the ratio Q/m is unchanged, and sum_R b_R^2=1. Thus J, mu and queried influence are precisely the component's quantities, even with earlier terminal cores and later unresolved components present. Averaging over earlier histories restores the original reach weights for component j. Hence total J and expected terminal entropy are the sums of the component bills, and all bounds in sections 5-6 add. The simultaneous rarity events never share an account or a physical coordinate. This proves the stated unbounded-dimensional, unbounded-component family, rather than merely finitely checking a quadratic example.

First unsupported universal line: every arbitrary Boolean f admits such a controlled-event decomposition, or another physical stopping rule with the same complete linear bill and uniformly certified terminal cores. Neither is proved. Allowing arbitrary g in section 2 without its terminal certificate leaves pH(g) unpaid; the threshold paper's Corollary 8.10 explains why this cannot be treated as a negligible remainder.

# exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

A023/B05 stage 2, run 20261012T010933Z-fleet05-A023-amortized-rarity-budget. All functions are genuine Boolean signs on a uniform physical cube, all entropy and KL units are bits. Let g be any core, a=1-Eg, and use disjoint physical blocks of s>=0 common controls and r,t>=1 exclusive controls. Their specified point indicators are A_s,A_r,A_t, with all shared literals compatible. Define

    F=1+A_s(A_r OR A_t)(g-1),
    u=2^-s, p=2^-r, q=2^-t, beta=p+q-pq.

The two events A_s A_r and A_s A_t share their common physical controls and the SAME core. Their intersection is counted once. The specified stopping rule queries common controls first, then the first exclusive block until its event succeeds or fails, and the second block only if the first fails. Stop at a constant or at g when the union event is resolved. This is finite, physical and adaptive; max depth s+r+t, with no core coordinate queried. Retain terminal entropy u beta H(g).

Primary theorem: if a>=11/16, every control query of this tree is good at the strict threshold K=4, B_v<4d_v whenever d_v>0. Thus its localized odd-face charge sum_(B_v>4d_v) w_v I(g_v h_v)/2 is exactly ZERO, in all dimensions and all independent widths s,r,t. Its queried influence and terminal resources are exactly

    I_ctrl=u a[s beta+r p(1-q)+t q(1-p)],
    I_leaf=u beta I(g), H_leaf=u beta H(g), I(F)=I_ctrl+I_leaf.

The complete erasure/entropy ledger gives H(F)<7I_ctrl+H_leaf, minority/KL debt plus terminal <=4I_ctrl+H_leaf, and minority/KL debt including its 3mu/ln2 allowance plus terminal <9I_ctrl+H_leaf. If g fairly selects ANY bent function on even k>=4 data bits or its one-vertex flip, H(g)/I(g)<3, so H(F)<7I(F), J+terminal<=4I(F), and full sufficient mismatch ledger<9I(F). These statements pay the common controls and shared core only once. They are scoped class results, not universal FEI.

This stage also retracts the erroneous prior A023 acceptance of A021's negative logarithmic remainder; no previous stage file is changed.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

## 1. Correct the old review, preserve unaffected mathematics

CORRECTION_A021_REVIEW.md is the source-bound correction. The coefficient 1/j-2/(j-1)+1/(j-2)=2/[j(j-1)(j-2)] is POSITIVE. Hence b(q)=-2(1-q)^2log2(1-q)=2cq-3cq^2+R(q), with R(q)=+4c sum_(j>=3) q^j/[j(j-1)(j-2)]. Its decrement remainder R(u)-R(2u)/2 is negative. The old A023 review's multiplication and its acceptance of A021-UNIFORM-RARE-CORE-EXPANSION@1 were wrong. Retract that acceptance; narrow its report-wide endorsement. The separate exact A021-CONTROL-FIRST-KL-LEDGER@1 remains valid.

An exact rational witness is R(1/2)=1/2-c/4>31/224>0 because ln2>56/81. The repaired v2 upper remainder follows by bounding each positive tail coefficient by 2/3. This confirms the objection without relying on a floating decimal. The first A023 family/tree claim used its direct node formula, elementary entropy inequalities and exact influence counting; neither R nor the decrement remainder occurred. Its constants and family theorem survive unchanged. No new contribution to A021 v2 is asserted, and no copied accepted v1 status may continue to authorize its false tail sign.

## 2. Exact resources for the overlapping events

At a common control coordinate, pivotality needs all other common controls active, the union of exclusives active, and g=-1. Its influence is u a beta. Each first-exclusive coordinate has influence u a p(1-q); each second-exclusive coordinate has influence u a q(1-p). Data influences are u beta times the core influences. Summing yields the stated partition. Terminal core probability is u beta, not u(p+q); paths where both events hold still have one copy of g and one terminal bill.

The rule is determined only by previously queried physical values. A mismatch of a common control stops immediately. A successful first exclusive event resolves the union and stops at g. If it fails, query the second exclusive event, stopping at its first mismatch or its success. A fixed-order tree that queries skipped irrelevant controls has the same ledger, since those splits have B=d=I(child product)=0; this equivalence is used by the finite implementation.

## 3. A local exposure inequality, not a guessed transfer

Consider a first-exclusive node with l controls remaining. Write C for the second event, whose conditional probability remains q, and rho=2^-(l-1). Its children are

    f0=1+C(g-1),
    f1=1+[C+(1-C)A_(l-1)](g-1).

Disagreement is d=a rho(1-q)/2. In Fourier fibers involving a NONEMPTY subset of the remaining l-1 first-exclusive controls, f0 has zero coefficient. Therefore each such parent packet has equal spectral-bit masses and B=2e. The squared point indicator's fraction on its empty label is rho, so these fibers have total mismatch (1-rho)d and erasure 2(1-rho)d exactly. All other fibers have total mismatch rho d and total packet mass M<=1. Concavity gives their erasure <=M h2(rho d/M)<=h2(rho d), since the latter expression increases with M. This is valid for signed mismatch as well as magnitude minority: binary entropy is the same whichever half is smaller. No unsigned substitution is made in a KL formula.

Using h2(x)<=x log2(e/x), obtain

    B/d<=2(1-rho)+rho log2(e/(rho d)).

The more general sufficient criterion is a(1-q)>e/8. Under this criterion rho d>e rho^2/16 and

    B/d<2+2rho[1+log2(1/rho)]<=4.

The final inequality is dyadic, not a continuous maximization: rho=2^-j and (j+1)2^-j<=1 for every integer j>=0, with equality only j=0,1. In our two-event class q<=1/2 and a>=11/16, so a(1-q)>=11/32>e/8. This proves first-exclusive goodness in all widths, independent of core dimension or Fourier details.

For a common-control node the inactive child is constant and the active child is an activation of the union-core function. After the first-exclusive event fails, a second-exclusive node is likewise constant-child. For any such split define d=Pr(child=-1). Its signed packet formula is

    Jsgn=d-d^2+d^2 log2((1-2d+2d^2)/d^2),
    B<=Jsgn+c d <=[1+c+2c/e]d<4d,

where d log2(1/d)<=c/e. At d=0 the split contributes zero. This formula remains valid for a negative child mean; minority allocation and signed allocation are not equated. Thus every node before the certified core is reached is good.

Rational constants: ln2>2(1/3+1/81)=56/81, hence c<81/56. Also 65/24<e<65/24+1/100<11/4: the exponential tail from 1/5! is bounded by the geometric ratio 1/6. These bounds prove 1+c+2c/e<4, 4+2c<7 and 4+3c<9 by Fraction arithmetic. The detailed exposure proof is saved immediately in OVERLAP_GOOD_NODE_PROOF.md.

## 4. Complete cost, terminal entropy and face ownership

For an actual split, Delta H=B+JS and JS<=2c d. One elementary proof writes two probabilities as s(1+z),s(1-z) and uses [(1+z)ln(1+z)+(1-z)ln(1-z)]/2<=1-sqrt(1-z^2). The derivative of the difference is z/sqrt(1-z^2)-artanh(z)>=0 on [0,1]. Summing gives JS<=2c mu<=2c d, with mu the magnitude minority mass. Thus the exact entropy telescope gives H(F)<=4I_ctrl+2cI_ctrl+H_leaf. Likewise Jminor<=B and mu<=d give the two required retained-KL bounds without discarding a terminal remainder.

Let Q(f)=2^(1-n) times its number of odd 2-faces, meaning corner product -1. At a split, faces not using that direction partition into child cubes, and faces using it are precisely boundary edges of the child product. Hence Q(f)=I(g_v h_v)/2+[Q(g_v)+Q(h_v)]/2. This proves first-revelation face ownership: every face is charged exactly at the first split along one of its two directions. The stopping tree retains expected Q(leaf), just as it retains expected H(leaf). We do NOT spend every odd face: since all its control nodes are good, the localized bad-node part is exactly zero. Total unlocalized face cost may still be large.

Coordinate ownership is also explicit. There is one common block account s u a beta, not two copies attributed to the two events. The exclusive block accounts include their survival factors (1-q) and (1-p). One terminal core account u beta I(g) pays u beta H(g). Each physical coordinate is queried at most once on any history; every expected debit is included once in I_ctrl. This resolves shared-control/shared-core ownership for this declared two-event family.

## 5. Unconditional all-dimensional terminal family

Let b be any bent sign on even k>=4 bits and v any vertex. Let b' flip b only at v, and let g choose b or b' by one fresh fair selector. Put delta=2^-k and s_v=b(v). Bent flatness gives I(b)=k/2 and |Eb|=sqrt(delta). An edge-count change gives I_i(b')-I_i(b)=2delta b(v)b(v^i), and selector influence delta. Hence

    I(g)>=k/2-(k-1)delta>=7k/16,
    H(g)<=k+1<=5k/4,
    H(g)/I(g)<=20/7<3.

The exact mean is Eg=Eb-delta s_v, so a>=1-sqrt(delta)-delta>=11/16. Thus every such core meets the exposure hypothesis, with independently unbounded k,s,r,t. Combining its certified terminal cost with section 4 yields H(F)<7I(F), J_tree+terminal<=4I(F), full sufficient mismatch ledger<9I(F). This improves the earlier disjoint-event constants while paying the overlapping point masks. The elementary core H/I bound itself is not claimed as novel.

## 6. Exact finite comparison of four COMPLETE orders

The frozen grid has s=0,1,2; r,t=1,2; k=2,4, with the positive quadratic bent core flipped at zero. That is 24 actual roots, each with four complete orders, plus its control-first stopping tree. Original coordinate order is common/exclusive-A/exclusive-B/data/selector. Orders are explicitly:

- selector-first: selector, all controls, data;
- control-first: all controls, selector, data;
- data-first: data, all controls, selector;
- control-data-selector: all controls, data, selector.

The maximum exact ratios Q_bad,4/I over this declared grid are:

| Complete order | Maximum |
|---|---:|
| selector-first | 14/137 |
| control-first | 12/137 |
| data-first | 0 |
| control-data-selector | 0 |

All 24 stopping trees have Q_bad=0, as the all-width proof predicts. Complete control-first still queries a possibly bad selector in its terminal core; its positive ratio is therefore consistent with zero CONTROL-node cost. The terminal entropy in the stopping theorem is not silently erased. k=2 is only a finite endpoint test, not part of the k>=4 terminal theorem. The zero data-first/grid result is not extrapolated: A021/A022 exhibit other cores whose data-first descendants are bad.

First unsupported universal line: arbitrary overlapping event systems admit a physical reveal order with sufficiently exposed disagreement a(1-q)>e/8 at the needed nodes, or have another single-use payment when that exposure fails, AND their terminal cores have a certified cost. With three or more events q can approach one and the current sufficient criterion may fail. Arbitrary core entropy remains a separate FEI-strength obligation even when a is large. No every-tree or universal selection theorem follows from the finite table.

# exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

A023/B05 stage 3, run 20261013T223305Z-fleet05-A023-amortized-rarity-budget. Uniform physical Boolean signs, all entropy/KL in bits. Let U,V,W be compatible point indicators on disjoint control blocks of independently arbitrary widths R,T,L>=1, X one fair physical separator bit, and g an independent BALANCED Boolean core. The controller has three overlapping events:

    A=XU OR XV OR WUV, F=1+A(g-1).

Write p=2^-L, q=2^-R, r=2^-T, beta=q+r-qr, eta=pqr, pi=(beta+eta)/2 and e=beta-eta. The physical stopping policy queries X first, resolves WUV if X=0 or U OR V if X=1, and stops at a constant or at g. It retains terminal entropy pi H(g). Core entropy is not discarded even on very rare paths.

All-dimensional scoped result: every descendant control node is strictly K=4-good; the separator may be bad and is not assumed good. Its exact one-use odd-face price is c_X=Jrem+e I(g)/2, where J(B)=I(1-2B)/2 and

    d_X=e/2,
    Jrem=[(L+R+T)eta+R q(1-r)+T r(1-q)]/2,
    I_ctrl=J(A)=d_X+Jrem, I_terminal=pi I(g).

The stopping tree has Q_bad either zero or c_X, hence Q_bad<=I(F)-d_X. With H(g)<=C0 I(g), a marked separator reserve spent once proves H(F)<=max(5+2/ln2,C0+1)I(F). Without this terminal hypothesis, the exact upper ledger is

    H(F)<=(5+2/ln2)J(A)+pi H(g)+(e/2)I(g).

The complete genuine minority/KL bill plus terminal is <=5J(A)+pi H(g)+(e/2)I(g); with its separate 3mu/ln2 allowance it is <=(5+3/ln2)J(A)+pi H(g)+(e/2)I(g).

For g=z times the selector between any even-k>=4 bent sign and its flip at one vertex, g is balanced and H(g)/I(g)<3. This gives H(F)<8I(F), retained J+terminal<=5I(F), and full sufficient mismatch ledger<10I(F) uniformly in L,R,T,k. No repeated-child bank or universal FEI theorem is claimed. The new theorem extends the existing A023 overlap claim; it does not open a separate broad candidate.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

## 1. Independent witness review before extending the mechanism

A021's actual controller is A=x1x2 OR x1x3 OR x0x2x3. Direct positive-literal truth tables, integer Walsh transforms and independent edge counts give pi=7/16, J(A)=3/4, d_x0=1/16 and face price I(F0F1)/2=1/4 for g=z. Its B-4d is exactly the source's rational prime-log expression, independently certified positive. The original fixed x0-first control order has Q_bad=1/4; x1-first has zero on this fixture.

For any balanced g, the nonempty-core Fourier rows have controller coefficients multiplied by ghat(T); their erasure kernel is homogeneous of degree two. Summing ghat(T)^2 gives one, while the pure-control row is unchanged. Thus control-node B and d are precisely the dictator-core quantities. The face price depends on I(g), not only balance. REVIEW_A021_WITNESS.md records HOLDS for exactly this source-bound scope, without accepting A021's tensor-prefix or finite exhaustive conclusions. No source repair was supplied.

## 2. The three-event extension and actual coordinate partition

The separator controller children are A0=WUV and A1=U OR V, with A0 contained in A1. Their densities are eta and beta. The parent density is pi, not beta+eta. Every active history reaches the same g once, including histories where multiple event descriptions hold.

A point indicator of width m and density rho has J=m rho by directed-edge counting. Therefore J(A0)=(L+R+T)eta and J(A1)=R q(1-r)+T r(1-q). The separator influence is (beta-eta)/2=e/2 because a balanced g is negative on half the data cube. Each other physical controller coordinate has its half-averaged child influence. Hence

    I(F)=J(A)+pi I(g), J(A)=e/2+[J(A0)+J(A1)]/2.

The W-coordinate contribution to Jrem is L eta/2; the U block's is R[q(1-r)+eta]/2, and the V block's is T[r(1-q)+eta]/2. These are actual physical coordinate accounts, not independently credited boundaries of the three named events. The formulas are verified against direct edges in every frozen case.

## 3. Descendants are good; the separator is treated separately

In the X=0 child, the controller is one point conjunction WUV. Every queried control has a constant inactive child. For signed disagreement d, exact Fourier grouping gives Jsgn=d-d^2+d^2log2((1-2d+2d^2)/d^2), and B<=Jsgn+c d, c=1/ln2. Thus B/d<=1+c+2c/euler<4. Here euler denotes the exponential constant, distinct from the controller density e. Endpoints d=0 contribute zero. This is an actual binary erasure statement and does not equate magnitude mismatch to signed disagreement.

In the X=1 child, U OR V is the previous two-event fresh-block case with a=1. At a U-control node with remaining point probability rho=2^-j, mismatch is d=rho(1-r)/2. Fibers with nonempty remaining-U label have one zero child amplitude and contribute 2(1-rho)d exactly. Other fibers have mismatch rho d, total packet mass at most one, and contribution <=h2(rho d). Hence B/d<=2(1-rho)+rho log2(euler/(rho d)). Since (1-r)>=1/2>euler/8, this is strictly <2+2rho(1+j)<=4. After U fails the V nodes are again constant-child; after U succeeds stop immediately at g. No descendant bad-control face bill exists in either branch, in any widths.

The X separator itself is never priced by an assumed fresh-event lemma. The finite checks happen to find it good in the frozen domain; the theorem and reserve work even if it is bad outside that domain. Root-goodness in all widths is a separate unsupported strengthening.

## 4. Exact root face price and the first-revelation reserve

Let D=A1-A0. It is an indicator of density e, and F0F1=1+D(g-1). Balance gives I(F0F1)=e I(g)+J(D). Direct control edges yield

    J(D)=R q(1-r)+T r(1-q)+(L+R+T)eta=2Jrem.

For example, a U pivot changes D if V=0, or if V=1 and W=1; its probability contribution is q[(1-r)+p r]. The analogous V contribution and the W contribution prove the displayed identity. Equivalently, the cut edges of A0 and A1 are disjoint, so their symmetric-difference boundary adds exactly. Therefore c_X=Jrem+e I(g)/2. Since e/2<=pi, c_X<=Jrem+pi I(g)=I(F)-d_X. Since descendants are good, Q_bad is zero if the root is good and c_X if it is bad.

This is a one-use face account. Odd faces involving X are charged only when X is first revealed, since Q(f)=I(f0f1)/2+[Q(f0)+Q(f1)]/2. The identity follows by partitioning cube faces into those lying inside children and those projecting to cut edges of their product. Descendants cannot debit these same faces.

Influence reserves must also be marked before descent. At rate K=5+2c, give each nonseparator physical control coordinate K times its expected influence. Reserve one unit of that rate exclusively for c_X's controller part; descendants get only K-1=4+2c. Their full erasure/recombination cost is within that remaining rate. Each terminal-core coordinate at rate C0+1 reserves one unit for the root's data face part; terminal entropy receives only C0. The actual data fraction consumed is e/(2pi)<=1; the leftover from this reserved unit is eta I(g), nonnegative. The separator's own influence is priced at K. Root charges are paid once from these named capacities. No reserve is recreated at child nodes. It would be invalid to apply the same one-unit reservation at every ancestor of an arbitrarily deep separator tree.

## 5. Entropy/KL telescope with all terminal cost included

For any actual split, Delta H=B+JS and JS<=2c mu<=2c d, with magnitude minority mu. This follows from the scalar inequality [(1+t)ln(1+t)+(1-t)ln(1-t)]/2<=1-sqrt(1-t^2): its difference has derivative t/sqrt(1-t^2)-artanh(t)>=0. Summing across packets gives the JS claim.

At the separator B_X<=4d_X+c_X irrespective of badness. For a good node this is immediate. Otherwise B<=h2(d)<=d log2(1/d)+c d<=c_X+4d, because the cube edge-isoperimetric inequality gives d log2(1/d)<=I(F0F1)/2. An elementary proof of that inequality is as follows: a set of m vertices spans at most m log2(m)/2 edges. Induct across a cube coordinate with child sizes a<=b; the new internal edges are at most a, and (a+b)log2(a+b)-a log2(a)-b log2(b)>=2a follows from h2(a/(a+b))>=2a/(a+b). The boundary therefore has at least m log2(2^n/m) edges, yielding the normalized influence assertion.

Summing the actual fair stopping tree gives

    H(F)<=(4+2c)J(A)+Q_bad+pi H(g)
         <=(4+2c)d_X+(5+2c)Jrem+pi H(g)+(e/2)I(g).

This is stronger than the stated uniform-rate upper ledger and displays which root-coordinate capacity is unspent. The entire pi H(g) is retained. If H(g)<=C0 I(g), the marked account proves H(F)<=max(5+2c,C0+1)I(F).

For the requested retained KL bill, define m_T=(|a_T|-|b_T|)^2/4, mu=sum m_T, Q_T=(a_T^2+b_T^2)/2, nu=m/mu, and Jminor=sum m_T log2(Q_T/m_T)=mu[log2(1/mu)-D2(nu||Q)]. Always Jminor<=B and mu<=d. Therefore J_tree+pi H(g)<=4J(A)+Q_bad+pi H(g)<=5J(A)+pi H(g)+(e/2)I(g). Its additive 3c sum mu allowance is at most 3c J(A), giving the full source ledger. No raw mu log term is substituted for Jminor, no KL reference is transferred across nodes, and terminal entropy is not omitted.

## 6. A nontrivial unbounded terminal family

Let b be any bent sign on even k>=4 bits, let b' flip b at one vertex v, and let h fairly select b or b'. Balance it by an independent sign z, so g=z h. Flatness gives I(b)=k/2. A one-point flip changes each data influence by 2delta b(v)b(v^i), delta=2^-k, and h's selector influence is delta. Thus I(g)=1+I(h)>=1+k/2-(k-1)delta, while H(g)=H(h)<=k+1. For k>=4 this ratio is <3. Its terminal certificate uses only a proved support/edge bound, not an unproved arbitrary-core estimate.

With c<81/56, 5+2c<8 and 5+3c<10 by exact rational arithmetic. Hence the full family has H(F)<8I(F), J+terminal<=5I(F), and full sufficient mismatch ledger<10I(F). L,R,T and k remain independent and unbounded. For fixed core and L, R=T going to infinity makes event density and total influence vanish; the same constants still apply. This is a three-event overlap extension of the existing first-revelation mechanism, not a claimed novel bound for bent cores themselves.

First unsupported universal line: the sum of marked separator reservations remains bounded per actual physical coordinate in arbitrary overlapping/deep controllers, with all terminals certified. The present one-separator reserve is not an arbitrary-depth potential. A021's balanced selector exposure shows precisely why repeating a residual J bank without consumption is invalid. No automatic maximal-influence policy, universal zero-badness statement, or full FEI follows here.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Full proof is PROOF.md. At a live selector query put l=j-1, rho=2^-l and d=rho gamma. Nonempty residual-selector Fourier rows contribute exactly 2(1-rho)d. Empty residual-selector packets have derivative mass rho^2 gamma and total mass <=1; the entropy remainder inequality and log-sum give rho^2 gamma log2(e/(rho^2 gamma)). ## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For every finite Boolean outer gate g, Boolean children f_i on mutually disjoint independent uniform physical blocks, and any fair physical singleton restriction x inside block j, derive the exact changed-mean entropy/deficit transfer, including deterministic descendants. Entropy is in bits. Physical influence is uniform flip influence; the outer spectral influence is computed in its actual induced product basis. This is a source-bound application/verification of the accepted A056 composition baseline and repair of A057's explicitly pending biased empty-sector identity, not another broad candidate opened despite B05's review queue. The additional result is the signed restriction cocycle and its exact normalization credit.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators
